## intake-2341#record — Underdog Bark 3.0 Clean (ConwayResearch): 0.6B Parakeet-TDT ASR finetune, 180 MB entropy-coded low-bit weights, built-in disfluency removal

### Claims (as verified)

0. SCOPE: this is a speech-to-TEXT (ASR/STT) model, not text-to-speech. HF pipeline_tag automatic-speech-recognition, base_model nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3 (finetune), license CC-BY-4.0, English only, not gated. It is a Stage-1 candidate for the STT half of the speech cascade, not a TTS candidate.
   - *unaffected*: Scope confirmed from HF API sha 6d34ed86: ASR, finetune of nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3, CC-BY-4.0, English only.
   - anchor `dfs_README.md` @ b7da294f: "_transcript_c_ is _transcript_b_ but with false starts removed. This is the most minimal transcript."
   - anchor `dfs_README.md` @ b7da294f: "_transcript_b_ is _transcript_a_ but with filled pauses, explicit editing terms, and discourse markers removed."

1. Model card claims 4.08% WER on cleaned DisfluencySpeech transcripts (250 clips) and 5.83% average over the Open ASR Leaderboard 7 English sets (LS clean 1.71, LS other 3.78, AMI 12.12, Earnings-22 8.55, GigaSpeech 9.76, SPGISpeech 2.66, VoxPopuli 2.23), against verbatim references, so removed fillers count as errors on AMI/GigaSpeech/Earnings-22.
   - *narrowed*: 4.08% is vs an unspecified DisfluencySpeech "cleaned" transcript (dataset has b = fillers/editing terms/discourse markers removed, c = also false starts removed); single-speaker studio re-recordings, training-data overlap undisclosed. WER cannot distinguish meaning-bearing deletions from fillers. The 5.83 seven-set average rests on the same cleaned-reference pipeline as the Plus rows and no scoring code is published.
   - anchor `clean/README.md` @ 6d34ed86: "**Clean reaches 4.08% WER against cleaned [DisfluencySpeech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08820) transcripts**"

   - anchor `clean/README.md` @ 6d34ed86: "Bark 3.0 Clean was measured with no silence added before the audio"
2. Card claims the verbatim foundation Bark 3.0 Plus averages 4.76% vs Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 4.94%, Qwen3-ASR-0.6B 5.16%, Whisper large-v3 6.04%; the card says Bark rows used its own scoring code and are not submitted leaderboard entries, and baseline rows are leaderboard results as of 9 Oct 2026.

Sets without cleaning (LS, SPGI) agree across cards. The footnote that baseline rows are published leaderboard results is not reconcilable with NVIDIA card (6.34, AMI 11.31). Relative ordering Bark>Parakeet holds only within the cleaned-reference pipeline.

3. Weights ship as model.bark (format bark-levels-v3): per-row fp16 levels plus one code per weight, ANS entropy-coded (constriction library), 16 column classes per tensor; other matrices 5-6 bit per-row integers; decoding is exact. 180 MB download, about 1.25 GB in memory once unpacked per the card, so the compression is a storage/transfer format and the unpacked runtime tensors are float.
   - *narrowed*: Decode is exact only with respect to the packed representation. Header (99,973 B JSON, Range-read): 627.06M params, 603.98M in 264 tensors at K=2 or 3 per-row levels (about 2.20 bits/weight entropy coded), 22.65M at 5/6-bit ints, 0.42M raw. The format is itself a lossy low-bit quantisation of the base; no ablation vs fp16 base is given. The torch loader materialises fp32 (2.5 GB), the MLX path fp16 (1.25 GB, matches card) with bf16 generate; no compressed-domain compute, so benefit is download/storage only.
   - anchor `clean/bark_loader.py` @ 97ee639c: "Decoding is exact: the weights you get are the weights that were evaluated."
   - anchor `clean/bark_loader.py` @ 97ee639c: "return model.eval().to(device=device, dtype=dtype or torch.float32)"
   - anchor `clean/bark_mlx.py` @ 5ee2653f: "weights = {k: mx.array(v).astype(mx.float16) for k, v in to_mlx_names(sd).items()}"
   - anchor `clean/bark_mlx.py` @ 5ee2653f: "Unpacking needs about 6 GB of RAM once."
   - anchor `clean/README.md` @ 6d34ed86: "Small enough for a phone: 180 MB to download, 1.25 GB in memory once unpacked."
4. Card claims a native Swift/MLX runtime transcribes a 136 s recording in about 4 s on an M5 Max including model load (vendor figure, Apple silicon only); the reference loader is a stock transformers ParakeetForTDT (torch, device cpu/cuda/mps) plus an MLX path.
   - *unaffected*: Vendor M5 Max timing is Apple-only and unverifiable here; irrelevant to EPYC/MI210. Not re-measured.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dive-2341): (1) Verdict not_applicable for our stack: English-only fails the it/de/fr language gate; Clean is a lossy rewriting STT (deletes false starts and discourse markers) and no card example or evaluation shows content words survive; faithful turns (CS D6) need Plus-type verbatim output. Stage-1 recommended action "evaluate cleaned output as LLM front end" is withdrawn; replace by deriving a cleaned view downstream from verbatim text. (2) The 6.26 vs 4.94 Parakeet discrepancy is explained by cleaned references (see claim_corrections 2), not by a different model. (3) "Exact decode" is exact to the packed levels, not to the base fp32. (4) Cleanup cost visible in the card own table: Clean vs Plus on verbatim refs AMI +4.36, Earnings +1.85, GigaSpeech +1.99, VoxPopuli +0.31 (near-clean speech, so some collateral change cannot be excluded), SPGI -0.98 (refs already filler-free). Read from disk: README, NOTICE, bark_loader.py, bark_mlx.py, config, HF API json, model.bark header via Range, DisfluencySpeech dataset card, moondream ultra and nvidia cards. NOT read: model.bark payload, Plus coreml/ files, DisfluencySpeech paper PDF body, Open ASR Leaderboard code. Not run: any code or weights. Second reader: 30/30 quotes verbatim; 21 yes, 9 partial (table-row quotes lacking column headers), 0 no. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv:2510.06961 -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2346#record (openasr-2510.06961) - ConwayResearch Bark repos -> declined: dive's own reason (no separate need) - constriction -> declined: dive's own reason

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 (dive-2341): Plus README read in full. Plus is the faithful (verbatim) arm; it states "does not clean speech up". Same 627M-param packing structure as Clean (identical 99,973 B header size). Win over Parakeet is within the author cleaned-reference pipeline only. Verdict kept worth_investigating but relevance lowered to low: English-only, no EPYC speed evidence, runner is a build item (fp16 safetensors via reviewed one-time converter, then transformers ParakeetForTDT or ggml/ONNX route). Core ML/Windows runtimes irrelevant (Apple/NVIDIA). Not read: coreml packages.

2. [dive-added] Weights are CC-BY-4.0 and the config class is stock ParakeetForTDT, but the checkpoint stores 626.9M parameters as F16 (not float32 "full precision") and carries vad_head.* tensors the stock class has no module for. Photon = moondream client (Apache-2.0 repo) -> kestrel (public, no licence file, README "Local inference is free") -> kestrel-kernels (PyPI licence text "proprietary and confidential", "for use with Kestrel only"); NVIDIA CUDA and Apple Metal only; telemetry includes the hostname.
   - *narrowed*: Card/blog say 'full precision'; config.json dtype float32 but the safetensors stores 626.9M params as F16 and only 0.37M as F32 (a11). Footprint 1.26 GB.
   - *narrowed*: config architectures = ParakeetForTDT (a10) is true, but the checkpoint also carries vad_head.* tensors (a12) that the stock class has no module for, and the repo ships no preprocessor/processor config (only config.json + tokenizer.json). A stock runner reproduces the transducer, not Photon's VAD segmentation.
   - *reattributed*: Entry said 'Photon closed per secondary reports (low authority)'. Primary: Photon = pip packages moondream (client; Apache-2.0 repo) -> kestrel 0.9.1 (public repo without a LICENSE file; README: local inference free) -> kestrel-kernels (PyPI licence text 'proprietary and confidential', 'for use with Kestrel only'); NVIDIA CUDA + Apple Metal only; telemetry includes hostname (a19-a24).

## intake-2344#record — moondream/parakeet-redux: ternary (1.58-bit) Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 encoder, 178 MB, CPU AVX-512 VNNI / NEON / Metal

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/moondream/parakeet-redux

### Claims (as verified)

0. Card (Photon only, author-reported): 113x real time on 8 physical cores of one chiplet of an AMD EPYC 9575F (Zen 5, DDR5-6000), one utterance at a time over LibriSpeech test-clean, 2.5x the fastest other Parakeet runtime (parakeet.cpp q8_0, 45x); ternary 178 MB; WER 1.94 on that set.
   - *narrowed*: Arithmetic true (113/45=2.51). Photon-only, never independently reproduced: independent open runner gets 75.6x vs 43.1x Q8_0 (1.75x) on a 9950X3D, and only 9-12% faster on a 180 s clip. Hardware is EPYC 9575F (5 GHz boost, 8 cores = one CCD), not our 9655; batch=1 per utterance; short-utterance regime, so it matches conversational turns but not long-form.

4. [dive-added] The packed ternary format (thrush-ternary-v2: 5 base-3 digits per byte, LSD first, fp16 scale per row per 128 columns, w = scale*(code-1), pad digits 0) is fully specified by ternary.json and the safetensors header and is decodable without Photon.
   - *reattributed*: Entry notes said readability without Photon was unknown; dive resolves it: yes, and decoded layer-0 q_proj/v_proj give code histograms matching ternary.json zero_fraction to 4 decimals.
   - anchor `ternary.json $.weight_rule` @ moondream/parakeet-redux@2bf128600aac4b16946f7ed8372e56117fe5e23b: "w = scales[row, col // group_size] * (code - 1) with code in {0,1,2}"
   - anchor `ternary.json $.packing.digit_order` @ moondream/parakeet-redux@2bf128600aac4b16946f7ed8372e56117fe5e23b: "element i of a row is base-3 digit i%5 of byte i//5, least significant digit first; pad digits 0"
   - anchor `model.safetensors header (bytes 8..109247) key encoder.layers.0.self_attn.q_proj.qweight` @ moondream/parakeet-redux@2bf128600aac4b16946f7ed8372e56117fe5e23b: ""encoder.layers.0.self_attn.q_proj.qweight":{"dtype":"U8","shape":[1024,205],"data_offsets":[61159642,61369562]}"

   - anchor `ternary.json $.quant` @ moondream/parakeet-redux@2bf128600aac4b16946f7ed8372e56117fe5e23b: ""group_size": 128"

## intake-2346#record — Open ASR Leaderboard: Towards Reproducible and Transparent Multilingual and Long-Form Speech Recognition Evaluation

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.06961
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2341#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Normaliser (paper sec 2.3): all hypotheses and references are normalised before WER by removing punctuation and casing plus an English pipeline closely following Whisper (number normalisation, spelling standardisation, filler removal). The paper does not name the multilingual normaliser; the repo uses BasicMultilingualTextNormalizer (lower-case, bracket/parenthesis deletion, symbols to space) with optional num2words, remove_diacritics=False.
   - anchor ``: ""
1. References: the PAPER (v4, leaderboard state 27 Mar 2026) scores the standard ESB sets (hf-audio/esb-datasets-test-only-sorted, AMI 12643 utterances). The LIVE leaderboard no longer does: commit f51b134 (2026-07-08) "Switch to cleaned sets" moved AMI, GigaSpeech, VoxPopuli (cleaned_aa) and Earnings22 (Cleaned-AA, chunked, call-level refs) to cleaned references (AMI-cleaned has 7805 utterances, a different set not just different text), dropped TED-LIUM from the public English columns and added URGENT2024 and Voice Arena Monsoon. The standard sets survive only as an "extra" family.
   - *narrowed*: The paper text (v4) is true for its own 27 Mar 2026 snapshot but is stale as a description of the live leaderboard. A reader consulting the paper for "which refs does the leaderboard use" would get standard refs; the live leaderboard uses cleaned refs for AMI/Giga/Vox/E22 since 2026-07-08. Both statements are recorded with their dates.
   - anchor ``: ""
2. The Parakeet-v3 averages 4.94 (Bark), 6.26 (moondream) and 6.34 (NVIDIA card) are three different measurands, not three measurements of one. 6.34 = NVIDIA card 8-set mean incl. TED-LIUM on standard refs (paper/leaderboard-era 6.32). 6.26 = moondream 7-set mean (no TED-LIUM) on standard-ref rows. 4.94 = Bark 7-set mean on cleaned AMI/E22/Giga/Vox. The 4.94-vs-6.26 gap is entirely the four cleaned sets (per-set deltas AMI -1.44, E22 -4.90, Giga -0.06, Vox -2.83, LS and SPGI 0.00; sum/7 = -1.32 = 6.26 - 4.94 exactly). The 6.26-vs-6.32 residual is NOT explained by the paper: moondream is lower than the leaderboard standard-ref row on all seven shared sets (LS-c 1.52 vs 1.92, Giga 8.05 vs 9.57, ...), consistent with a post-paper scorer change (hypothesis, untested).
   - *narrowed*: Upstream dives 2341/2342/2343 attributed 4.94 vs 6.26 to cleaned refs (confirmed exactly, per-set arithmetic). They did not separate the set-count difference (7 vs 8 incl. TED-LIUM) or the scorer drift behind 6.26 vs 6.32/6.34; those are added here.
   - anchor ``: ""
3. The scorer is not stable. The paper-era scorer was evaluate.load("wer") on EnglishTextNormalizer output. Since the paper the repo added acronym/name/compound normalisation (2026-04-22), moved normalisation into scoring and aligned with kaldialign merge_compounds=True (2026-06-11), i.e. split-vs-joined compounds count as zero errors. The paper itself changed multilingual numbers between v1 and v4 (Whisper large-v3 DE/FR/IT/ES/PT 4.97/6.59/5.14/3.32/4.38 in v1/v3 vs 4.26/6.36/4.69/3.65/4.96 in v4). Any number quoted from "the leaderboard" needs a date, a scorer SHA and a set list.
   - anchor ``: ""
4. RTFx = total audio duration / transcription time, over a batch; measured on one A100-SXM4-80GB, batch 64 (reduced adaptively if memory-bound), bf16 PyTorch harnesses (NeMo, Transformers), length-sorted data, one warm-up then one timed pass. The authors state the absolute values may not transfer to other hardware. These are batch throughput numbers, not batch-1 latency or CPU/MI210 serving numbers; they rank architectures (TDT/CTC far above Whisper) but cannot be converted into our RTF.
   - *narrowed*: moondream card claims Photon 9743x vs 6005x RTFx on a B200; the comparable leaderboard figure for base v3 is 3330 on A100 batch 64 (dive 2343 already narrowed). This paper confirms the leaderboard figure is bf16 PyTorch batch throughput and explicitly not portable.
   - anchor ``: ""
5. Paper tables (v4): short-form EN Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3 WER 6.32 RTFx 3330; Whisper large-v3 7.44 / 146; large-v3-turbo 7.83 / 200 (Table 3). Multilingual Table 4: Parakeet v3 avg 4.81, RTFx 1720, DE/FR/IT/ES/PT 4.20/5.42/4.81/3.73/6.16; Whisper large-v3 avg 4.81, RTFx 111, 4.26/6.36/4.69/3.65/4.96. Whisper large-v3-turbo has NO row in Table 4; its multilingual numbers exist only in repo scripts/data/multilingual.csv (DE 6.21, FR 6.90, IT 5.58, ES 4.34, PT 4.67 derived by averaging that file own per-dataset columns, avg 5.56, RTFx 176). Long-form Table 5: Parakeet v3 10.7 / 1000, turbo 11.0 / 148, large-v3 11.2 / 68.6.
   - *unaffected*: Table 3/4/5 values reproduced verbatim from the v4 HTML; Table 4 per-language values cross-check against the repo CSV (language means of the 13 dataset columns reproduce the table to 0.01 for Parakeet v3 and Whisper large-v3).
   - anchor ``: ""
6. Multilingual Whisper rows are run with task=transcribe and language auto-detect (not forced), so the Whisper it/de/fr numbers include language-ID errors; the Table 4 average is the mean over 13 dataset columns (FLEURS, MLS, CoVoST/MCV), not over the five languages.
   - anchor ``: ""
7. Fit with Annex S P-STT-1: the leaderboard normaliser contains transforms Annex S 1.3 forbids (bracket/parenthesis deletion, spelling and name mapping, acronym joining, compound tolerance, filler deletion, digits on both sides). It must not become the primary instrument; a pinned leaderboard-scorer run is usable only as a labelled secondary cross-check.
   - anchor ``: ""

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 1: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). Expanded from intake-2341#record, intake-2343#record (first listed is expanded_from; the rest are in cross_references). 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - github.com/huggingface/open_asr_leaderboard @7e8d1a1 (scorer, normalizer, HF Jobs) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2352#record (openasr-repo) - hf datasets ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA (+ AA-WER methodology) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2353#record (aa-earnings22-cleaned) - arXiv:2210.13352 ESB benchmark (title resolved on abs page) -> declined: origin of the standard sets, no new question for the bake-off (dive's own reason) - HF blog open-asr-leaderboard-private-data ('Adding Benchmaxxer Repellant to the Open ASR Leaderboard') -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2354#record (hf-benchmaxxer-blog) - HF blog open-asr-leaderboard-global-south (Voice Arena / Monsoon) -> declined: Hindi, outside the it/de/fr/en gate (dive's own reason) - voi_oiwer (orthographically informed WER), kaldialign -> monitor (dive's own): voi_oiwer/kaldialign, not resolved to a paper; only if D1 compound handling needs a lattice scorer

### Verdict

Do not quote any "Open ASR leaderboard average" without its date, set list, reference track (standard vs cleaned) and scorer SHA: 4.94, 6.26, 6.32 and 6.34 are four different measurands for one model. Do not transfer leaderboard RTFx (A100, batch 64, bf16 PyTorch) to CPU or MI210 batch-1 serving. Do not adopt the leaderboard normaliser as the Annex S instrument (it deletes bracketed text, maps spellings/names, joins acronyms, forgives compounds, strips fillers). Use the leaderboard only for architecture ranking and as a labelled, SHA-pinned secondary cross-check.

- updated `handoffs/active/vidya-belief-substrate-program.md`

### Claims (as verified)

0. Model coverage (README Models table): NVIDIA Parakeet CTC (0.6B/1.1B), RNNT (0.6B/1.1B), TDT (v2 0.6B, 1.1B), TDT v3 0.6B (25 European languages incl. en/it/de/fr), hybrid TDT+CTC (110M, 1.1B), realtime EOU 120M, Nemotron 3.5 ASR streaming 0.6B (40+ locales), Nemotron-3-Diarization, plus Moondream Parakeet Ultra (F16 v3 derivative) and Redux (ternary encoder) and Silero VAD, CED sound events. Ultra/Redux are NOT NeMo-validated. nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b is NOT in the table and a case-insensitive tree scan at 5c9b0fbb finds no support.

That is BEHIND our production lineage (llama.cpp ffc1bac82 ships ggml 0.16.0; CLAUDE.md lists whisper.cpp at 0.18.0 and qwentts.cpp at 0.17.0), so it is a fourth ggml generation and needs its own tree and LD_LIBRARY_PATH.

5. Streaming and long-form: cache-aware streaming (--stream, C-API) exists ONLY for parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 and Nemotron 3.5 streaming; it is rejected for packed Redux; every other model (CTC/RNNT/TDT v2/v3/Ultra) is offline. Long audio is handled by single-pass encode or by `transcribe --vad`, which cuts at pauses into pieces of at most 30 s (Silero or the Ultra/Redux VAD head), offline and greedy only. The Parakeet VAD head false-alarms on speech-free audio (99% of frames), so Silero is the always-on gate.

### Verdict

Treat parakeet.cpp as the leading candidate runner for a Parakeet TDT v3 STT arm (CPU first; HIP gfx90a is an untested build item of 1-2 days, not a decline reason). Do not quote its RTFx as EPYC or MI210 numbers and do not treat its ggml (0.13.0) as compatible with our frozen trees; every figure here is author-reported on 9950X3D/GB10/NeMo-baseline hosts.

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Prefer this runner over NeMo-Speech.cpp for gfx90a only after a HIP smoke: it has a CPU degraded-mode story (VNNI, concurrency pool) that NeMo-Speech.cpp lacks, but its ggml is older than ours.

## intake-2348#record — handy-computer/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b-gguf: transcribe.cpp GGUF quantizations of NVIDIA Parakeet-Unified (one RNN-T model for offline and buffered streaming ASR), English

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/handy-computer/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b-gguf
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, quantization)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2344#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. 'Unified' means ONE set of weights that serves both offline (full attention) and streaming (chunk-limited attention with right context, dynamic chunk convolution) decoding, trained with mode-consistency regularization for RNNT (MCR-RNNT; arXiv:2604.19079). It is NOT a merged multi-model checkpoint and NOT a multilingual merge. nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b is a 0.6B FastConformer RNN-T (not TDT), English with punctuation and capitalization, trained on ~530k h (mostly Granary).
   - anchor `README.md: README intro` @ handy-computer/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b-gguf@843b1b175ace04f5a89d379c79e2518c0d065b25: "trained as a 'unified' streaming/offline model. This port runs the model in both offline and buffered streaming modes."
   - anchor `nv_README.md: NVIDIA card intro` @ nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b@fe53cd885760c96b6a5f51a0bfd362cb4584a98b: "combining both offline and streaming inference (with a minimum latency of 160ms) in one model"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1: "training a single model to perform well in both offline and low-latency streaming settings remains challenging"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1: "supports both offline and streaming decoding within a single model, using chunk-limited attention with right context and dynamic chunked convolutions"
1. NVIDIA card WER (HF OpenASR leaderboard sets, whisper-normalizer, no PnC): offline 5.91 vs 6.04 for parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2; streaming 6.14 (2.08 s), 6.29 (1.12 s), 6.52 (0.56 s), 6.70 (0.40 s), 6.92 (0.32 s), 7.35 (0.24 s), 8.44 (0.16 s), 15.63 (0.08 s). Below 160 ms NVIDIA recommends nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b. The GGUF re-measures LibriSpeech test-clean offline: F32 1.59, F16 1.59, Q8_0 1.60, Q6_K 1.61, Q5_K_M 1.58, Q4_K_M 1.62 vs NVIDIA 1.63; buffered-streaming parity vs NeMo within 0.02 pp on 5 of 6 (L,C,R) configs (test-clean-512); the 80 ms config is an outlier at ~5.6-5.8%.
   - *unaffected*: Handy F32 1.59 vs NVIDIA 1.63 on test-clean agrees within 0.04 pp; independent re-measure by a third party (Handy), not a failed reproduction.

- 2026-10-21 stage2b: first read of this repo; narrowed licence expectation (claim 5) and the popularity metric (claim 6). ### Verdict

"Unified" = one Parakeet RNN-T model that works offline and streaming (NVIDIA, Apr 2026), English only, NVIDIA Open Model License, converted not retrained. The GGUF is unique to transcribe.cpp. Treat all speeds as M4/Ryzen 4750U author numbers; do not read them as EPYC or gfx90a results.

## intake-2349#record — NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp: official ggml runtime for NVIDIA Nemotron/Parakeet ASR, TTS, diarization (Apache-2.0)

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, quantization, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2343#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Official NVIDIA ggml speech runtime (Apache-2.0) for the Nemotron Speech family; ASR heads CTC, RNNT and TDT are all in the converter (head auto-detected). Supported ASR checkpoints: Nemotron 3.5 ASR Streaming 0.6B, Nemotron Speech Streaming 0.6B, Parakeet TDT 0.6B v3, Parakeet CTC 1.1B. No Canary/AED and no Whisper-style decoder.
   - *narrowed*: dive-2343 a39 summarised as parakeet offline only: true for TDT v3 only; Parakeet CTC 1.1B supports buffered streaming (docs/asr/models.md). TDT/CTC/RNNT support confirmed.
   - anchor `README.md:3` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "A lightweight native C++ runtime for the NVIDIA Nemotron Speech model family, with broad hardware support."
1. Converter accepts only NeMo-format input: a .nemo archive, an extracted NeMo dir (model_config.yaml + model_weights.ckpt torch pickle), or an HF repo ID from which only the single .nemo file is downloaded. It does not read safetensors or HF-transformers key names, so moondream/parakeet-ultra cannot be converted as is.
   - *unaffected*: dive-2343 a38 (.nemo only) confirmed at the new head. README says converts NeMo and Hugging Face checkpoints, but the HF path downloads the .nemo file only (conversion/source.py).
   - anchor `docs/model-conversion.md:25` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "the converter downloads only the `.nemo` checkpoint through the standard"
   - anchor `conversion/source.py:download_nemo_checkpoint` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "raise RuntimeError(f"no .nemo checkpoint found in Hugging Face repository {repo_id}")"
   - anchor `conversion/source.py:find_checkpoint_files` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "elif name == "model_weights.ckpt" and found["weights"] is None:"
   - anchor `moondream/parakeet-ultra model.safetensors header` @ moondream/parakeet-ultra@73175eb7aeb0d82f1e2a6b53b3aabc10a90bcd0b: ""encoder_projector.weight""
   - anchor `src/asr/decoders/rnnt_modules.cpp:175` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): ""joint.joint_net.2", cfg_.joint_dim, cfg_.joint_output_size(), true);"
2. CUDA-only fused rel-pos attention op (GGML_OP_FUSED_ATTN, ~1700 lines of CUDA, no CPU implementation) plus 15 other CUDA/CPU ggml patches; build presets are cpu/cuda/metal/vulkan, with no HIP preset or CMake option. The runtime has an unfused graph path used whenever patches are off (Vulkan/Metal presets) or use_gpu is false.
   - *narrowed*: dive-2344 said fused-attention/CUDA patches have no HIP path (2-4 days or unfused). Refined: the unfused path is a proven stock-ggml route (Vulkan/Metal presets); HIP needs a CMake hook (~20 lines) plus ggml-hip; porting the fused kernel needs 3-arg shuffles replaced and __shfl_down_sync, which the ggml hip.h shim does not define.
   - anchor `patches/ggml-add-fused-attention-op...patch:14` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "and by the MagpieTTS persistent decoder. There is no CPU implementation."
   - anchor `src/asr/encoder/rel_pos_attention.cpp:240` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "// stock-ggml build (NEMO_SPEECH_GGML_PATCHED=OFF) must not reference it"
   - anchor `CMakeLists.txt:142` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "# GGML_CUDA is forwarded directly to ggml's CMake via -DGGML_CUDA=ON."
   - anchor `patches/ggml-add-fused-attention-op...patch:~193` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "+ value += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, value, offset);"
   - anchor `ggml-cuda/vendors/hip.h@bd4f514d:34` @ llama.cpp@bd4f514db14d87fded667787a7a963bfbaa98e89: "#define __shfl_xor_sync(mask, var, laneMask, width) __shfl_xor(var, laneMask, width)"

3. Offline-only for Parakeet TDT (streaming rejected); long audio beyond the 5000-frame positional limit (~6.6 min) is split at the quietest 100 ms window near the limit and decoded segment by segment; Silero VAD is optional feature masking, not segmentation. Server is an OpenAI-compatible subset (POST /v1/audio/transcriptions, realtime WebSocket) plus a separate Riva-compatible gRPC server.
   - anchor `docs/asr/models.md:Parakeet TDT` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "The model does not support cache-aware streaming; inference is full-utterance"

   - anchor `docs/asr/configuration.md:VAD feature masking` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "Optional Silero VAD masks silence features before the encoder. It remains off"
   - anchor `docs/api.md:POST /v1/audio/transcriptions` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "accepted for client compatibility; this server uses its one loaded ASR model"
4. Published speed and WER numbers are for Nemotron Speech Streaming EN 0.6B (not Parakeet) on LibriSpeech test-clean: RTX 4090 66.6x RTFX at 160 ms chunks WER 2.66% vs NeMo FP32 2.69%; CPU i7-11700K 5.6x WER 3.00% on a 100-utterance subset. No AMD GPU, no EPYC and no Parakeet-TDT figure is published.
   - *unaffected*: dive-2343/2344: published CPU benchmark is Nemotron streaming; confirmed.
   - anchor `BENCHMARK.md:CPU` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "Intel Core i7-11700K, 8 threads for both engines, on a 100-utterance subset of test-clean (13.3 min)."
   - anchor `README.md:Performance` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 (head 2026-10-21, VERSION 0.2.0): "Streaming speech recognition with Nemotron Speech Streaming 0.6B (Q8_0), 160 ms chunks:"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 1: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). Expanded from intake-2343#record, intake-2344#record (first listed is expanded_from; the rest are in cross_references). 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - github.com/mudler/parakeet.cpp -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2347#record (parakeet-cpp-mudler) - nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2355#record (nemotron35-asr-streaming) - nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3 -> declined: conditional recommendation never triggered; nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3 is the base of intake-2343#record/2344 and already covered there - nvidia sortformer/Nemotron-3-Diarization -> declined: dive's own reason (diarization out of scope) - nvidia/parakeet-ctc-1.1b -> declined: dive's own reason - NemotronLabs-VoiceChat-11B, magpie_tts -> NemotronLabs-VoiceChat-11B declined (dive's own); magpie_tts selected at the wave-3 close-out and dived as intake-2384#record (magpie-tts-multilingual)

### Verdict

NeMo-Speech.cpp is the vendor reference for NVIDIA ggml speech models and strong for Nemotron streaming, but for OUR Parakeet TDT/ultra bake-off it is second choice to mudler/parakeet.cpp: converter takes .nemo only, no HIP wiring, offline TDT only. Its headline numbers are Nemotron-streaming on NVIDIA/Intel hardware and must not be cited as Parakeet or EPYC/MI210 speed. Do not infer that the fused-attention CUDA patch blocks a HIP build: the unfused path is stock ggml.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Do not adopt as the STT bake-off runner; keep as vendor reference for Nemotron 3.5 streaming. If a second ggml STT arm is wanted beyond mudler, build it unpatched (NEMO_SPEECH_GGML_PATCHED=OFF) with a new GGML_HIP hook in an experimental tree.

### Claims (as verified)

0. s2s.cpp (ServeurpersoCom, MIT, created 2026-09-17, 14 stars) is a C++17 ggml speech-to-speech LOOP, not a model: echo cancellation (LocalVQE v1.3), Silero VAD v5 (CPU, 1 thread), Smart Turn v3.2 end-of-turn (CPU), Parakeet Ultra (or base v3) STT, Qwen3-TTS 1.7B via qwentts.cpp, with the LLM left to any external OpenAI-compatible chat endpoint; OpenAI-Realtime-style WebSocket, full-duplex barge-in, MCP/agentic tool rounds. It is the same cascade shape as our CS interlocutor (VAD -> turn detect -> STT -> external LLM -> qwentts), built on the qwentts.cpp lineage we already froze.

1. Serveurperso/Parakeet-Ultra-GGUF ships F32 / Q8_0 / Q6_K / Q5_K_M / Q4_K_M GGUFs of moondream/parakeet-ultra for s2s.cpp; the card recommends Q8_0 (668 MB) and says Q4_K_M drifts (flips hesitation words). Parity is stage-by-stage against transformers ParakeetForTDT on ONE example recording (Q8_0 encoder cossim 0.999777, identical transcript; F32 0.999999). No WER is reported for any language.
   - *narrowed*: Card calls F32 'reference, source of the quants'. The F32 GGUF is the checkpoint's F16 values widened by convert.py (astype float32); it carries no precision beyond the F16 checkpoint (intake-2343#record dive a11). The cossim 0.999999 therefore measures the BN fold plus runtime numerics, not fidelity to a higher-precision original. Parity is one recording; no WER.

2. Backends: CPU (BLAS, ggml CPU variants), CUDA, Metal, Vulkan, SYCL. NO HIP: the build scripts have no HIP variant and the CMake backend-link loop is `cpu blas cuda metal vulkan sycl`. But the pinned ggml (ServeurpersoCom/ggml cc5df49, GGML_VERSION 0.27.0) contains a ggml-hip backend (ROCm >= 6.1, FP8 header guarded to HIP >= 6.3, so ROCm 6.2 skips FP8) and a thread-strided bitonic argsort. The README names Vulkan as its AMD route. A gfx90a build is therefore a one-line CMake omission plus flags, not a missing backend.
   - *narrowed*: intake-2343#record's dive recorded 'no HIP stated'. True, and the build scripts/CMake link loop omit hip, but the pinned ggml has a HIP backend (ROCm >= 6.1) so the gap is a build-system omission, not a missing backend. The cost estimate for L3 narrows accordingly (see DIVE.md Q5). Not yet built by anyone: nothing here proves it compiles on ROCm 6.2/gfx90a.

3. GGUF fidelity: convert.py widens every checkpoint tensor to float32 (so the 'F32' GGUF is the F16 checkpoint losslessly widened, not extra precision), folds each conformer BatchNorm into the depthwise kernel (numerically changes weights in fp32; eps 1e-5), sums the two LSTM biases, and never reads vad_head.* (the transformers reference itself lists vad_head.* as UNEXPECTED). Quantisation keeps conv kernels, sinusoid table, relative-attention biases and 1-D tensors at F32; all 2-D weights take the K-quant of the variant.
   - *unaffected*: vad_head dropped as the card states; confirmed independently by the converter's explicit tensor list and the transformers load report.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 1: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). Slug references in this entry resolve as: silero-vad=intake-2360#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - ServeurpersoCom/qwentts.cpp upstream master 5916bf7 (our frozen fork's upstream) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2361#record (qwentts-upstream) - ServeurpersoCom/ggml @cc5df49 (v0.27.0) as a ggml generation -> declined: captured in this entry's notes (dive's own reason) - Serveurperso/Parakeet-TDT-0.6b-v3-GGUF (base GGUF, same runner) -> declined: same runner/method as the Ultra GGUF; pinned in the L1 harness (dive's own reason) - llama.cpp PR 28957 "CUDA: bitonic argsort handles rows wider than one block" (arrived as llama/28957) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2362#record (llamacpp-pr28957-argsort) - Third-party Ultra ports: cstr/parakeet-ultra-GGUF (CrispASR), trevest, efeemece (parakeet.cpp), Nairod785 + dawsonvosburg (transcribe.cpp / NeMo-Speec -> declined (dive's own) except mudler/parakeet.cpp and NeMo-Speech.cpp (dived as intake-2347#record (parakeet-cpp-mudler), intake-2349#record (nemo-speech-cpp)); transcribe.cpp was later selected and dived as intake-2359#record (transcribe-cpp) - pipecat-ai/smart-turn (intake-2047#record) and LocalAI-org/LocalVQE (intake-2018#record) -> declined: already ingested, cross-referenced (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

A reader should conclude s2s.cpp is the best-matched OPEN reference for our whole interlocutor cascade and a ready ggml runner for Ultra (MIT, Q8_0 GGUF), and that gfx90a support is a small, unproven build item rather than a blocker. A reader must NOT conclude: that Ultra GGUF WER is verified (only 1-clip parity), that HIP builds exist (none), that its CPU/CUDA timings transfer to EPYC/MI210, or that adopting s2s.cpp changes the Annex S STT oracle (whisper.cpp production-speech-v1 stays the oracle until CS-2 is ratified).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): L3 (from intake-2343#record) re-costed: build ONLY parakeet-core + parakeet-transcribe from s2s.cpp @e620fab with -DGGML_HIP=ON -DAMDGPU_TARGETS=gfx90a plus `hip` added to the backend loop, in an experimental tree with its own LD_LIBRARY_PATH; parity vs transformers on CS-4 clips; do NOT build s2s-server/TTS on this ggml for CS-12 (the frozen qwentts stays).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Mine docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (turn state machine, defaults, revision/grace logic, barge-in cancel flag, recognizer/responder split) as design input for CS-20/22/23 and the language-gate-compatible STT arm.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Close or re-scope CS-43 after checking that ggml 0.27 argsort (llama.cpp #28957) is equivalent to our b86f660.

## intake-2351#record — Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx and -optimized-onnx: ONNX / MatMulNBits exports of Parakeet Ultra and base v3, with a paired FLEURS-validation benchmark

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, quantization, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2343#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Olicorne (a single HF/GitHub maintainer, also author of parakeet_web) publishes browser/CPU ONNX exports. 'optimized-onnx' is the BASE nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3 export and is marked 'probably superseded' by 'ultra-onnx', which is moondream/parakeet-ultra: HF safetensors key-renamed back onto the base .nemo (weights from moondream, VAD head dropped), then re-exported with the same pipeline. Fidelity of the Ultra export was checked on one JFK clip plus the FLEURS macro.
   - *reattributed*: intake-2343#record called the 'optimized-onnx' repo a source for the Ultra measurement. It is the BASE-model export (sunset, 'probably superseded'); only the base-arm 13.11 comes from it; Ultra numbers come from ultra-onnx.
   - anchor `ultra-onnx README How it was built [readme_Olicorne_parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx.md]` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx @ 3b80a92332b5d34c364a35a880ef049c7fede9f2: "a key rename only, every learned weight comes from moondream's safetensors, the VAD head is dropped."
   - anchor `ultra-onnx README How it was built [readme_Olicorne_parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx.md]` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx @ 3b80a92332b5d34c364a35a880ef049c7fede9f2: "Checked: the ONNX transcribes the JFK clip word for word like moondream's PyTorch model run in NeMo."
   - anchor `optimized README Licence [readme_Olicorne_optimized.md]` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-optimized-onnx @ 2c4379d0af488063bcf0cc177c231b094e26ab29: "The weights themselves are not fine-tuned or otherwise retrained."
   - anchor `optimized README banner [readme_Olicorne_optimized.md]` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-optimized-onnx @ 2c4379d0af488063bcf0cc177c231b094e26ab29: "> **Probably superseded, prefer [`Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx`](https://huggingface.co/Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-onnx).** That repository ships the same browser-ready layout and precisions, and this one is most likely only worse than it, so it should probably not be used an..."
1. 'Optimized' means graph and storage engineering with no retraining: export through a NeMo fork with `--web-optimized` (mask-free encoder, runtime positional encoding, 18 vs 90 Where ops), constant-fold 4682 -> 2110 nodes (bit-exact gate), 12-low-mantissa-bit rounding for compressibility (still fp32, 'more precise than an fp16 cast'), fp32 sharding under 2 GB, decoder int8 dynamic quantisation plus in-graph log-sum-exp / top-16 outputs, pointwise Conv1d -> MatMul so 288 MiB of encoder weights can enter MatMulNBits, then fp16 / MatMulNBits int8 (block 64) / w4a8 (block 32) encoders. Not fused custom ops: standard ONNX / ORT contrib ops.
   - *unaffected*: Optimisations are standard-graph engineering; no fused custom kernels. Nothing disputed.

2. Paired FLEURS result: 25-language macro WER on the VALIDATION split (8,967 clips), greedy, onnxruntime CUDA, `<unk>` banned, both reruns dated 2026-10-07: base v3 fp32 13.11 % vs Ultra fp32 10.95 % (-2.16 abs, -16.5 % rel). Moondream's card on the Open-ASR-leaderboard normalisers/test split: 11.62 vs 9.55 (-2.07 abs, -17.8 % rel). Direction and magnitude of the macro gain replicate on a non-Photon runner; absolutes sit ~1.5 pp higher (different split, normaliser and runner).

5. The `<unk>` ban is an Olicorne modification (joint output bias for `<unk>` set to -1e4) that matters for Ultra: ultra fp32 scored 11.55 on CPU before the ban and 10.95 on CUDA after it (0.60 pp, backend and ban confounded), while base v3 moved by <= 0.03 pp; on FLEURS fr the ban lowered Ultra WER 3.99 -> 3.74. The published 13.11 vs 10.95 pair is therefore a post-ban, CUDA-vs-CUDA pair; the pre-ban/CPU Ultra figure would give a -1.56 pp gap. Any runner we use must count `<unk>` emissions or ban them uniformly across arms.

6. Runtime reach: the harness configures only CUDAExecutionProvider and CPUExecutionProvider; the CPU EP smoke test passed on all six graphs and int8 MatMulNBits is the stated CPU/WASM default, so EPYC onnxruntime-CPU is supported by the artefacts (speed unmeasured; the only CPU figure is a 607 s long-pass proc/dur 0.234 on an unnamed CPU). Official onnxruntime-migraphx wheels exist on PyPI (1.27.1, 2026-07-12), but nothing in the repos exercises MIGraphX/ROCm: MI210 via ONNX is untested, and whether the MatMulNBits contrib ops are supported by the MIGraphX EP is unverified.
   - *uncertain*: Whether onnxruntime-migraphx can run these graphs on gfx90a (MatMulNBits support, dynamic time axis, ROCm version of the wheel) was not determinable from any source read; no one has published it.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 1: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web @3521817 (harness + hf-to-nemo.py + MatMulNBits scripts) -> declined: pinned and used via O1 (dive's own reason) - Olicorne/UltiMed-ASR-FR-v1 (dataset/scripts) and parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx (French medical fine-tune of Ultra: decoder change x1.5, phra -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2358#record (olicorne-ultimed) - Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-redux-onnx and redux-UltiMed-experimental-onnx (2-bit hybrid) -> routed to intake-2344#record (dive's own); not separately ingested - microsoft/onnxruntime MIGraphX EP (docs + op-support list) and onnxruntime-migraphx wheel metadata -> declined: not selected at any close-out; ingest was conditional on the O3 trigger, which did not fire in Stage 2 - istupakov/onnx-asr (MIT) @a2fd7530 -> declined: covered here as the runner for O1/O2 (dive's own reason) - beshkenadze/parakeet-ultra-onnx, mldecode/parakeet-ultra-onnx-int8 and sherpa-onnx-parakeet-ultra-int8 -> declined: restatements of the same conversion (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

A reader should conclude the Olicorne pair is the best available non-Photon evidence that Ultra's weights (not Photon) carry a ~2 pp macro FLEURS improvement over base v3, that a CPU-EP int8 ONNX is a ready candidate for the CS-26 fallback, and that Olicorne's harness is reusable. A reader must NOT conclude: that the it/de/fr deltas are replicated (no per-language data), that the absolutes match the card, that the improvement is independent of Olicorne's <unk> ban, or that MI210 can run these ONNX graphs (untested).

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Re-run the parakeet_web `wer-quants.py` FLEURS harness (public, pinned 3521817) on it/de/fr + en with fp32 and int8 ONNX under onnxruntime CPU on EPYC (no GPU) to obtain per-language Ultra-vs-base ORT numbers that the READMEs do not publish; score with the CS-2/Annex S normaliser as well as theirs; log <unk> counts.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Treat the int8 Ultra ONNX (650 MB + 18 MB, CPU EP) as the CS-26 CPU degraded-fallback candidate to measure (RTF on EPYC 9655, WER on it/de/fr/en); w4a8 is not recommended (+0.71 pp).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Optionally, a bounded MIGraphX probe: onnxruntime-migraphx on MI210 with the fp16/fp32 encoder (plain Conv/MatMul, no MatMulNBits); stop at first unsupported-op fallback.

### Claims (as verified)

0. The committed per-model CSV that carries the paper-era Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v3 row (avg 6.32; AMI 11.39, E22 11.19, Giga 9.57, LS-c 1.92, LS-o 3.59, SPGI 3.98, TED-LIUM 2.80, Vox 6.09) is a 2026-03-26 snapshot: the README labels scripts/data "CSV results as of 26 March 2026" and git -S shows the row was added once, in 090df12 on 2026-03-26, and never rescored. 2. 6.26 versus 6.32 is a coincidence of two opposite effects, not a replication. On the SAME seven standard-reference sets the March scorer gives 6.82 (47.73/7); dropping TED-LIUM from the 8-set mean raises it by +0.50 (6.32 -> 6.82), and the current scorer lowers every one of the seven sets, mean -0.59 (live standard-ref columns 6.23; per set AMI -0.81, E22 -0.42, Giga -1.51, LS-c -0.40, LS-o -0.46, SPGI -0.35, Vox -0.20; relative -3% to -21%). Moondream states it used the leaderboard pipeline "as of September 2026"; its seven standard-ref rows reproduce the live leaderboard store (hf-audio/open-asr-leaderboard-results @f3f9949, 2026-10-19) to within 0.02 on six sets and 0.28 on AMI (10.86 vs 10.58); Bark's Parakeet row (4.94) equals the live cleaned-track columns exactly. So the 04-22 and 06-11 changes are part of the cause but not the whole: at least five more steps sit in the same window, and per-change magnitude is not recoverable without running the scorer (not run here).
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 wrote that 6.26 vs 6.32 "is NOT explained by the paper". Settled here: the live results store contains standard-reference columns for Parakeet v3 (AMI 10.58, E22 10.77, Giga 8.06, LS-c 1.52, LS-o 3.13, SPGI 3.63, Vox 5.89; mean 6.23) that reproduce moondream's 6.26 within 0.03 on the mean. The gap to 6.32 is a scorer shift (-0.59 on seven sets) cancelled by the set-count change (-TED-LIUM, +0.50). Not a measurement of the same thing twice.

3. Scoring is pooled corpus WER per dataset (summed ins+del+sub over summed reference words, kaldialign merge_compounds=True) with no confidence interval (the repo benchmark_fitting README says it matches the leaderboard in publishing none). The headline "avg" is the unweighted mean of the per-dataset WERs that are PRESENT for that model, so the set count silently varies: the live Parakeet-v3 avg is 6.51 over nine public columns (incl. URGENT2024 11.52 and Monsoon 4.14), not the 4.94 seven-set figure, and live RTFx is 6228 (H200, HF Jobs) versus the paper's 3330 (A100).

4. Multilingual scoring (what our it/de/fr gate would be compared against): lower-case; delete anything in <>/[]/() on both sides; map EVERY symbol and punctuation character, including the apostrophe and hyphen, to a space (NFKC, diacritics kept); convert every digit run to words with num2words on BOTH sides; then forgive compounds twice (normalize_compound_pairs, which rewrites both sides when a mismatch region is character-identical ignoring spaces, followed by kaldialign merge_compounds=True). Consequences: French/Italian elision becomes two tokens (l'homme -> "l homme"), which enlarges the WER denominator relative to a token-preserving normaliser; German/Italian split-vs-joined compounds cost nothing; reference digits are rewritten, which Annex S forbids. Whisper multilingual rows are run with the language auto-detected (run_whisper_ml.sh passes no --language) whereas Voxtral/Cohere harnesses force it, so language-ID error is inside the Whisper WER only.
   - *reattributed*: Wave-1 ledger D1 proposed "a fr/it elision rule (apostrophe kept...)" as an addition to stt_norm/v2. Reading the HF multilingual path: it does not keep the apostrophe, it splits on it. The Annex S choice (keep one token) is therefore a deliberate divergence from the leaderboard, with a denominator consequence, not an alignment with it.

5. The instrument is not hash-pinned and has a history of being gamed through the harness. requirements_jobs.txt pins kaldialign==0.12.0 but leaves num2words unpinned (a dictionary-driven digit-to-word table that can change under the scorer). On 2026-05-22 a merged submission (SoundsgoodAI Zipformer XL) added code that rewrites its hypotheses using the reference ("Add spaces in predicted acronyms to match references"; AMI 15.97 -> 14.43 in the committed CSV). On 2026-06-11 the maintainers moved normalisation into scoring and the PR template now requires run_eval.py to save raw outputs; harnesses are told not to touch the reference, but the scorer itself still contains pair-aware rewriting (normalize_compound_pairs, merge_compounds).

   - anchor `benchmark_fitting/README.md section 1, Method` @ huggingface/open_asr_leaderboard @ 7e8d1a1003c201324e9003dd5bc34244340a63b0 (HEAD, committed 2026-10-21 17:27 +0200; cloned 2026-10-21): "The output reports denominators and eligible clip counts, but not confidence"
7. Long calls are scored by concatenating chunk predictions in chunk_index order against the call-level reference, with a warning that a missing chunk inflates deletions. This is the mechanism that makes Earnings22-Cleaned-AA-chunked (341 chunks of <=30 s from 6 calls) comparable with whole-call scoring.
   - anchor `merge_chunked_manifest docstring` @ huggingface/open_asr_leaderboard @ 7e8d1a1003c201324e9003dd5bc34244340a63b0 (HEAD, committed 2026-10-21 17:27 +0200; cloned 2026-10-21): "Chunks are non-overlapping and cover the session in order, so predictions are"
   - anchor `merge_chunked_manifest warning` @ huggingface/open_asr_leaderboard @ 7e8d1a1003c201324e9003dd5bc34244340a63b0 (HEAD, committed 2026-10-21 17:27 +0200; cloned 2026-10-21): "which will inflate the deletion count."
8. Fit with Annex S P-STT-1 (stt_norm/v2): copy the engineering (raw outputs stored, normalise at score time, score cache keyed on manifest SHA-256, chunk-concatenate scoring with a contiguity check, per-set labelled means); forbid the linguistics (bracket deletion, name/spelling/compound tables, acronym joining, filler regex families, digits on both sides, apostrophe-to-space, pair-aware or lattice forgiveness as the default). Each forbidden item is already outside Annex S 1.3 except two that need an explicit sentence: (a) the normaliser takes ONE string and no reference/hypothesis partner, (b) every third-party package a normaliser imports is version-pinned and covered by the recorded hash.
   - anchor `P-STT-1 sec 1.3 step 6` @ epyc-root measurement/protocols/speech.md (working tree 2026-10-21; copy saved as src/annexS_speech.md): "Direction: **hypothesis → reference form**, never both sides → digits."
   - anchor `P-STT-1 sec 1.3 step 4` @ epyc-root measurement/protocols/speech.md (working tree 2026-10-21; copy saved as src/annexS_speech.md): "**Apostrophes preserved.**"
   - anchor `P-STT-1 sec 1.3 forbidden transforms` @ epyc-root measurement/protocols/speech.md (working tree 2026-10-21; copy saved as src/annexS_speech.md): "No synonym or homophone mapping."

### Verdict

Do not quote "6.26", "6.32" or "6.34" as if they measured one thing: 6.32 = March scorer, 8 sets with TED-LIUM, standard refs; 6.23-6.26 = current scorer, 7 sets, standard refs; 4.94 = current scorer, 7 sets, cleaned refs; 6.51 = live avg over nine columns. Do not treat the repo CSVs in scripts/data as the live leaderboard (March snapshot). Do not adopt the HF English or multilingual normaliser as the Annex S instrument; copy its engineering (raw outputs, score-time normalisation, SHA-keyed cache, chunk-concatenation) not its linguistics. Do not claim the 04-22 and 06-11 commits alone explain the shift: at least five more behavioural changes sit in the window and none was measured separately.

## intake-2353#record — ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA (and -chunked) with the AA-WER v2 methodology

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2346#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. "Cleaned" means a manual verbatim correction of the REFERENCE TEXT only, performed by Artificial Analysis (AA) on six earnings calls. Corrections: wrong or misheard words and contractions; repetitions restored where the speaker really repeated ("the the"); incomplete word fragments ("evac-") removed; ungrammatical-but-clear speaker wording kept verbatim. Elements the Whisper normaliser already handles (case, punctuation, FILLER WORDS) were deliberately left in the references. AA reports model WER on its subset fell on average 5.6 pp after cleaning and no model got worse. Audio is unchanged.
   - *unaffected*: Correction types and the "fillers left in" statement were read directly from the card and the AA article and agree with each other.
   - anchor `README "Dataset Correction"` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "Elements already normalized by the [Whisper normalizer](https://github.com/openai/whisper/tree/main/whisper/normalizers) package (e.g., capitalization, punctuation, and filler words) were not modified"
   - anchor `README "Dataset Correction" bullet 2` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "**Missed words:** Retained or added repetitions for verbatim accuracy"
   - anchor `README "Dataset Correction" bullet 3` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "**Partial stuttering:** Removed incomplete word fragments"
   - anchor `README "Motivation for Correction"` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "On average, model WER on Earnings22 went down 5.6 percentage points (p.p.) after cleaning, and no models had higher WER after cleaning"
1. It is a DIFFERENT utterance set, not the leaderboard's Earnings22 test with fixes. Earnings22-Cleaned-AA is six whole calls (about 115 min, 15,814 reference words, 14.6-22.4 min each, one call = 21.6% of all words); the leaderboard's standard Earnings22 test config (hf-audio/open-asr-leaderboard, 2,741 utterances, 5.43 h) is six OTHER calls. Re-derived at 2026-10-21: the two sets of Rev call ids have empty intersection (AA: 4483046, 4474955, 4468919, 4482968, 4471586, 4475604; leaderboard standard: 4432298, 4450488, 4470290, 4479741, 4483338, 4485244; all twelve resolve in Rev's 125-call metadata.csv). The leaderboard's chunked track is the AA six cut into 341 chunks of at most 30 s with Silero VAD and scored by concatenating chunk hypotheses against the call-level reference.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 DIVE.md section 1 said 4.94 vs 6.26 "is fully the four cleaned sets" and ledger D8 said cleaned references are a different utterance set (AMI 7805 vs 12643). Both stand arithmetically. Added here: for Earnings22 the cleaned track is a disjoint set of six calls, so the -4.90 pp E22 term in that decomposition cannot be read as a reference-correction effect; it also carries a change of calls (and of scorer, chunking and run).
   - anchor `README "Dataset Summary" table` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "| Number of samples | 6 |"
   - anchor `README "Dataset Summary" table` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "| Total duration | ~115 minutes |"
   - anchor `dataset_info config earnings22 (the leaderboard standard track)` @ hf:datasets/hf-audio/open-asr-leaderboard @de79e57adf9983993bcc72586d7313460825f5eb README (wave-1 copy): "num_examples: 2741"
   - anchor `set_diff.json /overlap_calls (script set_diff.py over datasets-server rows of hf-audio/open-asr-leaderboard earnings22 test and the AA jsonl ids)` @ derived 2026-10-21 from src/e22.jsonl (sha256 9679063e...) and oal_e22_ids.json (sha256 0dafd66a...): ""overlap_calls": []"
   - anchor `chunked README "Overview"` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA-chunked @27f7d0056d593231974a752ce03c1e965da69b3f (lastModified 2026-06-29): "| Number of chunks | 341 |"
2. Licence: the Hub card says Apache-2.0 and defers to esb/datasets for "upstream terms"; ESB and Rev publish Earnings-22 under CC-BY-SA-4.0, and the cleaned references are an adaptation of the Rev transcripts over unchanged Rev audio. The Apache-2.0 tag cannot override a share-alike upstream, so the conservative reading is CC-BY-SA-4.0 (attribution, share-alike on adapted material). Internal evaluation, with the audio fetched by pinned revision and only hashes and scores committed, needs no redistribution; copying the audio or references into the public epyc-root repository would. Not legal advice.
   - anchor `README "License"` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): "This dataset is released under Apache-2.0. For upstream terms, see [esb/datasets](https://huggingface.co/datasets/esb/datasets)."

It is licensed under CC-BY-SA-4.0."
3. Provenance of the six calls is disputed and unanswered. The card says the data is a subset of the ESB English TEST split; a community discussion (#2, opened 2026-08-12, still open and unanswered at fetch) says the six audios are in the ESB TRAIN split. This dive could verify one half only: none of the six calls is in the leaderboard's standard test split. The dataset field reads "earnings_22_full_test", the name of the 125-call "full" config of distil-whisper/earnings22 whose single split is labelled "test" (125 rows, verified via datasets-server), which would explain a "test" label for calls drawn from the full 125-call corpus. Whether any model under evaluation trained on these calls is unknown; a leaderboard-trained-on-ESB model could have seen them.
   - *narrowed*: The upstream card claim "subset of ESB test" is not confirmed: not in the leaderboard standard test (verified), possibly in ESB train (community claim, unverified, unanswered).
   - anchor `discussion #2 "Clarification on the Dataset Split Used for the Cleaned Version"` @ hf discussion page, fetched 2026-10-21, status open, no reply from the dataset owner at fetch time: "I downloaded the esb/datasets data and found that the six provided audios are actually part of the training set."
   - anchor `earnings22_cleaned_aa_v1.jsonl, field "dataset" of every row` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA @5999936ee3d8d3cde160e3c65a8cc6ff5b7c5276 (lastModified 2026-02-17): ""dataset": "earnings_22_full_test""
4. AA scores with its OWN normaliser, not the HF one: jiwer on a custom extension of Whisper's EnglishTextNormalizer (digit splitting, leading-zero and spoken-symbol rules, ":00" stripping, US/UK variants, ID/phone formats, URLs and units, and "ambiguous proper nouns in our dataset" such as Mateo/Matteo), results audio-duration-weighted per dataset. The normaliser is not published (an unanswered community request on the sibling VoxPopuli dataset) and has had three releases in 2026 (v2.0 Feb, v2.1 Apr, v2.2 May to current) after v1.0 in Sep 2025. It contains dataset-fitted name equivalences, the pattern Annex S 1.3 forbids as synonym mapping.
   - anchor `methodology, Normalization Process` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text (curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 d0590cba...; text copy src/methodology.txt sha256 b1775b5a...; version v2.2 "May 2026-current"): "Accepted spelling variants where meaning is unchanged, including US / UK English and ambiguous proper nouns in our dataset"
   - anchor `methodology, WER Calculation` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text (curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 d0590cba...; text copy src/methodology.txt sha256 b1775b5a...; version v2.2 "May 2026-current"): "Results within datasets are aggregated as an audio-duration-weighted average WER so that numerous short clips do not bias results compared to longer files."
   - anchor `methodology, Version History` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text (curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 d0590cba...; text copy src/methodology.txt sha256 b1775b5a...; version v2.2 "May 2026-current"): "AA-WER v2.2 May 2026—current"
   - anchor `VoxPopuli-Cleaned-AA discussion #1 "custom text normalizer"` @ hf discussion page, fetched 2026-10-21, status open, no reply from the dataset owner at fetch time: "I was wondering if your custom text normalizer is publicly available."
5. Chunking has two protocols on one reference. AA batch scoring evaluates whole 14-22 minute parent audio by default and falls back to ~9-minute then ~30-second chunks only when a model or API cannot take it; the HF leaderboard always uses the 341 Silero-VAD chunks of at most 30 s. Both concatenate chunk hypotheses and score against the call-level reference. The chunked dataset ships no per-chunk reference text.
   - anchor `chunked README "Methodology"` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA-chunked @27f7d0056d593231974a752ce03c1e965da69b3f (lastModified 2026-06-29): "We used Silero VAD to identify speech start and end timestamps in each parent call. Adjacent timestamped segments were combined into chunks up to, but not exceeding, 30 seconds."
   - anchor `chunked README "Methodology"` @ hf:datasets/ArtificialAnalysis/Earnings22-Cleaned-AA-chunked @27f7d0056d593231974a752ce03c1e965da69b3f (lastModified 2026-06-29): "This chunked dataset does not include per-chunk reference transcripts"
   - anchor `methodology, Audio chunking (Earnings22)` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text (curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 d0590cba...; text copy src/methodology.txt sha256 b1775b5a...; version v2.2 "May 2026-current"): "WER: Chunk transcripts are concatenated back into their parent call, and WER is computed on the combined transcript against the parent-call ground truth."
6. Numbers that must not be mixed: Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v3 on Earnings22-Cleaned-AA is 4.9% in AA-WER v2 (AA normaliser, AA run) and 5.85% on the HF live leaderboard (HF scorer, chunked, H200 job); on the leaderboard's standard Earnings22 test it is 10.77%. AA reports its own cleaning delta for this model as 7.4 pp; the HF-store difference between the two tracks is 4.92 pp, but that difference crosses a change of CALLS as well as references (claim 1) and a change of scorer, so neither delta isolates the effect of cleaning.
   - *narrowed*: AA's "WER went down 5.6 pp after cleaning" is a within-subset comparison on AA's six calls (cleaned vs original references on the same audio); the HF-store gap 10.77 -> 5.85 is across different calls. Both are real; only AA's isolates the reference effect.

7. What a scorer must do with these references: 600 of 15,814 reference words (3.8%) are filler tokens (um/uh/hmm/ah/er/eh) and 398 (2.5%) contain digits, because only the Whisper-normaliser-handled layers were left alone. A scorer without a closed filler list counts every filler the reference carries and the hypothesis omits as a deletion; Annex S 1.3 has no filler step.

### Verdict

Do not describe Earnings22-Cleaned-AA as "Earnings22 test with corrected references": it is six other calls (0/6 overlap with the leaderboard standard test), so a standard-vs-cleaned Earnings22 WER difference mixes calls, scorer and references. Do not cite the Apache-2.0 tag as the licence of the audio or references; treat as CC-BY-SA-4.0 until AA states otherwise. Do not pool it with verbatim sets or use it to rank public models by under 1 pp (n = 6 clusters, one call is 21.6% of the words, train-overlap is unresolved). Do not copy the AA normaliser: it is unpublished, version-drifting and fitted to its own proper nouns.

## intake-2354#record — Adding Benchmaxxer Repellant to the Open ASR Leaderboard

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2346#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Design: the Open ASR Leaderboard added private English test sets supplied by two commercial data vendors, Appen and DataOcean (11 splits, about 29.7 h: Appen 7 splits 10.08 h scripted and conversational AU/CA/IN/US; DataOcean 4 splits 19.64 h scripted/conversational US/GB), "to prevent potential risks of benchmaxxing or test-set contamination". The blog is co-authored by Hugging Face staff and by Appen and DataOcean staff, so the vendors that sell this kind of data are among the authors. Published 2026-05-06.
   - anchor `blog TLDR` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "To prevent potential risks of benchmaxxing or test-set contamination, we will keep these datasets private"
   - anchor `blog, table "New high-quality, private datasets"` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "DataoceanAI Conversational US American 8.82"
   - anchor `blog byline` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "Sergio Bruccoleri SBruccoleriAppen"
1. Access protocol: a model submitter never touches the private audio. Maintainers verify the public-set results and then compute the private metrics themselves; per-split scores are intentionally NOT published (only macro-averages: Average WER over the two provider averages, Avg Scripted, Avg Conversational, Avg US, Avg non-US) "to avoid model developers from boosting their score with a specific data provider or accent". The default Average WER stays public-only; the private sets are an opt-in toggle with a "Rank delta" column.
   - anchor `blog, "How can I evaluate my model on this data?"` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "We will verify the results on the public sets and compute the metrics on the private ones."
   - anchor `blog, column definitions` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "We intentionally do not provide a score on each split, to avoid model developers from boosting their score with a specific data provider or accent."
   - anchor `blog TLDR` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "by default, the leaderboard’s Average WER remains computed on public datasets only."
   - anchor `blog, column definitions` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: ""Average WER" computes a macroaverage of the data provider averages, so that they are weighted equally."
2. Stated residual risk, in the authors' own words: the vendors sell datasets directly to ASR providers for training, so a model could have an advantage; the mitigation is contractual ("we've asked Appen and DataOcean to not provide this data to their clients"), diversification across two providers, and keeping private sets out of the default average. They concede that similar-distribution data "could still help". A community researcher (Olewave) raised the same objection on publication day. No measurement of whether private sets change rankings or detect fitting is given in the blog.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 listed this blog as "private-set contamination design". The blog's own text is narrower: it is a vendor-co-authored announcement with a contractual, not technical, hold-out and an acknowledged residual advantage; it contains no evidence on detection power.
   - anchor `blog, "Do models trained on the data providers have an advantage?"` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "They could. We’ve asked Appen and DataoceanAI to not provide this data to their clients."
   - anchor `blog, same section` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "data from a similar distribution could still help the model on the corresponding evaluation set"
   - anchor `blog, same section` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "having multiple data providers balances out the advantage a model may get from having used data from one of the providers"
   - anchor `blog, community comment by weiwchu, 2026-05-06` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "The two data vendors mentioned earlier are offering datasets directly to ASR service providers for training."
3. Implementation in the repo (HEAD 7e8d1a1): private families are hard-coded as separate CSV blocks (Appen, DataOcean, HF_English_Private_Set, Hindi); the PR template makes API providers attest they will not "train on, log, store, retain, or inspect" leaderboard audio "to ensure the held-out nature of the private sets"; the public results store (hf-audio/open-asr-leaderboard-results) carries public columns only. The held-out guarantee therefore rests on maintainer custody and vendor attestations, not on a technical control.
   - anchor `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md, API models checklist` @ huggingface/open_asr_leaderboard @ 7e8d1a1003c201324e9003dd5bc34244340a63b0: "This is important to ensure the held-out nature of the private sets."
   - anchor `eval_utils.py FAMILY_CONFIGS voicearena_private` @ huggingface/open_asr_leaderboard @ 7e8d1a1003c201324e9003dd5bc34244340a63b0: ""HF_English_Private_Set__test": ("HF_English_Private_Set", None),"
   - anchor `live results store header: public columns only` @ hf:datasets/hf-audio/open-asr-leaderboard-results @f3f994901b393d72e65efc239d1f0bfa395de6c1: "model,avg,RTFx,License,Size (B),# Languages,Encoder,Decoder,Training data disclosure,AMI-Cleaned WER"
4. The private-set design is paired with data-quality tooling: the authors say they built tooling to find low-SNR clips and transcript mismatches when preparing the sets (promised "in a future post", not shown). This is the same concern as the cleaned-reference tracks (Earnings22/VoxPopuli-Cleaned-AA), approached from the supplier side.
   - anchor `blog, "What's next?"` @ https://huggingface.co/blog/open-asr-leaderboard-private-data, published 2026-05-06, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 dccad781..., text copy src/blog.txt sha256 bf21fdc3...: "developing tooling to identify challenging cases such as low signal-to-noise conditions or transcript mismatches"
5. For Annex S: our corpus already has a held-out mechanism (P-STT-1 selection/confirmation strata keyed on the campaign seed; confirmation utterances must not appear in planner context), so a separate "private set" is not a new stratum but a stricter custody rule on the existing confirmation stratum. The open conflict is custody: the durability rule (MEASUREMENT.md sec 5: evidence behind a ratified claim "MUST therefore live inside a repository") and CS-4 ("in-repo and durable") point at repositories that are PUBLIC (epyc-root and epyc-inference-research, gh repo view 2026-10-21; epyc-web is private). A confirmation split whose audio or reference text is committed to a public repository is not held out from future model training or from anyone reading the repo, and the operator-recorded clips would also be publicly exposed voice data.
   - *unaffected*: Annex S facts (selection/confirmation strata, planner-context rule) and the repo-visibility facts were read from the working tree and gh at fetch time; they agree with each other and show the custody gap.
   - anchor `P-STT-1 sec 1.4, Selection / confirmation split` @ epyc-root measurement/protocols/speech.md (last commit 88148c42 2026-08-04; working tree 2026-10-21): "Confirmation utterances MUST NOT appear in planner context."
   - anchor `sec 5 Governance, evidence durability` @ epyc-root MEASUREMENT.md (working tree 2026-10-21, origin/main b397f203e): "MUST therefore live inside a repository"
   - anchor `gh repo view pestopoppa/epyc-root` @ gh repo view, run 2026-10-21: "pestopoppa/epyc-root {"isPrivate":false,"visibility":"PUBLIC"}"
   - anchor `gh repo view pestopoppa/epyc-inference-research` @ gh repo view, run 2026-10-21: "pestopoppa/epyc-inference-research {"isPrivate":false,"visibility":"PUBLIC"}"
6. Independent evidence that the problem is real, not a replication of this blog: the repo ships benchmark_fitting/ probes (see openasr-repo), and arXiv 2608.19936 (abstract read only) reports that the highest-scoring open-source ASR models reproduce benchmark reference spans even where the audio is contradictory, masked or ambiguous. Neither tests private-set effectiveness; the blog's central design claim (private sets are "less likely to be exploited") remains an unevidenced design intent.
   - anchor `arXiv 2608.19936 abstract (surfaced source; abstract only)` @ arXiv API record for 2608.19936v1 (title "Towards Quantifying Benchmark Optimization in ASR Models", published 2026-08-20), fetched 2026-10-21; abstract only, paper NOT read: "We find that the highest-scoring open source models output verbatim reference transcript spans even when the relevant audio is contradictory, masked, or ambiguous."

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 2: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). Slug references in this entry resolve as: aa-earnings22-cleaned=intake-2353#record, openasr-2510.06961=intake-2346#record, openasr-repo=intake-2352#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv:2608.19936 "Towards Quantifying Benchmark Optimization in ASR Models" (abstract read, title resolved via arXiv API; dedup 0) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2372#record (asr-benchmark-optimization) - HF blog "Voice Arena / Monsoon global south" (carried from wave-1) -> declined: Hindi, outside the it/de/fr/en gate (dive's own reason) - Promised future post on private-set audio and transcript quality tooling -> monitor (dive's own): promised future post not found at fetch

### Verdict

Do not cite this blog as evidence that private sets prevent benchmark fitting: it states design intent, discloses a vendor-supplied-data conflict, and measures nothing. Do not read "private" as a technical property: HF custody is maintainer-held plus vendor attestations. For Annex S the usable lesson is custody and disclosure (withhold per-utterance and per-split detail from the loop, keep confirmation material out of public repositories, report only aggregates), not the vendor-data model.
