## intake-2355#record — nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b: multilingual (40 locale) cache-aware streaming FastConformer-RNNT with language-ID prompting

- **URL**: https://huggingface.co/nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2349#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Coverage: 40 language-locales from one 0.6B model in three tiers. The it-IT, de-DE and fr-FR/fr-CA locales are 'transcription-ready' (19 locales) alongside en, es, pt, nl, tr, ru, ar, hi, ja, ko, vi, uk; 13 'broad-coverage' locales (pl, sv, cs, nb, da, bg, fi, hr, sk, zh, hu, ro, et) transcribe out of the box at 15-33% WER; 8 'adaptation-ready' locales (el, lt, lv, mt, sl, he, th, nn) are tokenizer-recognised only and need fine-tuning. So it passes the it/de/fr/en language gate in one model.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 dive and Stage-1 text said "40+ locales". Only 32 locales transcribe out of the box; 8 are tokenizer-only (fine-tune required). it/de/fr/en are all in the 19 transcription-ready tier.
   - anchor `README.md: Supported Languages` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "The model supports **40 language-locales** in total, across three tiers:"
   - anchor `README.md: Supported Languages table` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "French (fr-FR, fr-CA), Italian (it-IT), Portuguese (pt-BR, pt-PT), Dutch (nl-NL), German (de-DE)"
   - anchor `README.md: Supported Languages note` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "adaptation-ready locales require fine-tuning on in-domain data to enable full transcription"
1. Mechanism: cache-aware FastConformer-RNNT (24 encoder layers) with a language-ID prompt: a 128-dim one-hot language vector is broadcast over time, concatenated to the 1024-dim encoder output and projected into the RNN-T decoder. Chunk size is chosen at inference from 80/160/320/560/1120 ms (att_context [56,{0,1,3,6,13}]) without retraining. target_lang=auto detects the language and appends a <xx-XX> tag after terminal punctuation. Built-in punctuation and capitalisation.
   - *unaffected*: Architecture text is internally consistent with the runner docs (cache-aware, prompt conditioned, [56,x] contexts).
   - anchor `README.md: intro` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "offers runtime flexibility with configurable chunk sizes, including 80ms, 160ms, 320ms, 560ms, and 1120ms"
   - anchor `README.md: Model Architecture` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "along the feature axis → fused tensor (D + K, T)"
   - anchor `README.md: auto-detect tip` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "the model detects the spoken language and emits the corresponding **language code/tag**"
2. AUTHOR-REPORTED FLEURS-test WER (LangID given, card normalisation): fr 10.97 / 10.60 / 9.79 / 9.45 / 9.03 at 80 / 160 / 320 / 560 / 1120 ms; de 9.81 -> 8.31; it 5.23 -> 4.25; en 9.43 -> 7.91. WER falls monotonically with chunk size (fr -17.7% from 80 ms to 1.12 s). The card states its normaliser is imperfect across the 40 locales and may inflate error.
   - *narrowed*: Card figures use NVIDIA numeral/format normalisation; an independent pipeline gives fr/de/it 1.5-2.0 pp higher. Use the transcribe.cpp column for any like-for-like comparison.
   - anchor `README.md: model-index French` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "name: FLEURS (French) type: google/fleurs config: fr_fr split: test metrics: - name: WER (1.12s frame size, LangID) type: wer value: 9.03"
   - anchor `README.md: model-index German` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "name: FLEURS (German) type: google/fleurs config: de_de split: test metrics: - name: WER (1.12s frame size, LangID) type: wer value: 8.31"
   - anchor `README.md: model-index Italian` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "name: FLEURS (Italian) type: google/fleurs config: it_it split: test metrics: - name: WER (1.12s frame size, LangID) type: wer value: 4.25"
   - anchor `README.md: Performance table French, 80 ms LangID cell` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "French (fr-FR, fr-CA)</td><td align="center" style="background-color:#eef6e0;">10.97"
   - anchor `README.md: normalisation note` @ nvidia/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b@ea30d66debe3740a08b573_244_286_791d423d6b3e: "residual mismatches between normalized text can inflate the reported error rates"
3. LIKE-FOR-LIKE vs Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v3 (the incumbent multilingual arm): Nemotron 3.5 is 1.4-2.0x WORSE on it/de/fr/en offline. Same runner, same whisper-normalizer pipeline, Q8_0, FLEURS test (transcribe.cpp docs): fr 10.78 vs 5.30, de 10.33 vs 5.24, it 5.78 vs 3.02, en 7.99 vs 4.83 (Nemotron at its best [56,13] 1.12 s setting). NVIDIA's own cards agree in direction: Nemotron 3.5 fr 9.03 / de 8.31 / it 4.25 / en 7.91 vs v3 fr 5.15 / de 5.04 / it 3.00 / en 4.85 (the two cards use different normalisers, so the card-to-card gap understates the like-for-like gap). Its advantage over v3 is architectural, not accuracy: native cache-aware streaming down to 80 ms, a language tag, and built-in PnC; v3 is offline-only in the ggml runners.
   - *reattributed*: Stage-1 framing was "better multilingual streaming STT than Parakeet v3". On accuracy it is NOT (1.4-2.0x higher WER on it/de/fr/en under one pipeline). The gain is streaming architecture and language-ID, not WER.

4. Auto language detection is nearly free on FLEURS (card, LangID vs auto-detect at the same chunk): at 80 ms fr +0.06, it +0.05, de +0.09, en +0.29 pp; at 1.12 s fr -0.01, de -0.09, it +0.07, en +0.93 (en 7.91 -> 8.84; the largest auto-detect penalty). FLEURS utterances are single-language read speech, so this does not test code-switching or short hesitation clips.

5. VENDOR-REPORTED serving claim: on one H100 the cache-aware design sustains ~17x more concurrent streams than Parakeet RNNT 1.1B multilingual buffered streaming at 80 ms (240 vs 14) and 6x at 1120 ms (2,400 vs 400). H100 only; no CPU, no MI210, no EPYC datum, and the comparator is the 1.1B buffered model, not v3.

6. Licence: OpenMDW-1.1 (openmdw.ai), 'ready for commercial use'. This differs from its English sibling (NVIDIA Open Model License) and from Parakeet v3 (CC-BY-4.0); HF metadata says license: other. Weights were trained partly on pseudo-labels from an ensemble of Canary, Parakeet 1.1B, Whisper large-v3 and FunASR, plus proprietary Riva data.

7. Runners: NVIDIA ships an official Q8_0 GGUF inside the model repo and documents NeMo-Speech.cpp (ggml) as the local runtime; handy-computer/transcribe.cpp (ggml 0.26) ports it as F32..Q4_K_M; mudler/parakeet.cpp lists it but issue #33 reports a Q4_K load failure. CPU RTF (author-reported, transcribe.cpp, Q8_0, jfk 11 s): 30.8x on Apple M4 Max, 11.6x on Ryzen 7 PRO 4750U; no EPYC/MI210 number. NeMo-Speech.cpp's own docs say word boosting works on cache-aware RNNT (Nemotron) but is IGNORED for Parakeet TDT. A HIP/gfx90a build is a costed item, not a gate.

8. OPEN RUNNER DEFECTS that bear on a conversational use (2026-10-21): NeMo-Speech.cpp #48 (streaming RNNT with Nemotron-3.5 wedges after sustained zero-PCM silence on Vulkan and CPU; Vulkan can abort GGML_ASSERT(ne3 == ne13)), #49 (realtime WebSocket commit never completes for utterances >= ~1 s, endpointing stalls on trailing silence; reported with the English sibling on CUDA), transcribe.cpp #215 (committed text freezes for up to 34 s in 18 of 20 one-minute streaming passages with Nemotron 3.5; transcript correct, commit timing wrong). These are runner bugs, not model defects, but they hit exactly the silence/endpoint behaviour CS-22 depends on.

   - anchor `iss_handy-computer_transcribe.cpp_215.json: issue body` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp issue #215 (open, 2026-10-16): "on a typical passage the worst lag was 34 seconds"
9. Third-party corroboration is partial: transcribe.cpp (a different codebase) re-measures en FLEURS at 7.99 vs the card's 7.91 and LibriSpeech test-clean at 3.03 (NeMo ref) vs its own F32 3.04, so the English card numbers reproduce on an independent runner. For fr/de/it the independent figures (10.78 / 10.33 / 5.78) sit 1.5-2.0 pp above the card (9.03 / 8.31 / 4.25), which is normaliser difference plus Q8_0; no independent lab has published a multilingual comparison of Nemotron 3.5 against Parakeet v3 (web search 2026-10-21 found only aggregator pages and a blog without WER).

10. NVIDIA recommends the English sibling nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b for English-only use and Nemotron 3.5 for the other transcription-ready locales; its fine-tuning blog (NVIDIA-authored) advises replaying a slice of the other languages to avoid eroding them, and says adaptation-ready languages need fine-tuning. The blog also cites Artificial Analysis ranking Nemotron 3 ASR 2nd in latency among streaming ASR (not verified here).

Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv 2604.19079 (unified RNN-T / MCR-RNNT) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2357#record (unified-rnnt-2604.19079) - nvidia/nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2356#record (nemotron-streaming-en) - huggingface.co/blog/nvidia/fine-tuning-nemotron-35-asr + its companion GitHub repo -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2368#record (nemotron35-blogs) (blog dived; the companion repo is covered by that entry as a tutorial notebook) - Artificial Analysis streaming-ASR latency ranking (cited by the blog: Nemotron 3 ASR 2nd in latency) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2373#record (aa-streaming-asr-latency) - github.com/NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp issues #46/#48/#49/#71 (first-chunk padding, silence wedge, realtime commit, Arabic Q4/FP16 word drops) -> monitor (dive's own): NeMo-Speech.cpp issues #46/#48/#49/#71 via CS-22 acceptance N2 - arXiv 2608.21343 TurboBias 2.0 (streaming context biasing for transducers), surfaced by sibling olicorne-ultimed -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2366#record (turbobias2) - cjpais/Handy#2138 (app integration of transcribe.cpp allowed_languages) -> declined: application-level PR, no mechanism beyond transcribe.cpp #168 (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

Nemotron 3.5 ASR Streaming is a 40-locale cache-aware streaming transducer (NVIDIA, 2026-06-04, OpenMDW-1.1) that covers it/de/fr/en. It is a streaming/latency arm, not an accuracy upgrade: same-pipeline FLEURS WER is 1.4-2.0x worse than Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v3. Do not cite the card as showing it beats v3, and do not enable it for endpointing before the open runner silence/commit defects are cleared on our build. All numbers are literature-class until measured under Annex S.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Add Nemotron 3.5 Q8_0 as the STREAMING arm of the CS-8.P STT bake-off, scored under Annex S on it/de/fr/en at att_context right in {0,3,6,13}, forced-language and auto-detect, against Parakeet-v3 and the whisper cascade on the same clips.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Treat the runner defects (#48, #49, #215) as acceptance tests for CS-22: a zero-PCM silence soak and a commit-lag check before any endpoint decision uses this stack.

Four operating points are valid; trained att_context is [70,{0,1,6,13}].

Source inconsistencies, resolved: (a) the card says ~250,000 hours in the intro and 530k hours in the datasets section (the 530k figure includes the Riva 250k plus Granary/YTC/YODAS2/LibriLight/public corpora; the intro quotes only the Riva ASRSet); (b) three NVIDIA documents report different numbers for this one model: at 0.16 s the card gives 7.67, the unified card 7.84, the unified paper 7.92; at 0.08 s the card gives 8.43, the unified card 8.70 (the unified paper has no 0.08 s column); at 1.12 s 6.93 / 6.92 / 7.08. Spread 0.1-0.25 pp is evaluator/hardware difference, not a defect; (c) the 0.40 s cell (9.52 in the unified card, 10.91 in the paper) is a latency outside the trained look-ahead list, so it is an artefact, not a curve point.

   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 3.3/4.1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1: "The model also struggles with latency values (italic font) that were not included in the look-ahead list during training."
3. Relation to parakeet-unified-en-0.6b (the wave-1 'unified' model): NOT the same model and not its descendant. Nemotron-en is a streaming-only cache-aware model (state cached across chunks, no recompute); unified is one set of weights for offline and buffered streaming (left context recomputed per chunk; the paper defers cache-passing to future work). Unified is better from 2.08 s down to ~0.24 s (e.g. 6.14 vs 7.46 at 2.08 s) and in offline (5.91 vs 7.05 in the paper's evaluation), because the streaming-only model gains little from full context; Nemotron-en wins at <=0.16 s (7.84 vs 8.44 at 0.16 s, 8.70 vs 15.63 at 0.08 s) and is cheaper to run at scale. NVIDIA itself recommends nemotron-en for 80 ms.
   - *reattributed*: Wave-1 dive framed unified as the thing to compare with NVIDIA's streaming recommendation; the two are separate lineages (streaming-only cache-aware vs unified offline+buffered streaming). Nemotron-en is the sub-160 ms winner, unified is the winner from ~240 ms upward and offline.

   - anchor `unified_card.md: Model Architecture` @ nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b@fe53cd885760c96b6a5f51a0bfd362cb4584a98b: "The current inference pipeline supports only buffered streaming (left context is recomputed for each chunk)"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 4.2` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1: "The proposed model (2) is only slightly inferior to Nemotron-Streaming-0.6b at 0.16s latency point."
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1: "it did not gain much improvement from having the entire audio during decoding (offline mode)"
   - anchor `README.md: Model Architecture` @ nvidia/nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b@ebe59e5a817_142_986_528bbbee5dba8db7b38ed50: "the cache-aware architecture enables continuous transcription by processing only new audio chunks while reusing cached encoder context"
4. Third-party corroboration: transcribe.cpp (independent ggml codebase) measures LibriSpeech test-clean 2.31 (Q8_0) in cache-aware streaming at R=13, equal to its offline value and to NVIDIA's 2.32. The wave-1 NeMo-Speech.cpp benchmark (NVIDIA's own runner) is a second ggml datum (2.51 at 1.12 s, 2.66 at 0.16 s, 4090) restated from the wave-1 dive and not re-read here. CPU RTF (author-reported, transcribe.cpp, Q8_0, jfk 11 s): 14.8x on Ryzen 7 PRO 4750U; no EPYC/MI210 figure.
   - anchor `tc_nemotron_en.md: Streaming WER` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "| Q8_0 | 2.31% |"

Runners and gaps: official Q8_0 GGUF ships in NVIDIA's own repo; NeMo-Speech.cpp supports it with endpointing and word boosting; open NeMo-Speech.cpp #49 (realtime WebSocket commit never completes for utterances >= ~1 s) was reported against this very file. NVIDIA recommends THIS model for English-only use and Nemotron 3.5 for the other locales, which implies a (publicly unquantified) English cost of the multilingual extension.

### Verdict

nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b is NVIDIA's English cache-aware streaming transducer (March 2026) and the parent of Nemotron 3.5. It is a different lineage from parakeet-unified-en: it wins at <=160 ms lookahead, unified wins from ~240 ms and offline. English only (fails the language gate). Its value for us is as the English low-latency reference arm and as the control that prices the multilingual-extension tax; all numbers are literature-class until measured under Annex S.

- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Restrict streaming configs to trained operating points (right context 0,1,6,13).

## intake-2357#record — Reducing the Offline-Streaming Gap for Unified ASR Transducer with Consistency Regularization

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.19079
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance low; categories: multimodal, training_distillation)
- **Disposition**: monitor
- **Expanded from**: intake-2348#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism. One RNN-T (FastConformer) encoder serves both modes by (i) chunk-limited self-attention with a left context L, current chunk C and right context R, sampling [L,C,R] from L=70 frames, C in {1,2,7,13}, R in {0,1,2,3,5,7,13,26} (80 ms/frame) at every step; (ii) dynamic chunk convolution (DCConv) so depthwise convolutions see only chunk-legal context and share weights with offline mode; (iii) dual-mode training that runs the offline and streaming passes on the same batch with loss alpha*L_off + (1-alpha)*L_str + lambda*L_MCR, alpha=0.5; and (iv) MCR-RNNT, a symmetric KL divergence between the full RNN-T joint logits [T,U+1,V] of the two modes, computed on the fly in a fused Triton kernel (log-softmax recomputed in backward, near-zero memory overhead), lambda=0.3 best. Variants that failed: extending CR-CTC to hybrid CTC-RNNT consistently degraded streaming RNNT accuracy; KL over blank/target/rest or over lattice posteriors was worse than KL over the full joint.
   - *unaffected*: Wave-1 characterisation of unified (chunk-limited attention + DCConv + MCR-RNNT, not a merged checkpoint) is confirmed by the full text.
   - anchor `paper.txt: Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "supports both offline and streaming decoding within a single model, using chunk-limited attention with right context and dynamic chunked convolutions"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 3.3` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Context size for chunk-limited attention mask [L, C, R] was sampled from a predefined list [[70],[1,2,7,13],[0,1,2,3,5,7,13,26]]"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 2.3 eq 2` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Each step runs both modes on the same input batch and combines their RNNT losses"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 2.4` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "It computes KL divergence (KLD) directly from raw logits inside a fused GPU kernel integrated with PyTorch"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 2.4` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "imposes nearly zero memory overhead and tiny computational overhead compared to RNNT loss"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 4.3` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "The results showed that a symmetric KLD loss with λ=0.3 yields the best trade-off"
1. WER-versus-lookahead (AUTHOR-REPORTED, Open ASR Leaderboard English, average WER, greedy). L-size 128M on 120k h: MCR drives the low-latency tail of dual-mode training from 12.48 / 16.91 / 22.45 (0.32 / 0.24 / 0.16 s) down to 8.24 / 9.04 / 10.51; offline 6.63 vs 6.69; streaming-only baseline 9.44 / 10.01 / 9.84 beats it only at 0.16 s. XL 0.6B on 280k h PC data, balanced model: offline 5.91, 2.08 s 6.14, 1.12 s 6.29, 0.56 s 6.52, 0.40 s 6.70, 0.32 s 6.92, 0.24 s 7.35, 0.16 s 8.44 (the HF card adds 15.63 at 0.08 s, which the paper does not report); the larger-right-context model reaches 5.76 offline but 12.73 at 0.16 s. Ablation: symmetric KL beats teacher-only; lambda 0.3, alpha 0.5.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 H3 listed a 0.08 s point (15.63) as part of the published curve; it appears only in the HF card, not in the paper, whose Table 1 stops at 0.16 s.
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table 1 L-size DM` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Unified dual-mode (DM) | 6.69 | 7.14 | 7.48 | 8.12 | 9.86 | 12.48 | 16.91 | 22.45"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table 1 L-size DM+MCR` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Unified DM + MCR-RNNT (Ours) | 6.63 | 6.86 | 7.09 | 7.47 | 7.83 | 8.24 | 9.04 | 10.51"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table 1 L-size streaming baseline` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Baseline (Streaming) | 7.75 | 8.39 | 8.02 | 8.36 | 11.47 | 9.44 | 10.01 | 9.84"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table 1 XL balanced` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "(2) Unified DM + MCR-RNNT 0.6B (Ours, balanced) | 5.91 | 6.14 | 6.29 | 6.52 | 6.70 | 6.92 | 7.35 | 8.44"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table 1 XL larger right context` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "(1) Unified DM + MCR-RNNT 0.6B (Ours, larger right cont.) | 5.76 | 5.97 | 6.14 | 6.44 | 6.96 | 7.72 | 9.51 | 12.73"
   - anchor `unified_card.md: card table (adds 0.08 s column)` @ nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b@fe53cd885760c96b6a5f51a0bfd362cb4584a98b: "| nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b | **5.91** | **6.14** | **6.29** | **6.52** | **6.70** | **6.92** | **7.35** | 8.44 | 15.63 |"
2. Scope limits: every experiment is English only (Granary English subset: 120k h normalised for L-size, 280k h with PC for XL), evaluated on the English Open ASR Leaderboard sets with greedy decoding at batch 128. There is no multilingual experiment, no code-switching, no noisy/telephony evaluation, and no comparison on spontaneous conversational audio beyond AMI/Earnings22 inside the leaderboard average.
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 3.1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "To evaluate the ASR results, we used the Open ASR Leaderboard for English [14]."
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 3.1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "we used a subset of ∼120,000 hours of labeled English speech (with normalized transcripts) from the public Granary dataset"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 3.4` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "on 280,000 hours of English data from the Granary dataset, including punctuation and capitalization (PC)"
3. Costs and caveats: dual-mode training doubles the per-step compute versus single-mode (the authors halve the batch size to compensate); streaming inference here is buffered, recomputing the left context every chunk, which slows decoding; cache-passing streaming is future work. Below ~0.16-0.24 s the dedicated cache-aware streaming baseline remains the better choice (L-size: 9.84 vs 10.51 at 0.16 s; XL: Nemotron-streaming 7.92 vs 8.44).
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 4.3` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Currently, we recalculate the left context at each chunk step C, which slows inference speed."
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 2.3` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "This approach doubles the computational resources per training step compared to SM"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "making the streaming baseline more appropriate for low-latency streaming"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 4.1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "The model is only slightly inferior to the streaming baseline at 0.16s latency."
4. Reproducibility: the authors wrote the MCR-RNNT loss themselves because no public implementation of transducer consistency regularisation existed; the abstract says the framework and English checkpoint are open-sourced while footnote 1 of the same version says the framework 'will be released soon', so the training code was not confirmed public in v1. The checkpoint (nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b) is public and was reproduced at the GGUF level by a third party (wave-1: LibriSpeech test-clean 1.59 vs NVIDIA 1.63). The paper's own numbers for other models differ slightly from their HF cards, which the model card attributes to different evaluation machines. No independent replication of the training method was found (web and GitHub search 2026-10-21).
   - *narrowed*: Abstract says the framework is open-sourced; footnote 1 of v1 says it will be released soon. Code availability unverified.
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 2.4` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "we developed our own mode-consistency regularization loss for RNNT (MCR-RNNT)"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 1 footnote 1` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "Will be released soon"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Abstract` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "The proposed Unified ASR framework and the English model checkpoint are open-sourced."
   - anchor `unified_card.md: WER discussion` @ nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b@fe53cd885760c96b6a5f51a0bfd362cb4584a98b: "The obtained results for other models can be slightly different from the official HF model cards because of the different evaluation machines."
5. Generalisation to multilingual is UNTESTED. Nothing in the loss is language-specific (it acts on joint logits), and the Granary dataset used here is itself a 25-European-language corpus, so a multilingual unified model is feasible on paper; but NVIDIA's multilingual streaming release (Nemotron 3.5) uses the cache-aware route with language prompting, not this method, and no multilingual unified checkpoint exists. A negative signal within the paper: the CTC variant of consistency regularisation hurt streaming RNNT, so transfer across objectives is not automatic.
   - anchor `paper.txt: Sec 2.4` @ arXiv:2604.19079v1 (HTML, submitted 21 Apr 2026): "this consistently degraded streaming RNNT accuracy, even though offline performance remained strong"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 stage2b wave 2: first full read. Narrowed the wave-1 curve (0.08 s point is card-only) and the code-availability claim. Nothing overturned. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv 2608.21343 TurboBias 2.0 (title verified on abs page: "TurboBias 2.0: Streaming Context-Biasing for Production-Efficient ASR Systems", 2026-08-2 -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2366#record (turbobias2) - arXiv 2312.17279 Stateful Conformer / cache-aware streaming -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2369#record (cacheaware-2312.17279) - arXiv 2604.19079 cited prior work: Dynamic Chunk Convolution (Li et al., ICASSP 2023), Zipformer unification (Sharma et al., ACL 2025), TCR (Tseng et -> declined: background to a method we will not train (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

MCR-RNNT (NVIDIA, Apr 2026) trains one RNN-T for offline plus buffered streaming by adding a symmetric KL between the two modes' joint logits; it halves the low-latency WER penalty of naive dual-mode training (e.g. 12.48 -> 8.24 at 0.32 s) at 2x training compute per step, English only, no multilingual or replication evidence, and the dedicated cache-aware model still wins at <=0.16 s. Use the curve as a prior, not as a claim about it/de/fr.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Knowledge only for the STT bake-off: the WER-vs-lookahead curve is already carried by the wave-1 handy entry (H3); use it as the prior for CS-22 chunk choice.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Do not plan ASR training around MCR-RNNT; revisit only if a bake-off winner needs a custom streaming/offline model.

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identity and provenance: Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx (HF sha 82259d04, created 2026-09-04, renamed 2026-10-19) is a French-medical fine-tune of moondream/parakeet-ultra (itself a post-train of nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3), exported to ONNX (fp32/fp16/int8/w4a8 encoders) plus the .nemo checkpoint; CC-BY-4.0, vocabulary byte-identical to the base. It ships with three artefacts: the UltiMed-ASR-FR-v1 dataset, the model, and regex fix rules.

1. Training recipe: only the last 4 encoder layers plus the full decoder and joint are trained, the lower encoder frozen 'to preserve multilinguality'; optimiser AdamSPD (selective projection decay: weights are decayed toward their pretrained values only when the gradient step would move them further from it), LR 2e-5 cosine to 2e-6, 14,255 steps (~1.3 epochs) with the last two checkpoints averaged; one 24 GB GPU. Data: 2,398 h of medical text rendered by ONE synthetic TTS voice (Voxtral fr_female; no real clinician speech at all) plus 352 h (~11% of audio-seconds) of Common Voice rehearsal in fr/en/de/it/sv/fi. Without rehearsal, unprotected Swedish and Finnish drifted by 2.9 and 4.0 WER points.
   - anchor `README_model.md: Training table` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a: "| Trainable parameters | Last 4 encoder layers, plus the full decoder and joint network."

2. AUTHOR-REPORTED in-domain gain: on 11,747 French-medical test clips (dictionary, PARHAF, drugs, acronyms, drug sentences, held-out PARROT radiology) greedy macro WER falls from 9.0 (base ultra) to 3.4 (fine-tune); PARROT, never in any training manifest, 8.5 -> 4.2. Correction: roughly 30% of the base model's error is label-convention formatting (it writes 'mg' and '2' where labels say 'milligrammes' and 'deux'); folding units and numbers on both sides moves base 8.93 -> 6.17 but the fine-tune only 3.40 -> 3.22, so the genuine recognition gain is about 48% relative (6.17 -> 3.22), not 62%. The medical audio is synthetic and the author states the numbers are optimistic against real recordings.

3. General-speech REGRESSION despite rehearsal, frozen lower layers and SPD: on FLEURS validation (11 languages, 3,773 clips, int8, greedy) macro WER rises from 5.1 to 6.1 (+1.0; beam 5 +0.9). French, the fine-tuning language itself, gets worse (4.7 -> 5.2, +0.5), Italian +0.8, English +0.8, German +0.3, Slovak +3.1, Ukrainian +1.6. This is the failure mode 'must not regress inside the native window': the fine-tune buys medical terms at a measurable cost on ordinary it/de/fr/en speech.

4. Phrase boosting mechanism (parakeet_web phraseBoost.js, a JS port of the concept of NeMo's GPU phrase-boosting, PR #14277): a token-level trie of BPE-encoded phrases; at each decode step a token that continues or starts a phrase gets an additive logit-space reward nodeBonus = weight*(1 + depthScaling*(d-1)) (depth scaling 0.5, commitment-scaled by depth/length), gated by a min-p rule (reward only if the token's probability is >= 0.1x the top token's), with automatic surface-form augmentation (Title Case, ALL CAPS, French proclitics l'/d', symbol stripping, plurals). Works in greedy (best-effort, cannot rescue a pruned phrase) and in MAES beam (hypothesis-local active nodes). Measured effect (author, own list): medical macro WER base 9.0 -> 8.6 and fine-tune 3.4 -> 3.3, with a small FLEURS cost on the fine-tune (macro 6.1 -> 6.2). Boosting alone is a small lever; the min-p gate is an insertion guard that recovered up to ~1.8 CER at high strength in the author's 100-cell sweep.
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: file header` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "a token-level boosting trie that injects an"
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: file header` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "nodeBonus = phraseWeight * (1 + DEPTH_SCALING * (d - 1))"
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: min-p gate` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "iff `logit >= maxLogit + log(minp)`"
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: file header` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "a phrase already pruned; MAES weakens that limit by keeping rival hypotheses"
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: augmentations` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "export const FULL_AUGMENT = 'faphn';"
   - anchor `README_model.md: Phrase boosting macro (boost)` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a: "| **macro average** | 3.3 / 0.8 | 3.3 / 0.8 | 3.3 / 0.7 | 3.4 / 0.8 | 8.6 / 4.7 | 8.6 / 4.7 |"
   - anchor `README_model.md: FLEURS boost macro` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a: "| **macro average** | 5.1 / 1.8 | 5.1 / 1.8 | 6.2 / 2.3 | 6.2 / 2.3 | 6.6 / 2.5 | 6.2 / 2.3 | **+1.0 / +0.5** |"
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: constants` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "export const DEFAULT_BOOST_MIN_P = 0.1;"
   - anchor `src_parakeet_web/app/src/phraseBoost.js: constants` @ thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web@3521817e2257dc92b3e9e1e4952bf1261ed6a3f7 app/src/phraseBoost.js: "export const DEFAULT_DEPTH_SCALING = 0.5;"
5. Regex/term-fix rules: error mining then guarded substitution. Transcribe the whole dataset with each model, collect misspelling variants per target term (e.g. 17 variants for venlafaxine), compile accent/case-insensitive whole-word patterns, drop any variant that occurs in the 602,813 correct labels, 1M French Wikipedia sentences or is a common word, merge variants of one replacement into one alternation (3,963 drug + ~35.9k term rules + 1.9k manual). Generalisation is modest and the headline columns are not generalisation: the rules were learned on ALL splits including test, as the README itself says. Honest generalisation evidence: (i) held-out train+val build on test drugs, fine-tune 3.69 -> 2.63, base 10.79 -> 9.12; (ii) the author's own 206 unseen dictated clips, base 25.28 -> 23.34, fine-tune 10.07 -> 9.06. Costs: 96 of 100,000 French news sentences rewritten (about 1 in 1,000), English text corrupted (tried -> triiodé), so French-only. Rule-count discrepancy inside the author's docs: term rules are 35,876 (regex README) vs 35,996 (model README and parakeet_web).
   - *narrowed*: "boost + regex" columns are coverage of seen mistakes (rules learned on all splits including test); only the held-out build and the 206-clip set bound generalisation (~1-2 pp).

6. Real-voice check is small and single-speaker: the author's phone recordings of 10 dense medical texts (15 recordings, 5 texts read twice, misreadings counted as model errors); normalised CER at beam 5 is 4.9% (base), 2.3% (fine-tune), 1.9% (fine-tune plus boost plus rules). Texts and rules are disjoint by construction, which makes it the best generalisation evidence in the package, but n is 15 clips from one voice and the transcripts, not the audio, are published.
   - anchor `README_model.md: intro` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a: "normalised CER, beam 5: 4.9 % for the base model, 2.3 % for this finetune, 1.9 % with phrase boosting and the fix rules"

   - anchor `REAL_VOICE.md: intro` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a REAL_VOICE_STRESS_TEST.md: "Five texts were read twice"
7. Credibility: single hobbyist author, self-funded, one consumer GPU; the README states the export, quantisation and measurements were done with Claude Code and the benchmark tables are generated by a queue/publisher script (not independently audited); 0 likes, 313 downloads on the model; no independent replication found. Positives: the recipe, NeMo fork, scripts and annotated training config are public and the author reports his own regressions (FLEURS delta table, real-voice caveats, 'not a medical device'). Treat all numbers as AUTHOR-REPORTED literature-class with a synthetic-data optimism flag.
   - anchor `README_model.md: banner` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a: "This project is entirely self-funded on my minimum-wage salary"

   - anchor `api_model.json: HF API record` @ Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-ultra-UltiMed-onnx@82259d0411a57c0daa6241a39240ef8d339ac57a: ""likes":0"
8. Transferable mechanisms (judged on portability, not on the French-medical model): (a) a domain/proper-noun lexicon injected at decode time. NeMo-Speech.cpp already implements boosting inside the cache-aware RNNT decoder (Nemotron streaming; typical request scores 2-3, no extra artefacts) but its docs state Parakeet TDT ignores boosting, so the Parakeet-v3/ultra arm has no lexicon path except Olicorne's JS decoder; the boost is therefore a differentiator for the Nemotron streaming arm. (b) Error-mined, corpus-guarded post-decode substitution rules: runner-independent, CPU-only, zero latency. (c) Replay rehearsal plus SPD to limit forgetting. (d) A paired FLEURS non-regression table as the acceptance gate for any adapted STT.
   - anchor `nss_customization.md: Feature matrix` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 docs/asr/customization.md: "Word boosting works on CTC with the Flashlight LM decoder and on cache-aware RNNT"
   - anchor `nss_configuration.md: Word boosting` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 docs/asr/configuration.md: "greedy CTC without an LM and Parakeet TDT ignore it."
   - anchor `nss_customization.md: Word boosting` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373 docs/asr/customization.md: "Cache-aware RNNT requires no extra artifacts and typically uses 2-3."
9. Licensing of the artefacts: model and rules CC-BY-4.0; the dataset is CC-BY-4.0 except the PARROT radiology subset (CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0, eval-only, excluded from training so the weights stay CC-BY); medical audio is synthetic (Voxtral TTS), text from French open data (OPEN_MEDIC, BDPM) and PARHAF (CC-BY).
   - anchor `README_dataset.md: Licence section` @ Olicorne/UltiMed-ASR-FR-v1@b1bba3d285122ccbe7477d94d1dbf363f1ba1f05 README: "cc-by-nc-sa-4.0 covers the separate PARROT radiology subset."

Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv 2608.21343 TurboBias 2.0: Streaming Context-Biasing for Production-Efficient ASR Systems (abs page title verified; 2026-08-21) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2366#record (turbobias2) - NVIDIA-NeMo/NeMo PR #14277 "GPU-accelerated Phrase-Boosting (GPU-PB) for CTC, RNN-T, and TDT decoding" (title verified via GitHub API; merged 2025-08- -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2367#record (nemo-pr14277-boosting) - Olicorne/UltiMed-ASR-FR-v1 dataset and thiswillbeyourgithub/UltiMed-ASR-FR-v1-scripts recipe (synthetic-TTS domain corpus pipeline) -> declined: covered here; dataset is French medical (dive's own reason) - Olicorne/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-redux-UltiMed-experimental-onnx (2-bit redux + UltiMed decoder graft, 159 MB CPU/WASM) -> declined: experimental, French medical, 2-bit encoder (dive's own reason) - Selective Projection Decay (GT-RIPL/Selective-Projection-Decay; "Selective Projection Decay for Reducing Negative Transfer in Multi-Task Learning" as -> declined: ingest was conditional on CS-42 opening; not selected at any close-out

### Verdict

UltiMed is a single-author French-medical fine-tune of parakeet-ultra with in-domain macro WER 9.0 -> 3.4 on partly synthetic data, at a measured +1.0 pp macro cost on general FLEURS speech (French +0.5, Italian +0.8, English +0.8). Do not adopt the model for the it/de/fr/en gate. The transferable parts are (a) decode-time lexicon boosting (a Nemotron-only capability in the ggml runners), (b) error-mined guarded post-decode rules, (c) the paired non-regression gate and SPD+replay as forgetting control. Treat all numbers as author-reported, synthetic-optimistic.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Adopt the patterns, not the model: (1) a CS domain/proper-noun lexicon with in-decoder boosting on the Nemotron streaming arm and a recall measurand; (2) error-mined, corpus-guarded post-decode rules gated by the CS-4 exact-value set; (3) a paired FLEURS it/de/fr/en non-regression gate on any adapted STT; (4) AdamSPD + replay as the forgetting control for CS-42.

## intake-2359#record — transcribe.cpp: multi-family C/C++ speech-to-text library on ggml (Whisper, Parakeet, Nemotron, Canary, Qwen3-ASR, Voxtral, Silero VAD)

- **URL**: https://github.com/handy-computer/transcribe.cpp
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2348#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Scope: transcribe.cpp is a C/C++ STT library on ggml with a CLI, C API and Python/Rust/TS/Swift bindings. README says '16 model families and 60+ variants', but its own family table lists 20 ASR rows (Canary, Canary-Qwen, Cohere, Fun-ASR, GigaAM, Granite 4/4.1 and 5.0, MedASR, Moonshine and Moonshine Streaming, MOSS, Multitalker Parakeet, Nemotron 3.5 and Nemotron EN streaming, Parakeet, Qwen3-ASR, SenseVoice, Voxtral and Voxtral Realtime, Whisper) plus one diarizer, one language-ID and one VAD (Silero v6.2). One binary therefore covers every arm of our STT bake-off, including parakeet-ultra (drop-in for tdt-0.6b-v3, same 25 languages, CC-BY-4.0), parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3, parakeet-unified-en and nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b (32 language-locales), which no sibling runner (parakeet.cpp, NeMo-Speech.cpp) covers together.
   - *narrowed*: README '16 families' is stale against its own table (20 ASR rows plus diarize, langid, VAD); also corrects wave-1 handy-unified-en-gguf's '16 families'. Breadth is larger than reported, which only helps the harness case.
   - anchor `tc/README.md: README intro` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "16 model families and 60+ variants, streaming and batch."
   - anchor `tc/README.md: README family table (Whisper row)` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "| Whisper | `whisper-base`"
   - anchor `tc/README.md: README family table (Voxtral Realtime row)` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "| Voxtral Realtime (2602) | `voxtral-mini-4b-realtime-2602` | streaming |"
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/parakeet-ultra.md: Parakeet Ultra intro` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "A drop-in replacement for v3: same size, languages and API."
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b.md: Nemotron 3.5 intro` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "Multilingual speech-to-text across 32 supported language-locales"

1. ggml/HIP: vendored ggml is SHA d7cb5741 with VERSION 0.26.0 and 3 downstream patches (threadpool oversubscription, backend-reg filter, metal mapped buffer). It is the newest ggml generation on our host (v10 0.16.0, whisper 0.18.0, qwentts 0.17.0, parakeet.cpp 0.13.0), so it needs its own tree, LD_LIBRARY_PATH and verify_ggml_linkage. HIP is -DTRANSCRIBE_HIP=ON -DAMDGPU_TARGETS=<arch>, README says ROCm 6.1+; the only validation on record is PR #121 (4x gfx1201, ROCm 7.2.3) and the maintainer says he owns no ROCm device. gfx90a/ROCm 6.2 is an untested, costed build item (CPU build 0.5 day; HIP 1-2 days to a parity-checked encode).
   - *unaffected*: Wave-1 pins (ggml d7cb574, 0.26.0, gfx1201/ROCm 7.2.3 only) re-confirmed at the same head db678a03; nothing moved.
   - anchor `tc/ggml/UPSTREAM: ggml pin file` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "sha: d7cb574130e6f01ad25b3_289_685_489_200febcd74"
   - anchor `tc/ggml/CMakeLists.txt: GGML version` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "set(GGML_VERSION_MINOR 26)"
   - anchor `tc/README.md: README Build (HIP)` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "For HIP/ROCm (Linux + AMD GPU), which needs ROCm 6.1 or newer:"
   - anchor `issue_121.json: PR body validation` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a PR #121: "Env: Ubuntu 24.04, ROCm 7.2.3, 4x gfx1201"
   - anchor `issue_92_comments.json: maintainer comment` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp issue #92 comment 2026-07-19: "I don't have any RocM devices to test on"
2. Whisper path is NOT a reproduction of production whisper.cpp. Its reference is HF transformers (WhisperForConditionalGeneration, fp32 CPU); whisper.cpp is listed only as a 'primary cross-check'. Decode is greedy with suppress_tokens plus a temperature-fallback loop using HF-style compression-ratio and avg-logprob rules (defaults temperature_inc 0.2, compression 2.4, logprob -1.0, no_speech 0.6); there is no beam search anywhere in src/arch/whisper or the public headers; segment timestamps only (no word timestamps); KV caches default to F16; F16-cache drift is documented. The repo can load whisper.cpp .bin files (magic-byte dispatch). An independent third party (issue #89, 2026-07) showed the same weights and same prompt giving a silently truncated tail in transcribe.cpp while whisper.cpp transcribed it completely; fixed in #96, but the re-test still differed from whisper.cpp in wording. So output equivalence with production-speech-v1 must be MEASURED, not assumed.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 H2 gate ('its whisper turbo output matches production whisper.cpp') is now sharper: reference is HF transformers, no beam search, F16 KV default, and one third-party divergence (#89) exists. The gate stays but must compare against production-speech-v1 on pause-rich short clips.
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/whisper-large-v3-turbo.md: Numerical Validation` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "the transcript matches the HF reference verbatim"
   - anchor `tc/docs/porting/families/whisper.md: References: cross-check` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "`refs/ggml-org/whisper.cpp` — ggml-native C++ implementation. Primary cross-check"
   - anchor `tc/docs/porting/families/whisper.md: Family doc (stale?)` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "Beam search and temperature fallback (whisper.cpp's `temperature_inc` strategy) are follow-ups for the production decode path."
   - anchor `tc/src/arch/whisper/public.cpp: run_ext defaults` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "p->no_speech_thold = 0.6f;"
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/whisper.md: Capabilities` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "Word-level timestamps are not currently exposed."
   - anchor `issue_89.json: isolation matrix` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a issue #89: "Reference whisper.cpp with the same weights **and the same prompt** transcribes the same audio completely."
   - anchor `issue_89_comments.json: reporter re-test after #96` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a issue #89 comment 2026-07-22: "So the wording is not fully identical to whisper.cpp"
3. Server/API and long-form: there is no HTTP server, no OpenAI-compatible endpoint and no streaming server anywhere in the tree at db678a03 (scan: no hit for http server, httplib or /v1/audio outside vendored ggml examples). Integration is the C API, bindings, transcribe-cli (--batch / --batch-jsonl) and examples/hello_stream. Whisper long-form is internal 30 s windows with prev-context stitching (max_audio_ms 0, never errors on length); Parakeet is unbounded via a conformer; hard-cap families return INPUT_TOO_LONG. Streaming exists for the Parakeet streaming variants, Nemotron, Moonshine Streaming and Voxtral Realtime, not for Whisper. A server would have to be written (a thin wrapper over the C API) to replace the :9000 whisper endpoint.
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/whisper.md: Input limits` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "Whisper slices long audio into 30-second windows internally and stitches the"
4. Speed on the only hardware published: whisper-large-v3-turbo Q8_0 CPU on a Ryzen 7 PRO 4750U is 9.85 s for the 11 s jfk clip (1.12x real time); on an M4 Max CPU 2.85 s (3.85x). No EPYC 9655, no MI210, no ROCm number exists. Third-party issue #124 reports CUDA slower than Vulkan on very short clips and faster from about 5 s (FunASR, Nemotron), which says GPU dispatch overhead dominates short utterances. An opt-in adaptive-window patch (#149) reports ~3x end-to-end on 2-6 s utterances at a documented accuracy trade, which echoes our own finding that stock whisper pads to 30 s.
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/whisper-large-v3-turbo.md: Performance Ryzen 4750U` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "| CPU | jfk (11.0s) | 9.85 s (1.12×) | 9.58 s (1.15×) |"
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/whisper-large-v3-turbo.md: Performance M4 Max` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "| CPU | jfk (11.0s) | 2.85 s (3.85×) | 3.11 s (3.54×) |"
   - anchor `issue_124_comments.json: third-party measurement` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a issue #124 comment 2026-08-27: "On FunASR on small chuncks, Vulkan is faster but starting with 5s CUDA wins"
5. Silero VAD v6.2 is a first-class family in transcribe.cpp (MIT, 1 MB F32 GGUF, streaming API, segments sample-exact vs upstream get_speech_timestamps, also loads whisper.cpp ggml-silero-v5.1.2/v6.2.0 .bin). VAD is a separate role: it is not wired in front of the ASR families by the library, and AUTO resolves VAD to CPU because GPU is less accurate. This gives us a second, independently ported VAD runner for CS-22 beside parakeet.cpp's.
   - anchor `tc/docs/vad.md: VAD doc Backend` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "`TRANSCRIBE_BACKEND_AUTO` resolves to the CPU for VAD models"
   - anchor `tc/docs/vad.md: VAD doc parity` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "the segments match the reference sample for sample"
   - anchor `tc/docs/models/whisper.md: Capabilities: what is not supported` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "What's not supported (consistent across the family): real-time"
6. Multi-family harness comparison (single bake-off harness): transcribe.cpp covers Whisper + Parakeet (v3, ultra, unified, TDT/CTC/RNNT) + Nemotron 3.5 and EN streaming + Canary + Qwen3-ASR + Granite + Voxtral Realtime + Cohere with ONE API, a published per-model WER catalog, and Silero VAD; it lacks a server and is not validated against whisper.cpp. mudler/parakeet.cpp (ggml 0.13.0) covers Parakeet/Nemotron/EOU/Ultra/Redux/Silero with an OpenAI-compatible server but no whisper or unified. NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp covers NeMo checkpoints with CUDA-only patches. On breadth and ggml freshness transcribe.cpp wins; on serving shape mudler wins; on gfx90a risk all three are untested, with transcribe.cpp the only one whose HIP build has any third-party validation (gfx1201).

## intake-2360#record — Silero VAD v6.2.3: pre-trained streaming voice activity detector (JIT/ONNX/tinygrad; GGUF ports in whisper.cpp, transcribe.cpp, parakeet.cpp)

- **URL**: https://github.com/snakers4/silero-vad
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty low, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2347#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identity: snakers4/silero-vad, MIT, package version 6.2.3 (release 2026-09-23), head 1e261b03 (merge of PR #805, 2026-09-29), 10.5k stars, 16 open issues. Model line: v6.0 2025-08-25, v6.2 2025-12-10; the wiki lists one live model 'silero_vad' (~260K params, streaming, '6000+' languages claimed). Bundled weights: JIT, ONNX (several opsets, half, ifless, sequence, OpenVINO), tinygrad safetensors. The README license badge text says CC BY-NC 4.0 while its image and the LICENSE file say MIT; LICENSE (MIT, Silero Team) is what binds.
   - *narrowed*: README license badge reads CC BY-NC 4.0 in its label text but MIT in its image and in LICENSE and pyproject; the LICENSE file and PyPI classifier (MIT) govern. Wave-1 'MIT' stands.
   - anchor `sv/pyproject.toml: pyproject` @ snakers4/silero-vad@1e261b036686cd0017d500ee96acd1c4ba572a9d: "version = "6.2.3""

1. Parameter count is stated inconsistently: wiki ~260K, transcribe.cpp's port notes 309K for the 16 kHz sub-model (the JIT file packs both 8 and 16 kHz models, per FAQ). Not material to deployment (1 to 2 MB, sub-millisecond per 32 ms frame on one CPU thread).
   - anchor `wiki/Version-history-and-Available-Models.md: Available models` @ snakers4/silero-vad.wiki@959929d2c8af572a4fff226c37b079609583f782: "| `'silero_vad'` | ~260K | VAD"
   - anchor `tc_silero_model.md: model page` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "F32 only: the model has 309K parameters"
2. GGUF/ggml ports used by the runners: ggml-org/whisper-vad (ggml-silero-v5.1.2.bin and v6.2.0.bin, used by whisper.cpp's built-in VAD flags); handy-computer/silero-vad-v6.2-gguf (F32, 1 MB, MIT, ported from upstream 5cd7945, validated against silero-vad 6.2.3 TorchScript; transcribe.cpp also loads the whisper.cpp .bin files); mudler/parakeet.cpp ships a v6 GGUF (1.3 MB; wave-1). transcribe.cpp's segmenter is a line-for-line port of get_speech_timestamps_from_probs and reproduces segments 'sample for sample' with equal parameters; GPU backends change probabilities, so CPU is the reference. Upstream head moved after the transcribe.cpp pin (5cd7945 to 1e261b03, v6.2.3 and PR #805 neg-threshold); re-check segment parity if upstream utils change.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 said the pinned runner copy is mudler's 1.3 MB GGUF; there are three ports (ggml-org .bin, handy GGUF, mudler GGUF), and transcribe.cpp's was pinned at upstream 5cd7945 while upstream head is now 1e261b03.
   - anchor `tc_vad.md: VAD doc` @ handy-computer/transcribe.cpp@db678a031cf4c524bcab2c45a0dc6a7813106e4a: "`transcribe_vad_run` reproduces Silero's `get_speech_timestamps`: with the same"

3. Vendor-measured quality (wiki, snapshot 959929d2, vendor datasets, vendor thresholds): on a 17 h multi-domain validation set Silero v6 has ROC-AUC 0.97 and 31.25 ms-chunk accuracy 0.92 (v5 0.96/0.91; WebRTC 0.73/0.74); noise-only whole-file accuracy ESC-50 0.87 and private noise 0.71 (v5 0.61/0.44). The wiki states that no paper, dataset or training code has been published. There is NO hesitation, filler, backchannel, laughter or breath category, and no per-class false-positive/false-negative rate. The v6 ROC-AUC table row has one extra cell (column mismatch in the wiki).
   - anchor `wiki/Quality-Metrics.md: Test datasets` @ snakers4/silero-vad.wiki@959929d2c8af572a4fff226c37b079609583f782: "Multi-Domain Validation Set** was used for main testing and model comparison."

4. Independent measurement 1 (competitor, FireRedASR2S arXiv:2603.10420v1): on FLEURS-VAD-102 (9,443 files, manually labelled binary VAD, test set promised not yet released) at a fixed 0.5 threshold and 10 ms frames, Silero-VAD has AUC-ROC 97.99, F1 95.95, false-alarm rate 9.41%, miss rate 3.95%; FireRedVAD 99.60/97.57/2.69/3.62; TEN-VAD 97.81/95.19/15.47/2.95. Silero's false alarms are non-trivial on non-speech frames at the default operating point. Not a hesitation/backchannel test and not independently replicated. (A web summary swapped the Silero and TEN columns; the table header order is FireRed, Silero, TEN, FunASR, WebRTC.)
   - *narrowed*: FireRed's own announcement outperforming Silero is a competitor benchmark on an unreleased test set; the web-search summary mis-assigned the Silero and TEN columns. Silero numbers are 95.95/9.41/3.95/97.99.
   - anchor `p_2603.10420.txt: FLEURS-VAD-102 table` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "Silero-VAD TEN-VAD FunASR-VAD WebRTC-VAD AUC-ROC (%) ↑ \uparrow 99.60 97.99 97.81 – – F1 (%) ↑ \uparrow 97.57 95.95 95.19 90.91 52.30 FAR (%) ↓ \downarrow 2.69 9.41 15.47 44.03 2.83 MR (%) ↓ \downarrow 3.62 3.95 2.95 0.42 64.15"
   - anchor `p_2603.10420.txt: FLEURS-VAD-102 test set` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "We will release FLEURS-VAD-102 and its annotation protocol to facilitate reproducible research."
   - anchor `p_2603.10420.txt: operating point` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "we use a fixed posterior threshold of 0.5 for all neural VAD models"
5. Independent measurement 2 (F-Actor, arXiv:2601.11329v3): default FD-Bench Silero-VAD settings did not generalise to Behavior-SD dialogues, 'often misclassifying interruptions or assigning incorrect timestamps to short backchannels'. Per dialogue on the Behavior-SD test set, Silero-VAD segmentation missed 1.6 backchannels and 1.0 interruptions and added 0.3 and 0.8 extra, versus Parakeet word timestamps with grid-searched merging (missing 1.0/0.5, extra 0.2/0.5). This is event-detection after merging with FD-Bench rules, not frame-level VAD error, and the dataset is synthetic dialogue. It is the nearest primary source for backchannel behaviour and says short backchannels are the weak case.
   - anchor `p_2601.11329.txt: C.2` @ arXiv:2601.11329v3: "default thresholds for merging words into utterances and defining backchannels or interruptions did not generalize well to the Behavior-SD test set"

   - anchor `p_2601.11329.txt: Table 5 caption` @ arXiv:2601.11329v3: "Silero-VAD uses the default FD-Bench settings"
6. Why hesitation and backchannels are policy-bound, from source: get_speech_timestamps defaults are threshold 0.5, min_speech_duration_ms 250 (segments shorter are dropped, so a sub-250 ms 'mm' or 'uh' is discarded by default), min_silence_duration_ms 100 (a pause of 100 ms ends a segment, so a thinking pause splits the turn), speech_pad_ms 30. The maintainer, asked for semantic end-of-turn (issue #649, 'uh, umm' placeholders), replied that Silero is language agnostic by design and a semantic model over 6000 languages is impractical. So hesitation tolerance has to come from the controller's end-of-turn policy (silence timer, Smart Turn, EOU), not from the VAD.
   - anchor `sv/src/silero_vad/utils_vad.py: get_speech_timestamps signature` @ snakers4/silero-vad@1e261b036686cd0017d500ee96acd1c4ba572a9d: "min_speech_duration_ms: int = 250,"
   - anchor `sv/src/silero_vad/utils_vad.py: get_speech_timestamps signature` @ snakers4/silero-vad@1e261b036686cd0017d500ee96acd1c4ba572a9d: "min_silence_duration_ms: int = 100,"
   - anchor `i_649.json: feature request: semantic VAD` @ snakers4/silero-vad issue #649: "people often use a placeholder while thinking (uh, umm...)"
   - anchor `c_649.json: maintainer reply` @ snakers4/silero-vad issue #649 comment 2025-05-29: "The key problem / feature / design assumption with our VAD is that it is language agnostic."
7. Known failure modes in the maintainer's tracker: musical instruments and child-like voices trigger false alarms and persist in v6 (#663 linked to #563); v6 emits lower probabilities than v5 so v5 thresholds do not carry over (#685, closed as tune-the-threshold); boundaries are late, onset P50 about +40 ms with a tail to +1.4 s when a weak initial syllable never crosses threshold, offset about +50 ms, and speech_pad cannot fix both ends (#798, open, 8,966 files, third-party, maintainer acknowledged and requested data). Silero is a streaming model; a truly non-streaming one is unreleased.
   - anchor `i_798.json: boundary bias` @ snakers4/silero-vad issue #798: "onset | **+39 to +43 ms** (98-99% of files positive)"
   - anchor `i_663.json: music false alarms` @ snakers4/silero-vad issue #663: "the sounds of musical instruments of the string group, such as the piano and guitar, cause false alarms"
   - anchor `c_663.json: maintainer` @ snakers4/silero-vad issue #663 comment 2025-08-26: "issue still persists with v6 with child-like voices and voice-like music instruments"
   - anchor `c_798.json: maintainer: model is streaming` @ snakers4/silero-vad issue #798 comment 2026-09-18: "the model itself IS STREAMING"
8. Versus our stack: conversation-stack CS-22 plans to compare whisper.cpp's built-in Silero flags with a standalone Silero plus a silence-threshold end-of-turn policy, measured on CS-4 hesitation clips; the production Silero weights are not pinned (only a test fixture exists). Smart Turn v3.2 (intake-2047#record) is not an alternative to Silero: it is invoked only after Silero sees trailing silence, and TurnBench's Smart Turn row used Silero at 0.5 with a 1000 ms silence gate. Parakeet-EOU-120M (intake-2017#record) is the other end-of-utterance arm. The decision-relevant measurement, Silero false-cut and miss rates on real hesitation and backchannel speech in it/de/fr/en, does not exist in any primary source and must be produced on CS-4.
   - anchor `intake-2047#record-extract.txt: Smart Turn input contract` @ research/intake_index.yaml@origin/main intake-2047#record: "it is meant to be run only after a lightweight VAD (Silero) sees silence"
   - anchor `intake-2047#record-extract.txt: TurnBench correction` @ research/intake_index.yaml@origin/main intake-2047#record: "Silero gate at 0.5, 1000 ms of silence before the FIRST"

There is NO new latency or streaming-quality feature in the delta and no published benchmark or WER: the commits are refactors, sampling/ABI hygiene, build/portability (Docker, macOS, Windows, GGML_SOURCE_DIR) and ggml bumps. ## intake-2362#record — llama.cpp PR #28957 "CUDA: bitonic argsort handles rows wider than one block" (merged 2026-09-29) with companion PR #29231 (HIP FP8 guard >= 6.3)

- **URL**: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/28957
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: already_integrated (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: hardware_optimization, local_inference, multimodal)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2350#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. PR #28957 (ggml-org/llama.cpp, ServeurpersoCom, merge commit 6a2743f02, 2026-09-29, 3 files +50/-29, approved by JohannesGaessler and IMbackK) fixes argsort on non-CUB builds (HIP, MUSA): the bitonic kernel launched one thread per padded column, so rows above 1024 launched an invalid block. Each thread now owns several columns, the block is capped at 1024 threads and supports_op checks shared memory per device instead of a fixed 1024.
   - anchor `PR body [pr_body.md]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "Fix argsort on HIP and MUSA builds for rows wider than 1024."
   - anchor `reviews [pr_reviews.txt]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "IMbackK APPROVED"
1. It is the same algorithm as our gfx90a patch b86f660 (qwentts ggml submodule, 2026-07-31): thread-strided bitonic within 1024 threads/block, supports_op via a shared-memory bound. Mechanism-for-mechanism equal; ours predates it by two months and was never sent upstream. The upstream PR was written independently from qwentts.cpp issue #33 (Radeon 780M), not from our patch, and its draft was AI-generated and verified bit-exact on NVIDIA by forcing the bitonic path.
   - *reattributed*: The brief and the wave-1 ledger treat b86f660 as locatable in /mnt/raid0/llm/llama.cpp. It is in the qwentts ggml submodule (pestopoppa/qwentts.cpp ggml, commit b86f660238dc, 2026-07-31); llama.cpp has only the FP8 half, as 0ebf1b4d7.
   - anchor `PR body [pr_body.md]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "Nothing in-tree sorts rows that wide, it is a ggml level fix for downstream projects that do, such as qwentts.cpp."
   - anchor `PR body [pr_body.md]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "tested on ROCm by the reporter of https://github.com/ServeurpersoCom/qwentts.cpp/issues/33 on a Radeon 780M."
   - anchor `PR body [pr_body.md]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "Bit-exact against CUB on NVIDIA with the bitonic kernel forced on rows of 2048"
   - anchor `qwentts.cpp#33 comment [issue33_comments.txt]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "Fair warning: this was written with Claude Fable 5.1 in an agentic session and validated bit-exact only on NVIDIA by forcing the bitonic path"
   - anchor `commit b86f660 [our_b86f660_msg.txt]` @ pestopoppa/qwentts.cpp @ 2c1b5182e (ggml b86f660238dcc1a83b7cbf5a72d355a965de9245): "Replaced with a thread-strided"
2. The companion FP8 edit our b86f660 also carried (HIP_VERSION >= 60300000 around hip_fp8.h) landed upstream as llama.cpp PR #29231 (IMbackK, 2026-09-25, fixes issue #27303): a byte-identical two-line change. Our llama.cpp tree already has the same guard as its own commit 0ebf1b4d7 (2026-07-02, in production-consolidated-v10).
   - anchor `git log -S [v10_fp8_guard_log.txt]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp fork @ ffc1bac82 (production-consolidated-v10): "hip: guard OCP fp8 typedefs behind ROCm>=6.3 (gfx90a/ROCm6.2 build fix)"
   - anchor `PR 29231 patch [pr29231.patch]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #29231: "+#if HIP_VERSION >= 60300000"
   - anchor `PR body [pr29231_body.md]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #29231: "Fixes https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/27303"
3. CS-43 ("upstream the gfx90a argsort fix ... to the qwentts.cpp / ggml fork as hygiene") is moot as written: the fix is upstream in llama.cpp, in ServeurpersoCom/ggml (qwentts 71ad93d, 2026-09-14) and in the s2s.cpp-pinned ggml 0.27.0, authored by the qwentts maintainer. There is nothing left to upstream. What remains is verification on gfx90a, which nobody has done.
   - *narrowed*: CS-43 is moot as an UPSTREAMING task, not as a risk: the replacement task is gfx90a verification. Wave-1 S4 ("may be redundant") is upgraded to read-confirmed.
   - anchor `conversation-stack.md CS-43 [cs43.txt]` @ epyc-root conversation-stack.md @ working tree 2026-10-21: "upstream the gfx90a argsort fix"
   - anchor `PR body [pr_body.md]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp PR #28957 merge 6a2743f028f78bfb88a7189607b49bde30df3769: "Nothing in-tree sorts rows that wide, it is a ggml level fix for downstream projects that do, such as qwentts.cpp."
4. A llama.cpp rebase WOULD drop our FP8 guard (0ebf1b4d7 becomes an empty/conflict-free duplicate) and would pick up the argsort kernel without any local patch, because production-consolidated-v10 (ffc1bac82) does NOT contain #28957 (merge-base --is-ancestor: no) and still has the one-thread-per-column launch. v10 is safe today only because its supports_op returns ne[0] <= 1024 on non-CUB builds, so the llama.cpp scheduler falls back to CPU for wider rows; qwentts has no scheduler and crashes instead (remark by the PR author, who is the qwentts maintainer, in the PR thread). Our b86f660 itself lives only in the frozen qwentts ggml, not in llama.cpp, so a llama.cpp rebase never drops it; a qwentts ggml rebase would.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1 phrased it as "a ggml>=0.27 rebase of speech kernels may not need our patches". True for the qwentts ggml. For llama.cpp the relevant fact is that v10 lacks #28957 entirely and is guarded by supports_op <= 1024.
   - anchor `ggml-cuda.cu @ffc1bac82 [v10_supports_op_excerpt.txt]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp fork @ ffc1bac82 (production-consolidated-v10): "return op->src[0]->ne[0] <= 1024;"
   - anchor `argsort.cu @ffc1bac82 [v10_argsort.cu]` @ ggml-org/llama.cpp fork @ ffc1bac82 (production-consolidated-v10): "const dim3 block_dims(ncols_pad, 1, 1);"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 2: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - llama.cpp PR 29231 "HIP: bump HIP_VERSION required for fp8" (IMbackK, 2026-09-25) -> declined: covered here (dive's own reason) - llama.cpp issue 27303 (missing __hip_fp8_e4m3 on ROCm 6.2) -> declined: symptom already in our b86f660 comment (dive's own reason) - ServeurpersoCom/qwentts.cpp issue 33 -> declined: already read and anchored in this dive (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

CS-43 has nothing left to upstream; do not spend effort on it. Do not conclude the upstream kernel is proven on MI210: it is read-equivalent and unmeasured there. llama.cpp v10 is safe without it (CPU fallback); only the qwentts ggml path needs it.
