- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): llama.cpp-experimental champion rebase: expect 0ebf1b4d7 to drop; do not hand-resolve, drop it and run the FP8 compile on ROCm 6.2.

## intake-2363#record — FireRedASR2S: A State-of-the-Art Industrial-Grade All-in-One Automatic Speech Recognition System (VAD component: FireRedVAD, FLEURS-VAD-102)

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.10420
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2360#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identity and licence: the VAD is a component of FireRedASR2S (arXiv:2603.10420v1, Xiaohongshu FireRed team; sole version, v2 not found). Code: FireRedTeam/FireRedASR2S head 4e7d9aaf (2026-06-02, Apache-2.0, 722 stars) and a standalone FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD repo head c30ec49e (2026-05-06, Apache-2.0). Weights: HF FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD sha 7990aacc (Apache-2.0; VAD, Stream-VAD, AED folders, each model.pth.tar plus cmvn.ark). Three DFSMN models of about 0.6M parameters (about 2.2 MB fp32): non-streaming VAD (look-ahead order 20, i.e. about 200 ms of future context at 10 ms frames), streaming VAD (look-ahead 0, per-layer cache, causal) and a multi-label mVAD/AED (speech, singing, music). Features are 80-dim fbank, 25 ms window, 10 ms shift, CMVN. Output is a frame posterior turned into segments by smoothing, a threshold and a finite-state postprocessor with min speech, min silence, pad, merge and max-length parameters.
   - anchor `p.txt: abstract FireRedVAD` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "An ultra-lightweight module (0.6M parameters) based on the Deep Feedforward Sequential Memory Network (DFSMN), supporting streaming VAD, non-streaming VAD, and multi-label VAD (mVAD)."
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 model size` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "all three FireRedVAD models are extremely lightweight, each containing only ∼0.6M parameters (approximately 2.2 MB in float32 format)"
   - anchor `hf_FireRedTeam_FireRedVAD.json: HF API license tag` @ HF FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD@7990aaccc6b7aec1e527743bd30201f2c4a03b8c: ""license":"apache-2.0""
   - anchor `cstr_readme.md: GGUF variants` @ HF cstr/firered-vad-GGUF@f965baa5daf11f6dbad5fd10_068_455_666_980c717: "| `firered-stream-vad.gguf` | Stream-VAD | 2.3 MB | 568K | Streaming (no lookahead) |"
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 look-ahead` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "For non-streaming VAD and mVAD, we use look-ahead order 20 with stride 1 to utilize future context for improved offline segmentation. For streaming VAD, we use look-ahead order 0 to ensure causal inference."
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 features` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "FireRedVAD uses the same acoustic features as FireRedASR2"
1. FLEURS-VAD-102 Table 3, read from the HTML table header order (FireRedVAD, Silero, TEN, FunASR, WebRTC): AUC-ROC 99.60 / 97.99 / 97.81 / - / -; F1 97.57 / 95.95 / 95.19 / 90.91 / 52.30; false-alarm rate 2.69 / 9.41 / 15.47 / 44.03 / 2.83; miss rate 3.62 / 3.95 / 2.95 / 0.42 / 64.15. Frame level (25 ms window, 10 ms shift), one fixed threshold 0.5 for every neural VAD. Silero is therefore F1 95.95, FAR 9.41, MR 3.95; TEN is F1 95.19, FAR 15.47, MR 2.95. TEN has the lowest miss rate of the three neural VADs and the highest false-alarm rate. These are competitor-reported numbers on an unreleased set, with no independent replication found.
   - *narrowed*: Silero-dive web summary swapped Silero and TEN columns; header order is FireRedVAD, Silero, TEN, FunASR, WebRTC. Silero is 95.95/9.41/3.95/97.99 and TEN 95.19/15.47/2.95/97.81. Also the Silero dive wrote that TEN has 'the lowest miss rate in the FireRed table': FunASR-VAD is lower (0.42), TEN is lowest among the neural posterior-output VADs.
   - anchor `tbl3.txt: Table 3 (cells extracted from the HTML table)` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "Metric \ Model FireRedVAD Silero-VAD TEN-VAD FunASR-VAD WebRTC-VAD AUC-ROC (%) ↑ 99.60 97.99 97.81 – – F1 (%) ↑ 97.57 95.95 95.19 90.91 52.30 FAR (%) ↓ 2.69 9.41 15.47 44.03 2.83 MR (%) ↓ 3.62 3.95 2.95 0.42 64.15"
   - anchor `p.txt: 7.2 operating point` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "we use a fixed posterior threshold of 0.5 for all neural VAD models to provide a consistent operating point; tuning thresholds on a development set may further improve F1 for individual systems."
   - anchor `p.txt: 7.2 frame setup` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "All VAD metrics are computed at the frame level."
2. Which FireRedVAD model produced Table 3 is not stated in the paper. The Hugging Face card and the standalone repo README both say 'Non-streaming VAD achieves 97.57% F1', and the non-streaming model sees about 200 ms of look-ahead while Silero and TEN are causal streaming models. No streaming-model F1/FAR/MR appears in the paper, the FireRedASR2S README, the FireRedVAD README or the HF card (grepped). The causal Stream-VAD, the only variant usable as a live barge-in gate, therefore has NO published accuracy number and the 'outperforms Silero and TEN' headline is a non-streaming-versus-streaming comparison.
   - *narrowed*: The headline 97.57% F1 belongs to the NON-streaming FireRedVAD (HF card, repo README); the paper table does not name the model and no streaming-model number exists. The Silero dive described FireRedVAD as 'streaming and non-streaming ... stronger-on-paper gate candidate'; that holds for non-streaming only.
   - anchor `hf_card.md: card first paragraph` @ HF FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD README@7990aacc: "Non-streaming VAD achieves 97.57% F1 on FLEURS-VAD-102"
   - anchor `vadrepo/README.md: README intro` @ FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD@c30ec49e8cc69642b0ee65362eba11b9d11c6e54: "Non-streaming VAD achieves 97.57% F1 on FLEURS-VAD-102"
3. Test-set status as of 2026-10-21: NOT released. The paper says 'We will release FLEURS-VAD-102', and the FireRedASR2S README at head still says 'This VAD testset will be open sourced (coming soon)'; the guessed HF dataset URL FireRedTeam/FLEURS-VAD-102 returns 401 (no such public dataset). The set is about 100 manually labelled FLEURS test files per language over 102 languages (9,443 files). FLEURS is read speech, and the paper reports no hesitation, filler, backchannel, laughter or overlap category and no per-language breakdown (grepped: zero hits for hesitation, backchannel, filler). Whether it behaves better on hesitations and backchannels is therefore UNKNOWN, not 'yes'. it/de/fr/en are all FLEURS languages, so a future release could seed a language-gate check.
   - *unaffected*: Silero dive said the test set is unreleased; re-checked 2026-10-21 against repo README head and the HF URL: still unreleased.
   - anchor `p.txt: 7.2 test set` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "We will release FLEURS-VAD-102 and its annotation protocol to facilitate reproducible research."
   - anchor `repo/README.md: README FLEURS-VAD-102` @ FireRedTeam/FireRedASR2S@4e7d9aaf4482a47cec1724807026b9b151926eb5: "This VAD testset will be open sourced (coming soon)."
   - anchor `p.txt: 7.2 test set construction` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "sampling approximately 100 audio files per language from the FLEURS test set and manually annotating binary VAD labels, resulting in 9,443 audio files in total"
4. Hesitation and backchannel behaviour is parameter-bound exactly as for Silero. FireRedVAD's streaming postprocessor (smooth_window_size, speech_threshold, pad_start_frame, min_speech_frame, max_speech_frame, min_silence_frame) is the same parameter family. The README examples use min_speech_frame=8 (80 ms) and min_silence_frame=20 (200 ms) for streaming, and min_speech_frame=20 (200 ms) for non-streaming, against Silero's 250 ms / 100 ms defaults; a shorter min-speech default favours short backchannels, but that is a default, not a measured behaviour. The smoothing window (5 frames in the examples) adds a derived, unmeasured onset delay of the order of tens of ms.
   - anchor `repo/README.md: streaming config example` @ FireRedTeam/FireRedASR2S@4e7d9aaf4482a47cec1724807026b9b151926eb5: "min_speech_frame=8,"
   - anchor `repo/README.md: non-streaming config example` @ FireRedTeam/FireRedASR2S@4e7d9aaf4482a47cec1724807026b9b151926eb5: "min_speech_frame=20,"
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 postprocessing` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "a finite-state postprocessor enforces minimum voice and silence duration constraints"
5. Ports: the weights are tiny enough that porting is not a barrier. cstr/firered-vad-GGUF sha f965baa5 (Apache-2.0, F32, three files 2.3-2.4 MB: firered-vad.gguf 588K params, firered-stream-vad.gguf 568K, firered-aed-vad.gguf 589K) targets the MIT-licensed CrispStrobe/CrispASR C++ ggml runtime; Strg-Alt-Entf-0x00/FireRedVAD-GGUF (fp32/int16/int8, no licence tag), tardigrade-doc/FireRedVAD_onnx (model.onnx, Apache-2.0), plus C, CoreML and ESP32-P4 ports exist. The postprocessor is plain Python and must be reimplemented in the controller, as for Silero.
   - anchor `cstr_readme.md: GGUF target runtime` @ HF cstr/firered-vad-GGUF@f965baa5daf11f6dbad5fd10_068_455_666_980c717: "GGUF conversions of [`FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD`](https://huggingface.co/FireRedTeam/FireRedVAD) for use with **[CrispStrobe/CrispASR](https://github.com/CrispStrobe/CrispASR)**."
   - anchor `cstr_readme.md: GGUF no quantisation` @ HF cstr/firered-vad-GGUF@f965baa5daf11f6dbad5fd10_068_455_666_980c717: "All variants are F32 (no quantization needed"
6. Open issues touching the VAD in FireRedASR2S: #47 (open, 2026-04-11) reports a missing backtracking step in the POSSIBLE_SILENCE state that leaves redundant silence tails on segments, which would add to end-of-turn latency if the reference postprocessor is copied unchanged; #65 (open, 2026-06-17) asks whether real-time dialogue logic will be supported (no reply). No issue reports hesitation or backchannel behaviour.
   - anchor `i_47.json: postprocessor bug` @ FireRedTeam/FireRedASR2S issue #47 (2026-04-11, open): "Missing backtracking in POSSIBLE_SILENCE state leads to redundant silence tails in VAD segments"
7. Training data: 'thousands of hours of human-annotated acoustic event data' with binary labels derived from speech/singing/music/silence/noise annotation; the languages and per-language hours are not stated, and the HF card lists only en and zh as languages. 'Language-agnostic' and '100+ languages' rest on the FLEURS-102 evaluation alone. Fit for CS-22: it is a credible third gate arm (CPU, 0.6M params, ggml-ready), but it cannot replace Silero on paper evidence; replacement needs the CS-22 S2 grid on real it/de/fr/en hesitation and backchannel clips, on the causal Stream-VAD model.
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 training data` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1: "FireRedVAD is trained on thousands of hours of high-quality human-annotated acoustic event data"

## intake-2364#record — TEN VAD: low-latency, lightweight, high-performance streaming voice activity detector (ONNX + C/WASM; Apache-2.0 with Agora conditions)

- **URL**: https://github.com/TEN-framework/ten-vad
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2360#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identity and licence: TEN-framework/ten-vad head d68ab71c (2026-10-09), 2.3k stars, 25 open issues; a component of the TEN voice-agent ecosystem (Agora). Licence is Apache-2.0 WITH ADDITIONAL CONDITIONS (GitHub reports NOASSERTION): do not Deploy it in a way that competes with Agora's offerings or lets others develop/deploy Applications; use only for your own Application(s) and your direct end users; derivative works stay under this licence; pitch_est.cc carries BSD-2/3 LPCNet-derived code. An open question (#89, unanswered) asks whether fftw.h/fftw.c relate to GPL FFTW. For an internal speech stack the conditions are probably workable but they are not Apache-2.0 and are not OSI-clean; operator/legal reading is required before it enters a shipped build.
   - anchor `repo/LICENSE: LICENSE condition 1` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "You may not Deploy the ten-vad in a way that competes with Agora's offerings"
   - anchor `repo/LICENSE: LICENSE condition 2` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "You may Deploy the ten-vad solely to create and enable deployment of your Application(s) solely for your benefit and the benefit of your direct End Users."
   - anchor `repo/README.md: License section` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "This project is licensed pursuant to the Apache 2.0 with additional conditions."
   - anchor `i_89.json: FFTW naming` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad issue #89 (2026-03-31, open): "there are 2 files fftw.h and fftw.c"
1. Architecture and runtime: a small causal streaming network. From the third-party ggml README: 256-sample (16 ms) hop, pre-emphasis, 1024-point FFT, 40-band mel plus a pitch feature, 3-frame context, three separable convs, two LSTM layers (hidden 64), dense layers, sigmoid; 296 KB fp32. The official repo ships an ONNX model (src/onnx_model/ten-vad.onnx, 315 KB) plus the C++ preprocessing source (STFT, mel, pitch from LPCNet) and prebuilt libs for Linux x64, Windows, macOS, Android, iOS and WASM. The official Linux lib is x64 only (an ARM64 request, #71/#22, is open); that suits the EPYC host. Hop sizes 160 and 256 samples (10 and 16 ms) are the optimised configurations.
   - anchor `ggml_readme.md: architecture` @ HF danielbodart/ten-vad-ggml@b1c529cd230d44e3c8845e139bf1885246fb0af8: "Sep Conv → LSTM → Dense → Sigmoid"
   - anchor `ggml_readme.md: size` @ HF danielbodart/ten-vad-ggml@b1c529cd230d44e3c8845e139bf1885246fb0af8: "| Model size | 296 KB |"
   - anchor `repo/README.md: sampling rate and hop` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "TEN VAD operates on 16kHz audio input with configurable hop sizes (optimized frame configurations: 160/256 samples=10/16ms)."
   - anchor `repo/README.md: ONNX release` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "We **finally** released and **open-sourced** the **ONNX** model and the corresponding **preprocessing code**!"
2. Ports: official ONNX + C/C++ source; sherpa-onnx integration (per README, 2025-07); danielbodart/ten-vad-ggml (HF sha b1c529cd, MIT, 296 KB ggml .bin, Zig reference implementation, README says the only ggml port it knows of), plus ONNX, CoreML and Axera ports on HF. A ggml route therefore exists but is a single-author port; the official LSTM+mel+pitch front end must be reproduced exactly (the ONNX file does not include feature extraction, per issue #46 title and the README's separate 'preprocessing code').
   - anchor `repo/README.md: platform note` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "TEN VAD provides cross-platform C compatibility across five operating systems (Linux x64, Windows, macOS, Android, iOS), with Python bindings optimized for Linux x64, with wasm for Web."
   - anchor `ggml_readme.md: ggml port` @ HF danielbodart/ten-vad-ggml@b1c529cd230d44e3c8845e139bf1885246fb0af8: "This is the only GGML implementation of TEN-VAD that we're aware of."
3. Vendor accuracy evidence is a plot, not numbers: precision-recall curves versus WebRTC and Silero on a 30-file released test set (augmented clean speech plus noise/reverb from librispeech, gigaspeech, DNS), reproducible with plot_pr_curves.py. No false-alarm or miss number is published by the vendor. A third party (guynich, issue #23) could not reproduce the gap on about 50 h of clean LibriSpeech/MLS and the maintainer replied that the public set is easy-noise-added and that the larger test set (open corpora plus private real data) 'can not be shared'. A separate user (#84) could not reproduce the README curve either (open).
   - *unaffected*: README states the PR-curve test set is released; it is (30 wav + label files, 8.2 MB), but the maintainers say the larger set behind the 'reproducible' claim is private.
   - anchor `repo/README.md: PR curves testset` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "The evaluation is conducted on the precisely manually annotated testset. The audio files are from librispeech, gigaspeech, DNS Challenge etc."
   - anchor `repo/README.md: threshold` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "Note that the default threshold of 0.5 is used to generate binary speech indicators (0 for non-speech signal, 1 for speech signal)."
   - anchor `c_23.json: test set size` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad issue #23 comment 2025-06-09 (maintainer): "the data can not be shared"
   - anchor `c_23.json: test set too easy` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad issue #23 comment 2025-06-09 (maintainer): "the test sets are too easy"
4. Independent numbers: (a) FireRedASR2S Table 3, FLEURS-VAD-102, threshold 0.5, 10 ms frames: TEN AUC 97.81, F1 95.19, false-alarm rate 15.47%, miss rate 2.95% (Silero 97.99/95.95/9.41/3.95). TEN has the lowest miss rate of the neural VADs and the highest false-alarm rate. (b) guynich/vad_eval_comparison (LibriSpeech + multilingual LibriSpeech test, 512-sample chunks): ROC AUC TEN 0.985 vs Silero 0.981 (0.996 vs 0.994 excluding low-confidence), PR AUC 0.994 vs 0.993; 'similar'; TEN's usable threshold range is wider, so a shared 0.5 'could be misleading'. The two sources rank TEN vs Silero in opposite orders on AUC; the difference is within what threshold and data selection can explain. There is no source that measures TEN on hesitations, fillers or backchannels.
   - *narrowed*: dive-silero-vad said TEN has the 'lowest miss rate in FireRed table': true among the neural posterior VADs (2.95) but FunASR-VAD is lower (0.42, at 44.03% false alarms). The task asked for 'measured FA/miss': the only FA/miss measurement is FireRed's, 15.47/2.95; the vendor publishes none.
   - anchor `tbl3_ten.txt: Table 3 TEN column` @ arXiv:2603.10420v1 (via dive-fireredvad tbl3.txt): "Metric \ Model FireRedVAD Silero-VAD TEN-VAD FunASR-VAD WebRTC-VAD AUC-ROC (%) ↑ 99.60 97.99 97.81 – – F1 (%) ↑ 97.57 95.95 95.19 90.91 52.30 FAR (%) ↓ 2.69 9.41 15.47 44.03 2.83 MR (%) ↓ 3.62 3.95 2.95 0.42 64.15"
   - anchor `guynich_readme.md: ROC AUC table` @ guynich/vad_eval_comparison@main (README fetched 2026-10-21): "| all | 0.981 | 0.985 | |"
   - anchor `guynich_readme.md: comments` @ guynich/vad_eval_comparison@main (README fetched 2026-10-21): "Comparing both models with the same value for threshold (e.g.: 0.5) could be misleading."
5. Latency and cost: vendor RTF 0.0086 to 0.016 on x86 CPUs (AMD Ryzen 9 5900X 0.0150, Xeon Platinum 8253 0.0136, Xeon Gold 6348 0.0086 vs Silero 0.0127 on the same row only), library 277 KB to 731 KB versus Silero 2.16 MB (JIT). Silero's own wiki reports about 189 us per 31.25 ms chunk on ONNX (RTF about 0.006 on a Threadripper), so the cross-vendor RTF comparison is not like-for-like and is unreplicated. Either VAD is negligible CPU against the STT. The vendor claim that Silero 'suffers from a delay of several hundred milliseconds' is shown on one example plot; Silero's tracker measures onset about +40 ms and offset about +50 ms median (issue #798), so 'several hundred ms' is an end-of-segment policy effect (min_silence) rather than model delay. No end-of-turn latency number for TEN exists in any source.
   - *narrowed*: README 'much lower computational complexity' rests on RTF compared with Silero in one table row (Xeon Gold 6348: 0.0086 vs 0.0127); Silero's own wiki figure implies a lower RTF on different hardware. Unreplicated and not like-for-like.
   - anchor `readme_rtf.txt: RTF table` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "Intel(R) Xeon(R) Gold 6348 CPU @ 2.60GHz 0.0086 0.0127"
   - anchor `repo/README.md: delay claim` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad@d68ab71c8d5fbd8f98e97ae7c9986af76b5d72b1: "Silero VAD suffers from a delay of several hundred milliseconds"
6. Reported failure cases: #79 (open, Chinese, fast speech) found TEN did not cut continuous speech at 20 ms pauses where Silero cut at 40 ms, and the maintainer asked for wavs (no resolution); #50 (open) musical-instrument sound recognised as voice; #33 and #58 report -1 probabilities at the first frame / non-256 hop sizes; #29 asks for noise filtering. Maintainers state (#82) that TEN is trained on 'a large amount of languages' and is 'language independent', with no language list or per-language figure, so it is an assertion, not evidence, for the it/de/fr/en gate.
   - anchor `i_79.json: faster speech` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad issue #79 (2025-12-03, open): "ten-vad did not cut, whereas sirelo-vad performed better"
   - anchor `c_82.json: language claim` @ TEN-framework/ten-vad issue #82 comment 2025-12-23 (maintainer): "TEN VAD performs excellently across all languages and is language independent"
7. Fit for CS-22 and CS-26: a viable extra gate arm (CPU, causal, 16 ms hop, ONNX and ggml routes), not a replacement. Its measured weakness is false alarms (15.47% at 0.5) which on a half-duplex gate cost spurious barge-in; its strength is a low miss rate and fast frames. Whether it handles hesitations or backchannels better than Silero is unmeasured; the lack of a semantic end-of-turn is shared. The sibling ten-turn-detection repo (not read) is the TEN project's semantic end-of-turn model.
8. Licence-adjacent footprint: bundle contains a prebuilt closed libten_vad.so (313 KB) next to the readable src/, and issue #85 (open) asks how the official libraries were built; to stay inside our freeze/reproducibility rules a CS-22 arm should run the ONNX model with the in-tree preprocessing (examples_onnx), or the ggml port, rather than the prebuilt binary.

### Dive corrections

Slug references in this entry resolve as: sherpa-onnx=intake-2379#record, ten-turn-detection=intake-2376#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - TEN-framework/ten-turn-detection (semantic end-of-turn model of the TEN stack; sibling of ten-vad) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2376#record (ten-turn-detection) - k2-fsa/sherpa-onnx (integrates TEN VAD and Silero, ONNX CPU ASR/VAD runtime) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2379#record (sherpa-onnx) - guynich/vad_eval_comparison and HF datasets guynich/librispeech_asr_test_vad, guynich/multilingual_librispeech_test_vad -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2380#record (guynich-vad-eval) - danielbodart/ten-vad-ggml -> declined: artifact port, pinned in T1 (dive's own reason) - arXiv:2603.10420 FireRedASR2S -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2363#record (fireredvad)

## intake-2365#record — FD-Bench: A Full-Duplex Benchmarking Pipeline Designed for Full Duplex Spoken Dialogue Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.19040
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: benchmark_methodology, multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2360#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Identity and dedup: FD-Bench = 'FD-Bench: A Full-Duplex Benchmarking Pipeline Designed for Full Duplex Spoken Dialogue Systems', Peng, Chao, Ng, Ma, Ni, Ma, Chng (NTU Singapore per the repo licence), arXiv:2507.19040 v1 only (2025-07-25; v2 does not exist). Code pengyizhou/FD-Bench head 8a4b7df1 (2025-08-14); dataset HF pengyizhou/FD-Bench-Audio-Input sha 995e7178 (2025-10-09) and an Output dataset. NOT in origin/main research/intake_index.yaml (0 hits on 2507.19040 or 'FD-Bench'). It is NOT the indexed Full-Duplex-Bench family (Lin et al.: intake-2003#record, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2032): different authors, harness and metrics. The names are easy to conflate (the silero dive and F-Actor use 'FD-Bench' for this one; the index and PersonaPlex entry use 'FDB' for Lin's).
   - *reattributed*: The silero dive and F-Actor call this 'FD-Bench' and the silero dive guessed it was 'likely already indexed via the full-duplex entries'. It is not: the indexed full-duplex benchmarks are Lin et al.'s Full-Duplex-Bench family (intake-2003#record/2013/2014/2015/2032). FD-Bench (Peng, arXiv 2507.19040) is a separate, unindexed work.
   - anchor `p.txt: abstract` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "we present a comprehensive FD benchmarking pipeline utilizing LLMs, TTS, and ASR to address this gap."
1. What it is: a synthetic pipeline, not recorded data. GPT-4o writes 293 multi-round user/AI dialogues (up to 5 rounds, up to 4 interruptions each, 1,196 interruptions in five types: Affirmative Acknowledgment 350, Denial and Discontent 58, Further Inquiry 393, Requiring a Repeat 109, Topic Shift 316; some combined), only the USER side is synthesised with ChatTTS, F5-TTS or CosyVoice2 using LibriSpeech reference speakers, at Easy/Medium/Hard interruption density, with MUSAN noise as gap noise or background noise at several SNRs (over 40 h in total). The audio is streamed to the system under test through a server-client harness and its output is scored.
   - anchor `p.txt: abstract scale` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "using over 40 hours of generated speech, with 293 simulated conversations and 1,200 interruptions"
   - anchor `p.txt: 2.1 pipeline` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "we utilize State-of-the-Art speech synthesis models to generate speech of the users from the simulated conversations"
   - anchor `p.txt: Table 1 caption` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "We have various types of interruptions—Affirmative Acknowledgment, Denial and Discontent, Further Inquiry, Requiring a Repeat, and Topic Shift"
   - anchor `p.txt: 2.2 totals` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "The entire corpus includes 293 multi-round conversations with up to 4 interruptions each, resulting in around 1.2k interruptions"
2. What it measures: interruption-handling rates SRR (successful reply to a non-interrupted inquiry), SIR (successful interrupt), SRIR (successful reply to an interrupt), EIR (early interrupt by the AI), NIR (AI interrupted by noise in a gap between inquiries); timing medians IRD (interrupt-response delay), FSED (first-speech-emit delay), ERT (early-reply time), EIT (early-interrupt time); output WER (Whisper large-v3-turbo / WhisperX vs the system's own text), conditional perplexity (Llama-3.3-70B int8) and a GPT-4o 1-10 six-aspect quality score. Timing comes from Silero-VAD at threshold 0.5 on BOTH the user stream and the system output. It does NOT define a backchannel-retention metric (does the system keep talking through 'mm-hm'): 'Affirmative Acknowledgment' appears only as one of five interruption types and the metric list contains no reward for continuing; hesitation/pause-holding is not covered either. Those are covered by Full-Duplex-Bench (Lin), already indexed.
   - *narrowed*: The abstract advertises backchanneling, but no backchannel metric is defined in the metric section; Affirmative Acknowledgment is one of five interruption types (350 of 1,196). It does not test whether a system correctly keeps talking through a backchannel.
   - *narrowed*: The silero dive described F-Actor/FD-Bench scoring as 'Silero-based backchannel/interruption scoring'; confirmed: Silero-VAD at 0.5 produces the timestamps for both user and system audio, which makes the scorer circular for any Silero-gated system.
   - anchor `p.txt: 2.3 interruption metrics` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "SRRate (measures the odds of Success-Replies per User-non-interrupt-inquiries)"
   - anchor `p.txt: 2.3 noise metric` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "NIRate (measures the rates of Noise-Interrupts per Noise-gaps between user inquiries)"
   - anchor `p.txt: 2.3 timing metrics` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "Timing metrics—IRD(interrupt-response-delay), FSED(first-speech-emit-delay), ERT(early-reply-time), and EIT(early-interrupt-time)"
   - anchor `p.txt: 3 scoring setup` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "We apply Silero-VAD with a threshold of 0.5 to get timestamp information from both the simulated user speech and the model’s output."
   - anchor `p.txt: 3 ASR` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "we utilize Whisper-large-v3-turbo to perform ASR with a beam-size of 5"
   - anchor `p.txt: 3 C-PPL` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "we use the Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct model to calculate Conditioned-PPL with the precision int-8"
3. Results (CosyVoice2 speech, single H20 GPU, English only): Moshi (built-in full duplex, 80 ms chunks) beats the VAD-gated Freeze-Omni (107 ms, VAD threshold 0.8) and VITA-1.5 (200 ms, 0.7) on SRR/SRIR/SIR; Moshi easy: WER 5.3, SRR 61.7, SRIR 79.4, SIR 83.1, EIR 27.9, median IRD 1345 ms, FSED 4155 ms, ERT 221 ms, EIT 2008 ms; all systems sometimes reply early or interrupt by about 2 s, response delays above 10 s in the VAD-based systems are attributed to VAD misjudgement and server instability; a high VAD threshold (0.8) shields Freeze-Omni from gap noise; SRR drops over 40% relative for Moshi and VITA under frequent interruptions. These are single-run numbers on three systems, with no confidence intervals, and cannot be compared with our cascade (different architecture, English synthetic user speech).
   - anchor `tbl34.txt: Table 3` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "FD-Type Model Chunk-size VAD-thresh. built-in Moshi 80 - VAD-based Freeze-Omni 107 0.8 VITA-1.5 200 0.7"
   - anchor `tbl34.txt: Table 4 Moshi easy` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "Moshi 5-E 5.3 61.7 79.4 83.1 27.9 1345 4155 221 2008 20 4.43"
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 finding` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "Moshi consistently outperforms VAD-based-interruption systems in interruption-related metrics"
   - anchor `p.txt: 4 noise` @ arXiv:2507.19040v1: "likely due to a high VAD threshold of 0.8 that effectively filters most gap noise"
4. Language and licence: English only (HF card language: en; GPT-4o dialogue text, English TTS prompts). Repo and data carry the NTUITIVE (NTU) non-commercial dual licence; the LICENSE text refers to a different repository (Shilin-LU/MACE), a template carry-over, so the intended scope is ambiguous. Internal research evaluation is plausibly within non-commercial terms; shipping any derived data/code is not cleared. The pipeline (dialogue-generation prompts, TTS scripts, benchmarking.py with metric definitions, WER and C-PPL scripts) is open and re-runnable with our own LLM and TTS in it/de/fr/en; the English dataset cannot serve the it/de/fr/en gate directly.
   - anchor `repo/LICENSE.txt: licence head` @ pengyizhou/FD-Bench@8a4b7df1b4dcb0fc50a7a5660247a2ffe8d394eb: "Non-Commercial Use Only."
   - anchor `hfd.json: HF card license` @ HF dataset pengyizhou/FD-Bench-Audio-Input@995e7178445b90d57dee8fab500c6e37a2f2c49e: ""license":"other","license_name":"ntuitive""
   - anchor `hfd.json: HF card language` @ HF dataset pengyizhou/FD-Bench-Audio-Input@995e7178445b90d57dee8fab500c6e37a2f2c49e: ""language":["en"]"
   - anchor `repo/README.md: Released` @ pengyizhou/FD-Bench@8a4b7df1b4dcb0fc50a7a5660247a2ffe8d394eb: "Dataset release to Hugging Face"
   - anchor `repo/README.md: dataset download` @ pengyizhou/FD-Bench@8a4b7df1b4dcb0fc50a7a5660247a2ffe8d394eb: "git clone https://huggingface.co/datasets/pengyizhou/FD-Bench-Audio-Input"
5. Fit for CS-4/CS-22/CS-23/CS-36: the protocol shape is the useful part. CS-4 set 5 ('multi-turn referent and interruption scripts') needs interruption scripts with controlled insertion times and type labels; FD-Bench's five-type taxonomy, controlled-density levels, noise-gap and background-noise conditions, and the SIR/SRIR/NIR/IRD/EIR definitions give a ready metric set for the barge-in chain (CS-23, cancel latency measured separately), false-cut by non-speech (CS-22) and end-to-end acceptance (CS-36). The synthetic-speech objection in CS-4 (synthetic TTS cannot test paralinguistic perception) does not apply to timing tests, but does apply to anything about hesitation or backchannel naturalness, which still needs the real recordings. Its scorer reuses Silero-VAD at 0.5 as oracle on both channels, so it must not be used unchanged to score a Silero-gated system (circular); timing for the user side is known exactly from the synthetic timeline and the system side should be taken from ASR word times with the F-Actor grid-search lesson.
6. Credibility: a clear, reproducible benchmark paper (code, metrics, data released) with an honest scope statement, single v1 preprint, three systems evaluated, no human validation of 'success' labels reported in the text read, and subjective scores from GPT-4o. The abstract claims 'backchanneling' as a capability, but the evaluated metrics cover interruptions, delays and noise, not backchannel behaviour.

Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". ## intake-2366#record — TurboBias 2.0: Streaming Context-Biasing for Production-Efficient ASR Systems

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21343
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2358#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism: TurboBias 2.0 is decoder-side shallow-fusion phrase boosting for transducers (RNNT and TDT), no retraining, no auxiliary CTC model or text-correction stage. Phrases are tokenised with the model's own SentencePiece tokenizer into a weighted acceptor with suffix links (a trie in the case-sensitive version of the earlier TurboBias); every matched token adds a bonus to the token logits during greedy or beam decoding. Three additions over TurboBias: (a) a case-insensitive graph built from a 'variative BPE' chain whose arcs span any vocabulary token that lowercases onto the same character path, with a cumulative score potential per state (Eq. 1, tau controls how front-loaded the bonus is) so every segmentation earns the same total; (b) per-stream models: all active boosting graphs are concatenated into one tensor store addressed by a per-stream model id (-1 = no biasing); (c) streaming greedy and beam decoding (ASLD++, beam 32) with chunk-level hypothesis storage.
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism is described in the paper text (II-A to II-C) and matches the NeMo source read in the sibling dive nemo-pr14277-boosting.
   - anchor `paper.txt: I Introduction` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "TurboBias introduced a GPU-accelerated phrase-boosting tree for shallow-fusion context biasing"
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-A` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "every matched token receives a shallow-fusion bonus during decoding"
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-A case-insensitive` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "each character arc can be replaced with a set of two parallel arcs for case variants"
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-A Eq.1 text` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Setting τ=0 gives a uniform split"
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-B` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "we concatenate the tensors of all active boosting trees into merged multi-model storage"
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-B` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "while id −1 denotes no biasing"
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-C` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "The implementation remains compatible with CUDA Graphs and fully GPU-based batched operations"
   - anchor `paper.txt: V Conclusion` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "without auxiliary CTC inference, post-recognition correction, or model retraining"
1. AUTHOR-REPORTED rare-term gains (English only, Contextual Earnings-22, keyword F-score): parakeet-unified-en-0.6b offline 65.2 (greedy, no context) -> 81.5 (greedy, per-stream global list with distractors) -> 87.5 (beam), WER 14.4 -> 13.1 -> 12.6; per-stream local list (oracle phrases only) 91.2. Streaming at 1.12 s worst-case latency: 62.2 -> 81.7 F, WER 16.3 -> 14.3 (beam, global). TDT: offline parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2 reaches 83.0 F / 13.3% WER with per-stream global beam boosting, against 82.0 F / 13.7% for the authors' own reproduction of CTC-WS (needs a second TDT-CTC model plus a correction stage). Internal 3 h medical set: Unified 71.1 F / 14.0% WER vs Nemotron-streaming-en 68.2 F / 18.0% at 1.12 s.
   - *unaffected*: Numbers copied from Table II and Section IV; internal medical set cannot be checked (not released). All are author-reported.
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, TDT-v2 offline` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "reaching 83.0 F-score and 13.3% WER on Earnings22 with beam search"
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, Unified offline` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "the Unified model reaches 87.5 F-score and 12.6% WER on Earnings22"
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, CTC-WS repro` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "from 64.9 to 82.0 F-score and reduces WER from 14.7% to 13.7%"
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, Argmax reference` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "remains below the reported Argmax point of 88.2 F-score and 12.7% WER"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table II no-context greedy row` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Greedy | – | – | – | 65.2 (92.6/50.3) | 14.4"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table II beam per-stream global row` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Beam | Global | case-ins. | ✓ | 87.5 (92.4/83.0) | 12.6"
2. FALSE INSERTIONS are measured only as keyword precision, never as insertions on lexicon-free audio. Precision falls when a shared list is applied to every stream: Unified greedy, no context P 92.6; shared full list case-insensitive P 89.9 (-2.7 pp) while recall rises 50.3 -> 64.8; beam shared lower-cased list P 90.9. Per-stream global lists (target phrases plus realistic distractors) recover P 91.5 (greedy) and 92.4 (beam) at R 73.4 / 83.0. So distractors are the cost driver and scoping the list to the stream is what controls them. The paper reports no WER on audio that contains none of the listed terms and no score sweep, so the false-insertion-vs-boost-score curve our lexicon trial needs is NOT in the source.
   - *narrowed*: The abstract says boosting improves contextual recognition while preserving latency; the paper never measures insertions on lexicon-free audio, only keyword precision, and precision DROPS 2.7 pp under a shared list. The Stage-2 sibling (olicorne-ultimed) asked for false-insertion evidence: it is absent here.
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table II full case-insensitive greedy row (precision 89.9 vs 92.6 unbiased)` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "case-ins. | – | 75.4 (89.9/64.8) | 13.7"
   - anchor `paper.txt: I, motivation for per-stream` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "shared boosting tree can introduce distractors and false acceptances across streams"
   - anchor `paper.txt: III-D metrics` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "keyword precision, recall, and F-score on contextual phrases only"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table II beam, shared full list` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Beam | Full | lower | – | 72.5 (90.9/60.3) | 13.3"
3. RUNTIME COST (single RTX A5000 24 GB, 128 concurrent streams, Unified model, Earnings22; RTFx higher is better): greedy 1812 unbiased -> 1726 with one shared tree (-4.7%); beam 1150 -> 1005 (-12.6%). Per-stream models compiled on demand with no cache fall to RTFx 61 (greedy) / 59 (beam); CPU-memory cache 1493 / 905 (the paper's '9-17%' overhead); registering models at decoder construction restores 1717 / 989. A boosting model is typically < 1 MB. All GPU/PyTorch; there is no CPU, ROCm or ggml datum.
   - *narrowed*: The abstract and conclusion say low overhead / high throughput. True only with decoder-level model registration (RTFx 1717 vs 1812 unbiased). Uncached per-stream compilation costs 97% of throughput (RTFx 61). The quoted 9-17% memory-cache overhead is measured against the decoder-registered baseline; against the unbiased baseline the dive computes -17.6% (greedy full), -19.0% (greedy global) and -21.3%/-22.7% (beam full/global).
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table III greedy, unbiased vs full tree` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "No biasing | – | – | 65.2 | 1812 | Full | – | – | 75.4 | 1726"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table III per-stream, no cache` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Full | – | ✓ | 75.4 | 61"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table III per-stream, memory cache` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Full | memory | ✓ | 75.4 | 1493"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table III per-stream, decoder registration` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Full | decoder | ✓ | 75.4 | 1717"
   - anchor `paper.txt: Table III beam, unbiased vs full tree` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "No biasing | – | – | 65.9 | 1150 | Full | – | – | 81.4 | 1005"
   - anchor `paper.txt: III-D hardware` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "We use a single NVIDIA RTX A5000 24GB GPU."
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, speed paragraph` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "Keeping compiled models in CPU memory introduces only a small recognition-speed overhead of approximately 9-17%."
   - anchor `paper.txt: II-B size` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "an individual boosting model is typically below 1 MB"
4. Scope limits: every model and dataset in the paper is English (parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2, nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b, parakeet-unified-en-0.6b); one public benchmark (Contextual Earnings-22: 738 unique keywords) plus an unreleased internal 3 h medical set. No Italian, German or French result, so portability of the numbers to the CS language gate is untested. Code is open in NVIDIA NeMo via PRs #15800 (case-insensitive), #15125 (per-stream) and #15753 (batched streaming beam search for RNN-T and TDT).
   - *unaffected*: English-only scope is stated by the paper itself.
   - anchor `paper.txt: III-A` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "we use publicly available English Transducer-based ASR models"
   - anchor `paper.txt: III-B medical set` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "The dataset contains approximately 3 hours of English medical speech"
   - anchor `paper.txt: I footnote` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "The proposed TurboBias 2.0 framework is open-sourced as part of the NVIDIA NeMo toolkit"
   - anchor `paper.txt: III-A TDT` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "a 600M-parameter model based on a FastConformer encoder [23] and a TDT decoder"
5. Latency: streaming TDT-v2 (chunked) degrades badly vs offline and boosting does not repair it; the Unified RNNT model keeps its gains in streaming (offline 87.5 F vs streaming 1.12 s 81.7 F with beam). Streaming boosting needs a model that is itself good at streaming, which is the argument for pairing it with a cache-aware RNNT arm (Nemotron) rather than a TDT arm for conversation.
   - *unaffected*: Streaming claims are stated with their own latency settings.
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, streaming Unified 1.12 s` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "improves from 62.2 to 81.7 F-score and from 16.3% to 14.3% WER after global boosting and beam search"
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, medical streaming` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "at 1.12s latency it reaches 71.1 F-score and 14.0% WER, compared with 68.2 F-score and 18.0% WER for Nemotron-streaming"
   - anchor `paper.txt: IV, streaming TDT` @ arXiv:2608.21343v1: "context biasing cannot fully compensate for this loss"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 stage2b wave 3: first primary read. Narrowed the abstract throughput claim (decoder registration only) and the implicit false-insertion claim (precision only). Reattributed the parakeet_web lineage to PR #14277 (v1), correcting the sibling olicorne-ultimed dive-surfaced-source note. Expanded from intake-2358#record, intake-2355#record (first listed is expanded_from; the rest are in cross_references). Slug references in this entry resolve as: nemo-pr14277-boosting=intake-2367#record, nemo-speech-cpp=intake-2349#record, nemotron35-asr-streaming=intake-2355#record, olicorne-ultimed=intake-2358#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv 2508.07014 TurboBias: Universal ASR Context-Biasing powered by GPU-accelerated Phrase-Boosting Tree (ASRU 2025; title resolved on arXiv API 2026 -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2374#record (turbobias1) - arXiv 2604.07354 Contextual Earnings-22: A Speech Recognition Benchmark with Custom Vocabulary in the Wild (abs title resolved) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2375#record (contextual-earnings22) - arXiv 2406.07096 Fast Context-Biasing for CTC and Transducer ASR models with CTC-based Word Spotter (abs title resolved) -> declined for now (dive's own); resurfaced by intake-2374#record (turbobias1) as ingest-and-dive and DECLINED under the operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22) - arXiv 2505.22857 NGPU-LM: GPU-Accelerated N-Gram Language Model for Context-Biasing in Greedy ASR Decoding (abs title resolved) -> monitor (dive's own): NGPU-LM 2505.22857; declined in the dive of intake-2374#record (turbobias1) as weaker than the phrase tree for isolated words - NVIDIA NeMo PRs #15800 (case-insensitive, merged 2026-08-07), #15125 (per-stream, merged 2026-01-14), #15753 (batched streaming beam for RNN-T and TDT -> declined: knowledge only; read the diffs only if T4/T5 trigger (dive's own reason) - nvidia/parakeet-unified-en-0.6b (best model in the paper) and arXiv 2604.19079 -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2357#record (unified-rnnt-2604.19079)

### Verdict

TurboBias 2.0 is decoder-side phrase boosting that works on TDT as well as RNNT, needs no retraining, and costs 5-13% throughput on GPU when models are pre-registered. Its evidence is English-only and measures false positives only as keyword precision; do not cite it as showing low false-insertion rates on general speech, and do not read its TDT result as a streaming result. For us it justifies a lexicon-boosting trial, not a deployment.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Run the CS domain-lexicon boosting trial (M1) with the trie already in NeMo-Speech.cpp on the Nemotron-3.5 streaming arm first; it is the same mechanism family as TurboBias (v1 semantics, greedy, host-side trie).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Make the trial measure what the paper does not: lexicon-term recall AND insertions of lexicon terms on lexicon-free it/de/fr/en audio, across a boost-score sweep.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Treat case-insensitive matching as a cheap host-side addition (graph compile only) because our lexicon is mixed-case (EPYC, MI210, gfx90a).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Treat beam search as a separate, larger build item (greedy-only in NeMo-Speech.cpp); +6.0 F points in the paper is the price tag.

## intake-2367#record — NVIDIA/NeMo PR #14277: GPU-accelerated Phrase-Boosting (GPU-PB) for CTC, RNN-T, and TDT decoding

- **URL**: https://github.com/NVIDIA/NeMo/pull/14277
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_component (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2358#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Yes, NeMo supports phrase boosting for TDT. PR #14277 (merged 2025-08-08, 19 files, +1253/-801) adds GPU phrase boosting ('GPU-PB') in shallow-fusion mode for CTC, RNN-T and TDT, greedy and beam, with CUDA graphs, built on the NGPU-LM infrastructure. The PR body states 'TDT uses the same logic as RNN-T models'. TDT-specific code is in tdt_label_looping.py (+190 lines, greedy) and tdt_malsd_batched_computer.py (+189 lines, beam).
   - *unaffected*: Verified in the PR body and in the merged TDT decoder diff.
   - anchor `pr_body.txt: PR body, summary` @ NVIDIA/NeMo#14277 merge commit 8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c (merged 2025-08-08T11:40:55Z, author andrusenkoau, head 01fc913f): "Implementation of GPU-accelerated Phrase-Boosting method (GPU-PB) in shallow fusion mode for CTC, RNN-T, and TDT decoding based on NGPU-LM infrastructure."
   - anchor `pr_body.txt: PR body, summary` @ NVIDIA/NeMo#14277 merge commit 8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c (merged 2025-08-08T11:40:55Z, author andrusenkoau, head 01fc913f): "The method supports greedy and beam search decoding, including CUDA graphs mode."
   - anchor `pr_body.txt: PR body, usage` @ NVIDIA/NeMo#14277 merge commit 8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c (merged 2025-08-08T11:40:55Z, author andrusenkoau, head 01fc913f): "TDT uses the same logic as RNN-T models"
1. Mechanism in the TDT decoder: the boosting tree is one entry in a list of 'fusion models'; at each label-looping step its per-token score vector (alpha * advance(state)) is added to the TOKEN logits only (the slice before blank and the duration logits), the argmax over those boosted logits picks the non-blank label, and the blank-versus-non-blank decision is kept from the UNBOOSTED logits. Duration prediction is untouched. The tree can be evaluated on CPU (a PyTorch path exists when Triton/CUDA is absent), so a reference oracle for a ggml port does not need a GPU.
   - *unaffected*: Read from the merge-commit source of tdt_label_looping.py.
   - anchor `tdt_label_looping.patch.txt: tdt_label_looping.py diff (torch_impl)` @ NVIDIA/NeMo@8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c: "logits_with_fusion[:, : -num_durations - 1] += self.fusion_models_alpha[fusion_idx] * fusion_scores"
   - anchor `tdt_label_looping.patch.txt: tdt_label_looping.py diff (torch_impl)` @ NVIDIA/NeMo@8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c: "preserve "blank" / "non-blank" category"
   - anchor `tdt_malsd.patch.txt: tdt_malsd_batched_computer.py diff` @ NVIDIA/NeMo@8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c: "fusion_models: list of fusion models (ngram_lm_model and boosting_tree_model)"
   - anchor `src_boosting_graph_batched.py: GPUBoostingTreeModel.advance` @ NVIDIA/NeMo@8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c: "scores, next_states = self._advance_pytorch(states=states)"
2. Evidence strength in the PR itself is thin: the only TDT test is a smoke test (test_tdt_greedy_decoding parametrised with a two-phrase list 'hello','nvidia' on parakeet-tdt_ctc-110m, no accuracy assertion found) and the PR body has no results section. The accuracy evidence for TDT boosting comes later, from TurboBias 2.0 (parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2: 83.0 F-score on Contextual Earnings-22 with per-stream beam boosting), and from the parakeet_web author's informal report of lower CER on medical terms.
   - *narrowed*: Stage-1 framing and the operator brief read PR #14277 as showing TDT boosting works. It shows TDT boosting is IMPLEMENTED and smoke-tested; no accuracy result is in the PR.
   - anchor `test_test_rnnt_decoding.py.patch.txt: test_tdt_greedy_decoding` @ NVIDIA/NeMo@8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c: "BoostingTreeModelConfig(key_phrases_list=["hello", "nvidia"])"
   - anchor `pr_body.txt: PR body, results section absent (template line only)` @ NVIDIA/NeMo#14277 merge commit 8f434e6be72f569bb234eba58791a51e895af48c (merged 2025-08-08T11:40:55Z, author andrusenkoau, head 01fc913f): "Add specific line by line info of high level changes in this PR."
3. NeMo-Speech.cpp (the official ggml runner) does NOT implement it for TDT: its docs say 'greedy CTC without an LM and Parakeet TDT ignore' speech_contexts. The code explains why: the ggml RNNT path ports the same Aho-Corasick tree (context_biasing.h/.cpp, 'Ported from NeMo's ContextGraph + GPUBoostingTreeModel') and passes a logit bias into the joint graph, but build_joint_tail throws 'TDT joint logit bias is not supported', and TdtGreedyDecoder (lines 359-489 of rnnt_greedy_decoder.cpp) contains no bias/boost reference at all. So a request with speech_contexts on a TDT model is ignored silently, with no error.
   - *unaffected*: Wave-1/wave-2 statement that NeMo-Speech.cpp ignores TDT boosting is confirmed against docs and code; refined: it is an explicit gap in the TDT joint graph plus a missing decoder hookup, and the failure is silent.
   - anchor `nss_configuration.md: docs/asr/configuration.md, Word boosting` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373: "greedy CTC without an LM and Parakeet TDT ignore it"
   - anchor `nss_rnnt_modules.cpp: RnntJointModule::build_joint_tail` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373: "TDT joint logit bias is not supported"
   - anchor `nss_context_biasing.h: context_biasing.h header comment` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373: "Ported from NeMo's context_biasing.ContextGraph + GPUBoostingTreeModel"
   - anchor `nss_rnnt_greedy_decoder.cpp: RnntGreedyDecoder boost path` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373: "NeMo/riva semantics (rnnt_label_looping.py): blank vs non-blank is"
   - anchor `absence_check.txt: grep absence check on rnnt_greedy_decoder.cpp` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373: "# matches at line >= 359: 0"
   - anchor `nss_rnnt_greedy_decoder.cpp: file header` @ NVIDIA/NeMo-Speech.cpp@2bdfb03b38d5e3b0fa55aeb91a079a9f57385373: "Host-side RNNT/TDT greedy decoding over the staged predictor and joint graphs."
4. Cost of closing the gap in a ggml runner (DIVE ESTIMATE, not measured): about 250-400 lines of C++ and 3-5 days including a parity test, no new GPU kernels (ggml_add on a token-logit view plus one extra argmax), so gfx90a needs no new op support beyond what the TDT graph already uses. Work items: (1) in build_joint_tail remove the TDT throw, add a bias to the token slice only and emit a second argmax over boosted non-blank logits (blank decision stays on unboosted logits); (2) thread a logit_bias argument through joint_tdt_argmax / predict_and_joint_tdt_argmax in model.h/.cpp; (3) factor the request-to-tree construction (rnnt_greedy_decoder.cpp lines 51-135) into a shared helper and give TdtGreedyDecoder the same bias_tree_/bias_node_ state, advancing the match state on each non-blank commit and resetting it at utterance boundaries; (4) parity test against NeMo's PyTorch GPUBoostingTreeModel on CPU with a 20-phrase list on parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3 clips, token-identical at fixed alpha.
   - *unaffected*: Estimate, labelled as such; no build attempted.

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 stage2b wave 3: first primary read. Narrowed "PR shows TDT boosting works" to "implemented and smoke-tested"; refined the runner-gap statement (explicit throw plus missing decoder hookup; silent ignore). Expanded from intake-2358#record, intake-2349#record (first listed is expanded_from; the rest are in cross_references). Slug references in this entry resolve as: nemo-speech-cpp=intake-2349#record, olicorne-ultimed=intake-2358#record, turbobias2=intake-2366#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - NVIDIA/NeMo PRs #15125 (per-stream boosting), #15753 (batched streaming beam search RNN-T/TDT), #15800 (case-insensitive boosting) -> declined: knowledge only (dive's own reason) - NVIDIA/NeMo docs: ASR context biasing / phrase-boosting page -> declined at the wave-4 close-out (operator): NeMo docs context-biasing/phrase-boosting page not selected - Kieirra/murmure issue #338 (downstream app discussing phrase boosting, linked from the PR thread) -> declined: application-level discussion (dive's own reason) - thiswillbeyourgithub/parakeet_web (browser port of PR #14277) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2358#record (olicorne-ultimed)

### Verdict

NeMo (PyTorch) supports phrase boosting for TDT since 2025-08-08, so the NeMo-Speech.cpp statement that Parakeet TDT ignores boosting is a missing ggml feature, not a model limitation. Do not read the PR as accuracy evidence; use TurboBias 2.0 for numbers. The missing feature is small to build because the RNNT ggml path and the tree already exist.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Close the TDT gap in NeMo-Speech.cpp (or our fork) so the Parakeet-v3/ultra arm has a lexicon path: est. 250-400 LOC, 3-5 days, CPU-verified parity first.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Before then, make speech_contexts on a TDT model fail loudly instead of being ignored.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Use NeMo PyTorch GPUBoostingTreeModel on CPU as the parity oracle.

### Claims (as verified)

0. Fine-tune recipe and its cost: the Hugging Face fine-tuning blog (NVIDIA, 2026-06-04) describes a FULL fine-tune of the Nemotron 3.5 streaming RNNT checkpoint (init_from_nemo_model, same cache-aware FastConformer-RNNT recipe, every clip tagged with target_lang) on a ~2000 h balanced Greek+Bulgarian mix (Granary, Common Voice, FLEURS), 'a single GPU for a quick pass', with a fixed step budget. The blog states no GPU type, no step count, no wall-clock and no learning rate. Result, author-reported, FLEURS test at 80 ms: Greek WER 35 -> 24 (-32% relative), Bulgarian 22 -> 15 (-31%), rounded to integers; adding ~2000 h of parliamentary speech took the pool from ~290 h to ~2,300 h and improved the weakest further. LoRA is 'not supported now' (author reply, ~Aug-Sep 2026).

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 stage2b wave 3: first primary read. Overturned the wave-2 pointer to a "companion repo" as the recipe (it is an AN4 smoke tutorial); narrowed "recipe with cost" and "replay lesson"; reattributed the latency figures to the English sibling. Expanded from intake-2355#record, intake-2356#record (first listed is expanded_from; the rest are in cross_references). Slug references in this entry resolve as: nemotron-streaming-en=intake-2356#record, nemotron35-asr-streaming=intake-2355#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - Artificial Analysis AA-WER Streaming Index vs Time to Final Transcription (cited by the fine-tuning blog) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2373#record (aa-streaming-asr-latency) - github.com/nvidia-riva/tutorials (asr-finetune-nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-prompt.ipynb at 83ca0000) -> declined: covered by this entry (dive's own reason) - nvidia/Granary dataset (multilingual pseudo-labelled corpus with LLM-generated PnC labels; the dev-blog author says PnC labels came from it) -> declined: training-only, no STT training planned (dive's own reason) - nvidia/magpie_tts_multilingual_357m (TTS model used in the Daily voice-agent pipeline, 7 languages) -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2384#record (magpie-tts-multilingual) - eigenai.com blog 2026-06-04 day-0 inference for Nemotron 3.x ASR (linked from the fine-tuning blog) -> declined: partner marketing page (dive's own reason) - Daily pipecat voice-agent example and Modal deployment guide (linked from the dev blog) -> declined: covered by existing pipecat entries - nvidia/parakeet_realtime_eou_120m-v1 -> declined: covered (intake-2017#record)

### Verdict

These are NVIDIA marketing-plus-tutorial posts. The fine-tune results (Greek, Bulgarian) are outside our language gate and cannot be reproduced from the published notebook, which is an AN4 toy. Take from them only: replay and re-check non-target languages; evaluate at deployment latency; EOU model for cache reset. Do not take throughput, latency or time-to-final figures as expectations on EPYC/MI210, and remember most of them describe the English model.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Add "replay slice plus held-out regression check on the non-target languages at deployment settings" to the CS-42 regression guard and to any STT adaptation task.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Treat the vendor throughput/latency figures as literature-class context, not as expectations for EPYC or MI210.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Do not plan an STT fine-tune from this recipe; the published material cannot reproduce its own numbers.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Add time-to-final-after-end-of-speech as a reported metric in the CS-8.P5 streaming arm so the 24 ms / 70 ms vendor claims have a like-for-like local counterpart.

## intake-2369#record — Stateful Conformer with Cache-based Inference for Streaming Automatic Speech Recognition

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.17279
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty low, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2355#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Mechanism (NVIDIA, arXiv 2312.17279, ICASSP 2024 per the abs page): make a FastConformer encoder streamable by (1) limiting both look-ahead and past context during TRAINING so train and inference see the same context (fully causal convolutions, layer norm instead of batch norm, no utterance-level feature normalisation), using chunk-aware attention (chunk of C frames sees the whole chunk plus a limited number of previous chunks, so effective look-ahead does not grow with depth), and (2) at inference caching, per layer, the input activations of self-attention (grows to the left-context size) and the last K-1 inputs of each depthwise convolution, plus the RNNT predictor LSTM states, so each audio frame is processed exactly once with no overlapping buffer. The result is stated to be exactly the same as processing the whole clip at once with the same limited context. Works with CTC or RNNT heads; a hybrid CTC/RNNT model shares one encoder (loss weight 0.3 on CTC). This is the architecture of the Nemotron streaming models (att_context [56, N]).
   - *unaffected*: Mechanism read from Sections 3.1-3.3; matches the later Nemotron att_context description in the sibling dives.
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Abstract` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "introducing an activation caching mechanism to enable the non-autoregressive encoder to operate autoregressively during inference"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 3.1` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "We avoid using normalization in the mel-spectrogram feature extraction step"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 3.3` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "the cache size for a self-attention layer grows from zero up to the past context size"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 3.3` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "By maintaining such caches, the prediction of the network would be exactly the same as when the entire audio is processed in a single step."
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4.1` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "The accuracy of our streaming model is exactly the same when evaluated in offline and streaming modes"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "of the hybrid loss is set to 0.3"
1. Latency/accuracy curve (AUTHOR-REPORTED, ~114M-parameter FastConformer, LibriSpeech test-other, hybrid chunk-aware, 'average algorithmic latency' columns 0/40/240/520/680/1360 ms): RNNT WER 9.5 / 9.0 / 7.8 / 7.5 / 7.3 / 6.3 (offline full context 5.0); CTC 10.6 / 10.1 / 8.8 / 8.4 / 8.0 / 7.1 (offline 5.7). Steepest gain is between zero look-ahead and ~240 ms (RNNT -18%), then diminishing, with a further step at 1360 ms. Chunk-aware beats regular look-ahead at equal latency (RNNT 6.3 vs 7.1 at 1360 ms). On 26K-hour NeMo ASRSET 3.0 the RNNT average over 9 sets is 12.1 / 11.3 / 10.6 at 40 / 240 / 520 ms. Latency here is algorithmic only (EIL, average word delay = half the look-ahead), not wall-clock and not the 'chunk size' labels (80-1120 ms) that Nemotron uses.
   - *unaffected*: Numbers copied from Tables 1-3; the latency definition (EIL, half the look-ahead) is part of the claim.
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 2, RNNT hybrid chunk-aware row (columns 0/40/240/520/680/1360 ms)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "9.5 | 9.0 | 7.8 | 7.5 | 7.3 | 6.3"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 2, CTC hybrid chunk-aware row` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "10.6 | 10.1 | 8.8 | 8.4 | 8.0 | 7.1"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 1, RNNT offline reference` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "Offline | Full context | 5.0"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 3, RNNT cache-aware 40 ms row (ASRSET, averaged column last)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "RNNT | 40 | 6.4 | 2.6 | 6.1 | 21.0 | 15.2 | 6.2 | 13.8 | 8.2 | 29.1 | 12.1"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4.2` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "The average of latency for each case is half of the look-ahead size."
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4 (latency definition)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "All the average latencies in this paper are referring to algorithmic latency induced by the encoder (EIL)"
2. Versus buffered streaming: on LibriSpeech the buffered RNNT baseline (2 s chunks, 4 s buffer) is far worse (WER 11.3 at 2000 ms) than chunk-aware streaming (6.3 at 1360 ms). On ASRSET the RNNT claim holds everywhere (buffered average 15.0 vs 10.6-12.1). For CTC it does NOT hold at every latency: buffered CTC (average 12.0) beats cache-aware CTC at 40 ms (13.3) and 240 ms (12.6) and only loses at 520 ms (11.2); on Earnings22 (20.1 vs 21.2), GigaSpeech (14.0 vs 14.3) and Tedlium (5.5 vs 5.8) buffered CTC is better even at 520 ms. So the paper's 'outperform on all benchmarks' is an RNNT statement.
   - *narrowed*: The paper says cache-aware models outperform buffered streaming on all benchmarks. Table 3 shows that is true for RNNT but false for CTC at 40 and 240 ms (average) and on Earnings22, GigaSpeech and Tedlium even at 520 ms.
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 1, RNNT buffered row` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "Buffered | Buffering | 11.3 | 2000"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 1, RNNT chunk-aware row` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "Chunk-aware | 6.3 | 1360"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 3, RNNT buffered row` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "2000 | 9.4 | 4.8 | 8.8 | 23.8 | 16.4 | 7.0"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 3, CTC buffered row (Earnings22 20.1, GigaSpeech 14.0, Tedlium 5.5)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "2000 | 7.7 | 3.6 | 6.8 | 20.1 | 14.0 | 5.5"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 3, CTC cache-aware 240 ms row (Earnings22 22.2, avg 12.6)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "240 | 7.3 | 3.4 | 6.6 | 22.2 | 15.8 | 6.5 | 15.1 | 8.8 | 27.3 | 12.6"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 3, CTC cache-aware 520 ms row (Earnings22 21.2, avg 11.2)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "520 | 6.2 | 2.6 | 6.1 | 21.2 | 14.3 | 5.8 | 13.6 | 7.8 | 23.3 | 11.2"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4.3 (the sentence the CTC rows contradict)` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "the cache-aware streaming models outperform the buffered streaming models on all benchmarks"
3. One model, many latencies: training with a randomly chosen chunk size per batch gives a single checkpoint that matches or beats single-latency models (RNNT 6.2 / 5.5 / 5.2 vs 6.4 / 5.9 / 5.4 at 40 / 240 / 520 ms; CTC 7.6 / 6.5 / 6.0 vs 7.9 / 7.3 / 6.2), at the cost of needing more training steps. This is why a Nemotron checkpoint exposes five chunk sizes without retraining.
   - *unaffected*: Table 4; the training-steps caveat is the paper's own.
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 4, RNNT single-lookahead row` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "Single-Lookahead | RNNT | 6.4 | 5.9 | 5.4"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Table 4, RNNT multi-lookahead row` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "6.2 | 5.5 | 5.2"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4.4` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "The multi-lookahead model may need more steps to achieve the same accuracy as training a single latency model."
4. The 'lower inference time' claim in the abstract and the 'significantly faster than buffered' sentence are asserted from the design (no recomputation), not measured: the paper contains no real-time factor, wall-clock, throughput or FLOP count. Its latency numbers exclude network compute time by construction. Quantitative speed evidence for the design comes later and from NVIDIA's own serving blogs (nemotron35-blogs), not from this paper.
   - *narrowed*: Abstract says lower inference time; the paper reports no inference-time measurement.
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: Abstract` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "better accuracy with lower latency and inference time compared to a conventional buffered streaming model baseline"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4.1` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "It makes the cache-aware streaming models significantly faster than buffered streaming models."
5. Scope: English only; ~114M-parameter models; LibriSpeech (with 10% speed perturbation) and NeMo ASRSET 3.0 (26K h); trained >= 200 epochs; open-sourced in NeMo. The arXiv title changed between versions (v1 'Stateful FastConformer ...', v3 'Stateful Conformer ...'); the technique applies to Conformer or FastConformer but only FastConformer is evaluated. Peer-review status rests on the author comment 'ICASSP 2024' (not checked against proceedings).
   - *unaffected*: Scope facts are stated in the paper; venue is from the abs page comment.
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "which have ≈114M parameters"
   - anchor `paper_v3.txt: 4` @ arXiv:2312.17279v3: "with total size of 26K hours"
   - anchor `abs.html: Comments field` @ arXiv abs page 2312.17279 (fetched 2026-10-21): "ICASSP 2024"

### Dive corrections

- 2026-10-21 stage2b wave 3: first primary read. Narrowed two claims (CTC "all benchmarks"; "lower inference time"). Expanded from intake-2355#record, intake-2356#record (first listed is expanded_from; the rest are in cross_references). Slug references in this entry resolve as: nemotron-streaming-en=intake-2356#record, nemotron35-asr-streaming=intake-2355#record, nemotron35-blogs=intake-2368#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv 2305.05084 Fast Conformer with Linearly Scalable Attention for Efficient Speech Recognition (abs title resolved) -> declined at the wave-4 close-out (operator), verbatim: "Haven't we already collected plenty of knowledge of the parakeet and nemotron architectures? They've appeared over and over in various research-intake sessions." (arXiv 2305.05084 FastConformer) - arXiv 2010.11148 FastEmit: Low-latency Streaming ASR with Sequence-level Emission Regularization (abs title resolved) -> declined: training-time loss regulariser (dive's own reason) - Emformer (ICASSP 2021, Shi et al.), Chen et al. streaming transformer transducer, scout network, synchronous transformers (paper refs [33], [9], [19], -> declined: background prior art (dive's own reason) - huggingface.co/nvidia/stt_en_fastconformer_hybrid_large_streaming_multi (paper ref [35], the released model) -> declined: superseded by the Nemotron streaming checkpoints (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

This is the mechanism paper for cache-aware streaming (Nemotron style): limited-context training plus per-layer activation caches give streaming that equals offline for the same context. Use it for how the latency/accuracy knob works and as a runner correctness property. Do not cite it for measured speedups (none) or for CTC superiority over buffered streaming (the CTC tables contradict it). English, small models.

### Linked handoffs and actions

- updated `handoffs/active/conversation-stack.md`
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Use the paper's property "streaming output equals offline output for the same limited context" as a correctness test for the ggml cache-aware runners (token-identical in F32).
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Report both algorithmic latency (half look-ahead) and chunk-size labels in the STT sweep so results compare to this paper and to vendor labels.
- Recommended action (dive proposal, not an instruction): Plan the Parakeet-v3 (offline TDT) arm as utterance-final decoding; a model not trained with limited context cannot be made cache-aware by a runner, only buffered, and buffering costs accuracy (11.3 vs 6.3 in Table 1).

## intake-2370#record — omnivoice.cpp: GGML port of OmniVoice multilingual zero-shot TTS (ServeurpersoCom)

- **URL**: https://github.com/ServeurpersoCom/omnivoice.cpp
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: worth_investigating (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2361#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. What it is: a C++17 / ggml port (MIT, single squashed commit 1a468d8, 2026-10-16, maintainer ServeurpersoCom, the same author as qwentts.cpp and s2s.cpp) of OmniVoice (k2-fsa, Xiaomi; arXiv 2604.00688): a non-autoregressive, mask-predict (MaskGIT) multilingual zero-shot TTS on a Qwen3-0.6B backbone (28 layers, 8 RVQ codebooks at 25 Hz, Higgs-Audio-v2 codec, 24 kHz mono), 646 languages, voice cloning, voice design, auto voice. It is a TTS runner only: no STT, no dialogue loop. Tools: omnivoice-tts, omnivoice-codec, an OpenAI-compatible tts-server with a resident cloned-voice registry, and a C ABI (libomnivoice.so).
   - anchor `repo/docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:20` @ ServeurpersoCom/omnivoice.cpp @ 1a468d8b9d65b3544ca1004d2d7c05dfc65d1f3e (depth-1 clone, 2026-10-16 "ggml: fork rebase", cloned 2026-10-21); ggml submodule cc5df49a6b878db2b68b9a7b45976e279594627d: "non autoregressive, mask-predict (MaskGIT) generative model running on top of a Qwen3 backbone"

4. Speed (author's own logs, one 102-character prompt, 6.72 s of audio, Q8_0, 32 MaskGIT steps, CFG batch of 2, no KV cache because attention is bidirectional so every step is a full prefill): LM forward 13,543 ms on CPU (16 threads, zen4 backend, host not otherwise stated), 3,579 ms on Vulkan0, 323 ms on CUDA0 (RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell). Upstream OmniVoice claims RTF as low as 0.025 on GPU. The CPU figure is above real time for the LM alone (about 2.0x the audio length) before the codec decode, so CS-26 (CPU degraded fallback) is not served at 16 threads; no 96-core EPYC measurement exists. Streaming is chunk-level (long text is chunked, pcm streamed per chunk), not token-level.

Validation: the README reports cosine 1.000000 and token-exact 100% versus the PyTorch reference, but the committed logs show that only the F32 path on CUDA and CPU reaches that (tts-CPU-F32 and tts-CUDA0-F32 both 100% exact). Divergence of sampled token trajectories from an F32 reference is expected to be chaotic and is not itself a quality measure, but the repo ships Q8_0 as the default and contains no WER, speaker-similarity or UTMOS measurement for it; quality of the shipped quantised path is unevidenced here.

7. Design worth copying regardless of model choice: tts-server registers a cloned voice once (a WAV encoded server-side, or a pre-encoded .rvq) and keeps it resident in RAM selected by name on every request; `language` overrides the server default per request. qwentts upstream commit 4eb8c7d adopted this per-request language pattern (wave-2 qwentts-upstream), which is how this repo was surfaced.

- 2026-10-21 Stage-2b wave 3: entry created already dive-verified (Stage 1 and 2 combined). Slug references in this entry resolve as: llamacpp-pr28957-argsort=intake-2362#record, qwentts-upstream=intake-2361#record, s2s-cpp-ultra-gguf=intake-2350#record. 2026-10-22 Stage-2 close-out (integrator bark-integrator), dive-surfaced source dispositions. Operator ruling 2026-10-22: "Didn't I say that we shouldn't do more than four stage 2 waves? Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv:2604.00688 "OmniVoice: Towards Omnilingual Zero-Shot Text-to-Speech with Diffusion Language Models" (title resolved on arXiv abs 2026-10-21; nam -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2381#record (omnivoice-paper) - k2-fsa/OmniVoice upstream model and code (Apache-2.0 code, CC-BY-NC weights; HF sha c5fdb5ccb189668d56333f77ba2629f4cd7535f4) -> declined: folded into the paper entry (dive's own reason) - github.com/0xShug0/audio.cpp (a ggml audio-model collection; its PR 168 reports test-backend-ops 11953/11953 on ROCm0 for gfx1151 per a web search sni -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2385#record (audio-cpp) - Strix Halo PyTorch OmniVoice benchmark (sleepingrobots.com/dreams/omnivoice-strix-halo; found by web search, not opened) -> declined: PyTorch on gfx1151 (dive's own reason) - ServeurpersoCom/ggml fork cc5df49 as a standalone entry -> declined: covered by qwentts-upstream (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

omnivoice.cpp is a sibling TTS runner, not a replacement for qwentts.cpp and not an STT or interlocutor component. Do not cite its README as evidence of working ROCm: the tree has no HIP build path or log, and the only gfx90a-relevant fact is that its ggml fork (cc5df49, GGML 0.27.0, the same pin as upstream qwentts.cpp) compiles the CUDA kernel set for HIP by glob, so the CS-43b gfx90a verification of that ggml covers it for free. Do not cite the 100%-exact parity numbers for the quantised model; they hold for F32 only. CPU generation is slower than real time at 16 threads, so it is not a CS-26 fallback candidate without a 96-core measurement. Weights are CC-BY-NC.

## intake-2371#record — wavo: local speech-to-text at GPU speed in pure Rust (Parakeet, GigaAM, Whisper, Moonshine)

- **URL**: https://github.com/vladkens/wavo
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: not_applicable (novelty low, relevance low; categories: multimodal, local_inference)
- **Disposition**: knowledge_only
- **Expanded from**: intake-2348#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. What it is: wavo (vladkens, MIT, Cargo 0.3.1, changelog entry 2026-10-14, repo head 2026-10-21) is a pure-Rust offline speech-to-text library and an ollama-like CLI that runs transcribe.cpp's GGUF models (Parakeet TDT 0.6B v2/v3, GigaAM v3, Whisper tiny to large-v3 and turbo, Moonshine, Breeze-ASR-25) with the encoder on the GPU through wgpu (Metal on Apple Silicon, Vulkan on Linux and Windows, DX12 fallback). It splits long audio at pauses and returns text with token timestamps, JSON or SRT. It is written by AI agents (Claude Code, Codex) under human review. It is the project named in transcribe.cpp issue #206 and flagged in two wave-1/2 dives (handy-unified-en-gguf, transcribe-cpp); the brief attributed the flag to the parakeet-cpp-mudler dive, but that dive does not mention wavo.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-1/2 dives described wavo as a "Rust GPU STT engine running handy GGUFs ... possible non-ggml GPU path". Confirmed on the engine and the path (pure Rust, wgpu, its own WGSL and Metal kernels, no ggml). The "possible" is resolved: it is a non-ggml GPU path but only for Apple, NVIDIA and generic Vulkan/DX12 adapters, with no AMD Instinct or ROCm story and no streaming.
   - *reattributed*: The wave-3 brief says the flag came from dive-parakeet-cpp-mudler; grep of that directory finds no mention of wavo. The flags are in dive-handy-unified-en-gguf/DIVE.md and dive-transcribe-cpp/DIVE.md (transcribe.cpp issue #206).
