### Verdict

wavo does not matter to our STT or speech plane today. It is a young, AI-written, offline-only Rust runner for the same GGUF files transcribe.cpp publishes, with its own speed claims on Apple and NVIDIA hardware and no accuracy, streaming or AMD Instinct evidence. Do not cite its speed table as a Parakeet or Whisper latency measurement for our hardware, and do not treat its agreement with transcribe.cpp as independent validation of transcribe.cpp. Its one use is as a second implementation to diff against if transcribe.cpp output on a clip ever looks wrong.

## intake-2372#record — Towards Quantifying Benchmark Optimization in ASR Models

- **URL**: https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.19936
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance high; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2352#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Method (Hume AI Research, 2026-08-20, arXiv cs.SD, not peer reviewed): three behavioural probes measure whether an ASR model reproduces a benchmark reference rather than the audio where the audio underdetermines the reference. accept-ref = share of probe positions where the model emits the reference rendering r instead of the audio-true rendering a. (1) Reference disagreement: spans where the reference contradicts the audio, found by a four-model consensus panel (Kimi-Audio, Qwen3-ASR-0.6B, Voxtral-Mini-3B, Moonshine-Streaming; members scored leave-one-out) = 1,113 flagged edits on 745 VoxPopuli test clips, 93% of which also appear in the human-annotated AA-cleaned set; about 40% of clips and about 3% of reference words carry a flagged edit. (2) Masked-number recovery: silence a number, see if the model still emits it, compare to the silenced-audio LM prior (audio lift). (3) Orthographic switching (Mr/Mister, anyone/any one): switch rate above 0.5 means the model tracks per-corpus or per-clip convention.
   - *unaffected*: The three-probe taxonomy surfaced by wave-2 as "reference disagreement, masked-number recovery, orthographic switching" matches the paper exactly.
   - anchor `paper.txt` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "we use a consensus panel of independent models to flag reference errors"
   - anchor `paper.txt:239` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "edits on 745 VoxPopuli test clips"
   - anchor `paper.txt:242` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "93\% of consensus-flagged edits also appear"
   - anchor `paper.txt:244` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "40\% of all clips, and roughly 3\% of all reference words, carry a flagged edit"
1. Headline result on 11 open models, VoxPopuli English: the six models with the best VoxPopuli WER (5.4-5.8%) are exactly the six with the highest reference-disagreement accept-ref (0.18-0.30); every model at 6.5% WER or worse is at or below 0.10. This is an 11-point rank association, not a causal estimate; the paper itself states the training mechanism (benchmark-guided selection, leakage, memorisation) is not established.
   - *narrowed*: Wave-2 notes (openasr-repo, hf-benchmaxxer-blog) summarised this as "reports that the highest-scoring open-source ASR models reproduce benchmark reference spans". True, but the paper's own statement is a rank association on 11 models and VoxPopuli English; it does not show the models were trained on or tuned to VoxPopuli, and it says the training mechanism is not understood.
   - anchor `paper.txt:345` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "the six models with the best VoxPopuli WER ( 5.4 – 5.8\% ) are exactly those with the highest accept-ref ( 0.18 – 0.30 )"
   - anchor `paper.txt` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "every model at 6.5\% WER or above sits at or below 0.10"
2. Per-model values on the human-annotated edit set (Table 1; consensus accept-ref / human accept-ref): Cohere-Transcribe 0.30/0.52, Granite-Speech-4.1-2B 0.21/0.42, Canary-Qwen-2.5B 0.23/0.41, Higgs-Audio-v3-8B 0.21/0.39, Phi-4-Multimodal 0.19/0.38, Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v2 0.18/0.38, Qwen3-ASR-0.6B 0.09/0.19, Moonshine-Streaming 0.06/0.14, Voxtral-Mini-3B 0.04/0.09, Kimi-Audio-7B 0.03/0.07, Whisper-Large-v3 0.02/0.08. Parakeet reproduces the reference error on 192 of 512 human-annotated edits; Whisper-Large-v3 on 39 of 514.
   - anchor `paper.txt` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "Parakeet-TDT-0.6B-v2 0.18 0.38 [0.32, 0.43] 192/512"
   - anchor `paper.txt` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "Whisper-Large-v3 0.02 0.08 [0.05, 0.11] 39/514"
   - anchor `paper.txt` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "Canary-Qwen-2.5B 0.23 0.41 [0.36, 0.47] 210/507"
3. The behaviour is narrow, not a general mis-mapping: it weakens on generic TTS voices, on truncated audio, on appended non-benchmark audio, and on fresh same-domain speakers (ep-fresh, European Parliament June 2026; libri-fresh), and several models show higher masked-number recovery on the public benchmarks than on the fresh sets. Models can still transcribe the same phrase faithfully off-distribution.
   - anchor `paper.txt:371` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "often rest closer to the generic accept-ref"
   - anchor `paper.txt:355` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "Several of the same models had slightly higher masked accept-ref on the public benchmarks"
4. Mechanism: activation patching shows both encoder and decoder carry the override; a low-rank diff-in-means direction from 22 courtesy-splice pairs steers Cohere, Parakeet, Canary (partly Granite) in both directions; projecting it out cuts accept-ref by 82-92% for Cohere, Canary and Parakeet (Parakeet layer 12). Parakeet has no teacher-forced readout (transducer), so its likelihood-based probes are missing.
   - anchor `paper.txt:435` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "accept-ref falls by 82 – 92\% when the steering vector is projected onto Cohere, Canary, and Parakeet"
5. Recommendations: do not use i.i.d. test splits (temporal or speaker stratification is the minimum), report on fully held-out non-public sets, and do not trust public WER alone: any model below about 3% WER on VoxPopuli must be transcribing reference errors. No leaked-speaker advantage was found (0.22 leaked vs 0.26 unleaked speakers), so the 40% train/test speaker overlap in the HF VoxPopuli split does not by itself explain the effect.
   - anchor `paper.txt:475` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "we recommend against using i.i.d. test splits for benchmark evaluation"
   - anchor `paper.txt:482` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "should be fully held-out, non-public evaluation sets"
   - anchor `paper.txt` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "any model below 3\% WER has to transcribe reference errors"
   - anchor `paper.txt:479` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "no model showed a leaked-speaker advantage on our probes"
6. Scope limits (verified here, not in the abstract): English only (VoxPopuli English, LibriSpeech); 11 models; Parakeet is the v2 0.6B (not v3); no Nemotron, no Canary-1B, no Whisper variant other than large-v3; no private-set or multilingual result. Nothing here measures it/de/fr.
   - *unaffected*: No verdict is changed by the scope limits; they bound where the findings may be applied.
   - anchor `paper.txt:140` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "We evaluate 11 widely used open source ASR models spanning the two dominant architectures"
   - anchor `paper.txt:153` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "We focus our analysis on VoxPopuli [45] (English)"
   - anchor `paper.txt:151` @ arXiv:2608.19936v1 (submitted 2026-08-20), HTML https://arxiv.org/html/2608.19936v1 curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, pv1.html sha256 727a6587..., text copy paper.txt sha256 10ab94d8...: "We report teacher-forced likelihood metrics for all models but Parakeet-TDT"

7. Reference implementation (tlebryk/asr-benchmark-optimization = HumeAI/asr-benchmark-optimization, same HEAD 9b55a563, Apache-2.0): four methods; reference-error detection and orthographic switch rate need only per-clip predictions, masked-entity recovery needs audio and re-inference, teacher-forced NLL needs weights. The Open ASR Leaderboard benchmark_fitting/ scorers are explicitly adapted from it (wave-2 dive openasr-repo).
   - *reattributed*: The brief and wave-2 notes name github.com/tlebryk/asr-benchmark-optimization as the reference implementation. The paper footnote points to github.com/HumeAI/asr-benchmark-optimization; both resolve to the identical HEAD 9b55a563 (git ls-remote), so tlebryk is the first author's mirror of the Hume repo. Cite the HumeAI URL as canonical.

Move to stage 3 planning after this fourth wave"; every source surfaced after wave 4 is declined with the reason "operator 4-wave cap (2026-10-22)". Dispositions: - arXiv:2607.14846 "RW-Voice-EQ Bench: A Real World Benchmark for Evaluating Voice AI Systems" (title resolved on arXiv abs 2026-10-21; same Hume group; -> SELECTED and dived as intake-2382#record (rw-voice-eq) - arXiv:2606.07608 "Subtitle-Aligned Fine-Tuning of Whisper for Swiss German ASR: Benchmark Contamination, Convention Mismatch, and an Honest Baseline a -> declined: Swiss-German Whisper fine-tune, outside the language gate (dive's own reason) - HF dataset HumeAI/ASR-benchmark-optimization-predictions (17 corpora, 308 runs per the repo README) -> declined: data artifact of this entry (dive's own reason) - Hume blog hume.ai/blog/measuring-benchmark-optimization-in-speech-recognition (found by web search; HTTP 200, not read) -> declined: restatement by the authors (dive's own reason) - ArtificialAnalysis/VoxPopuli-Cleaned-AA (human-corrected references used as this paper's validation set) -> declined: folded into aa-earnings22-cleaned (dive's own reason)

### Verdict

Do not cite a vendor or model-card VoxPopuli/LibriSpeech WER for Parakeet-class, Canary-Qwen-class, Cohere, Granite, Phi-4 or Higgs models as clean evidence of transcription ability: on VoxPopuli the best-WER open models are exactly those that reproduce reference errors, and the effect is worth on the order of a percentage point of WER. Whisper-Large-v3 showed the least fitting of the 11 (0.02 consensus / 0.08 human), which supports using the frozen whisper.cpp model as a scoring oracle but says nothing about its accuracy in it/de/fr. Do not read the result as proof of training contamination, and do not extrapolate it to Parakeet v3 or Nemotron 3.5, which were not tested; the screen is cheap enough to run on them.

## intake-2373#record — Artificial Analysis AA-WER Streaming: streaming speech-to-text accuracy and latency leaderboard

- **URL**: https://artificialanalysis.ai/speech-to-text/streaming
- **Verification**: dive-verified
- **Verdict**: adopt_patterns (novelty medium, relevance medium; categories: multimodal, benchmark_methodology)
- **Disposition**: integrated
- **Expanded from**: intake-2355#record

### Claims (as verified)

0. Located: the Artificial Analysis (AA) page https://artificialanalysis.ai/speech-to-text/streaming (AA-WER Streaming, announced 2026-06-01; 47 model/endpoint rows, 32 of 47 shown by default). The NVIDIA fine-tuning blog says Nemotron 3 ASR "ranks 2nd in latency among all streaming ASR models - with just 0.07 seconds to final transcript after end of speech" and sits in the "most attractive quadrant". The figure is right (Nemotron 3 ASR 80ms: 0.0730 s to final, 0.0409 s to first partial). The ranking is true only in one sense and only at launch: on first-partial latency it was 2nd (0.04 s) behind Deepgram Flux in AA's 2026-06-01 article; in the 2026-10-21 snapshot it is 3rd on first partial (AssemblyAI Universal Streaming English at 0.032 s is now ahead) and 9th of 47 on time to final (7th counting the Together-hosted copy of the same model).
   - *narrowed*: The NVIDIA blog's "ranks 2nd in latency among all streaming ASR models" is true of first-partial latency at AA's launch (2026-06-01), not of time to final, and not of the current board: 2nd on first partial in June, 3rd now, 9th of 47 on final. The blog attaches the 0.07 s time-to-final figure to the "2nd" rank, which conflates the two metrics. The "most attractive quadrant" statement could not be reproduced from the page text (80 ms variant is 46th of 47 on WER).
   - anchor `ft_blog.txt` @ NVIDIA Nemotron fine-tuning blog (saved by wave-2 dive nemotron35-asr-streaming), copy sha256 66bebad7...: "ranks 2nd in latency among all streaming ASR models — with just 0.07 seconds to final transcript after end of speech"
   - anchor `ft_blog.txt:103` @ NVIDIA Nemotron fine-tuning blog (saved by wave-2 dive nemotron35-asr-streaming), copy sha256 66bebad7...: "sits in the "most attractive quadrant" of the AA-WER Streaming Index vs. Time to Final Transcription leaderboard"
   - anchor `article.html.txt:29` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/articles/new-streaming-speech-to-text-benchmark-aa-wer-streaming, published 2026-06-01, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, text copy sha256 f6140db3...: "on First Partial, it’s followed by @NVIDIA Nemotron 3 ASR 80ms (0.04s) and Soniox Realtime (0.05s)"
   - anchor `rows.txt:10` @ derived from flight.txt by the dive (47 streaming rows), rows.txt sha256 3e049b31...: "Nemotron 3 ASR 80ms | host NVIDIA | openWeights true | WER 8.38 | final 0.0730 s | partial 0.0409 s"
   - anchor `rows.txt:2` @ derived from flight.txt by the dive (47 streaming rows), rows.txt sha256 3e049b31...: "Deepgram Flux | host Deepgram | openWeights false | WER 7.39 | final 0.0212 s | partial 0.0204 s"
1. Methodology (AA-WER v2.2 plus streaming): audio is streamed in real time in 20 ms chunks (or the model's recommended chunking); SileroVAD detects end of speech; for models supporting forced endpointing AA sends the endpoint request at that moment and times the next final transcript (Time to Final) or the first transcript-bearing event (Time to First Partial); models without forced endpointing use the first natural final within 2 s, else the first partial after 2 s, else the last partial. Provider endpoint settings are set so that only the forced endpoint triggers a final. Latency is the simple mean over samples within each dataset, then 50/25/25 weighted; WER is audio-duration weighted. Network delay is included in every latency number.
   - *unaffected*: Methodology read directly from AA's methodology page and the page text; consistent with the launch article.
   - anchor `meth.txt:63` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, text copy meth.txt sha256 1b30021f...; methodology version AA-WER v2.2 (May 2026-current): "time of first Final-denoted transcript shared after SileroVAD detected end of speech"

   - anchor `meth.txt:172` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, text copy meth.txt sha256 1b30021f...; methodology version AA-WER v2.2 (May 2026-current): "Audio is streamed in real time in 20ms chunks, or as recommended by the public model config."

2. Data: about 8 hours, three English sets weighted AA-AgentTalk 50% (AA's private held-out voice-agent set, introduced February 2026), VoxPopuli-Cleaned-AA 25%, Earnings22-Cleaned-AA 25%, with AA's own normalizer. Everything is English; the Nemotron endpoint id is cache-aware-parakeet-rnnt-en-US-asr-streaming-80ms for model "NVIDIA-Nemotron-3.5-ASR-Streaming-Multilingual-0.6b". There is no it/de/fr result.
   - anchor `page.txt:74` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/speech-to-text/streaming, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, html sha256 (page) ; text copy page.txt sha256 b24062dd...; data in flight.txt sha256 a664dbb4...: "AA-WER Streaming consists of around 8 hours of audio from three datasets: AA-AgentTalk (50%), VoxPopuli (25%), and Earnings22 (25%)."

3. Numbers (AA streaming; WER % / time to final s / time to first partial s): Nemotron 3 ASR 80 ms 8.38 / 0.073 / 0.041; 160 ms 7.61 / 0.098 / 0.073; 560 ms 5.96 / 0.252 / 0.215; 1120 ms 5.36 / 0.418 / 0.362 (all NVIDIA-hosted, open weights). 5. Not published: GPU or CPU hardware behind any endpoint, client location or network path, concurrency or load during the runs, and the percentile of latency (means only). So hardware, network and concurrency cannot be matched or subtracted for a local deployment.
   - anchor `meth.txt:177` @ https://artificialanalysis.ai/methodology/speech-to-text, curl 2026-10-21 HTTP 200, text copy meth.txt sha256 1b30021f...; methodology version AA-WER v2.2 (May 2026-current): "Network delay is included in all latency measurements."
6. Fit as a third-party prior for CS-22: usable for the metric definition and protocol (end-of-speech by Silero VAD, forced endpoint or flush, time to the next final, 20 ms chunks), which is the same measurement shape as CS-22 end-of-turn to STT final; usable as order-of-magnitude priors for cache-aware streaming transducers (final tail 0.07-0.4 s as chunk size goes 80 to 1120 ms, WER falling about 3 pp across that range); not usable as latency targets for our local MI210 or CPU path (network included, hardware unknown, English only, no whisper.cpp row).

### Verdict

Do not repeat "Nemotron is 2nd in streaming ASR latency": it was 2nd on first-partial latency at AA's June launch, is 3rd on that metric now and 9th of 47 on time to final, and its 80 ms variant is 46th of 47 on WER. The NVIDIA claim of the "most attractive quadrant" is not reproduced from the page. AA numbers are hosted-endpoint measurements with network delay included, unpublished hardware and concurrency, English only; the same model differs by host. Use AA for its protocol (Silero end of speech, forced endpoint, time to final, 20 ms chunks) and as an order-of-magnitude prior for chunk-size trade-offs (80 to 1120 ms chunks: final tail 0.07 to 0.42 s, WER 8.4 to 5.4), never as a target for our local path or as evidence about it/de/fr.
